# Tips reworded in migration 20261013000002 (before → after)

611 of 823 live tips. One idea a paragraph; the tip screen shows a paragraph a step.

- `1c3decf9` alfajores-y-chicles · -s or -es

  Before:

  > A word ending in a vowel adds **-s**: *galletita → galletitas*, *chicle → chicles*. One ending in a consonant adds **-es**: *alfajor → **alfajores***. With a number, *un* and *una* drop out: *dos paquetes*. Containers take **de**: *una botella de agua*, *un paquete de chicles*.

  After:

  > A word ending in a vowel adds **-s**: *galletita → galletitas*. One ending in a consonant adds **-es**: *alfajor → **alfajores***.
  > 
  > With a number, *un* and *una* drop out: *dos paquetes*.
  > 
  > Containers take **de**: *una botella de agua*, *un paquete de chicles*.

- `73cc59dc` buen-dia · Gracias, de nada

  Before:

  > When someone says *gracias*, answer **de nada**. *Of course* is **claro**. And when everything is fine or agreed, porteños say **¡joya!** — *¿Todo bien? — ¡Joya!*

  After:

  > When someone says *gracias*, answer **de nada**.
  > 
  > *Of course* is **claro**.
  > 
  > When everything is fine or agreed, porteños say **¡joya!**: *¿Todo bien? — ¡Joya!*

- `bc1f8253` buen-dia · Buen día

  Before:

  > In Buenos Aires the morning greeting is **buen día**, until lunch. After that it's **buenas tardes**, and once it's dark **buenas noches** — hello *and* goodbye. Short for any of them: **buenas**.

  After:

  > In Buenos Aires the morning greeting is **buen día**, until lunch.
  > 
  > After that it's **buenas tardes**, and once it's dark **buenas noches** — hello *and* goodbye.
  > 
  > Short for any of them: **buenas**.

- `88e0500d` como-te-llamas · Me llamo, te llamás

  Before:

  > The little word in front changes with the person, and so does the verb:
  > 
  > | yo | **me llamo** | my name is |
  > | vos | **te llamás** | your name is |
  > 
  > The question puts *cómo* first: **¿Cómo te llamás?** The vos form ends in **-ás**, with the stress on the end.

  After:

  > The little word in front changes with the person, and so does the verb:
  > 
  > | yo | **me llamo** | my name is |
  > | vos | **te llamás** | your name is |
  > 
  > The question puts *cómo* first: **¿Cómo te llamás?**
  > 
  > The vos form ends in **-ás**, with the stress on the end.

- `2b07464d` cuanto-cuesta · ¿Cuánto sale?

  Before:

  > **¿Cuánto sale?** is the everyday Argentine way to ask a price — **¿cuánto cuesta?** works too and means the same. For more than one thing the verb changes: **¿cuánto salen?**, **¿cuánto cuestan?**

  After:

  > **¿Cuánto sale?** is the everyday Argentine way to ask a price. You can also say **¿cuánto cuesta?**
  > 
  > For more than one thing the verb changes: **¿cuánto salen?**, **¿cuánto cuestan?**

- `b29680d4` cuanto-cuesta · Mil, never un mil

  Before:

  > Argentine prices run into the thousands, so you will say **mil** all day. One thousand is just **mil** — never *un mil* — and the word does not change: *Sale mil pesos.* *¿Mil pesos? Es caro.* **Caro** and **barato** end in **-a** for a *la* word: *una gaseosa no es cara*. **¡Qué caro!** — *How expensive!*

  After:

  > One thousand is just **mil** — never *un mil* — and the word does not change: *Sale mil pesos.*
  > 
  > **Caro** and **barato** end in **-a** for a *la* word: *una gaseosa no es cara*.
  > 
  > **¡Qué caro!** — *How expensive!*

- `fe95866f` cuanto-sale · Pago, pagás, paga

  Before:

  > **¿Cuánto sale?** is the everyday Argentine way to ask a price — *¿cuánto cuesta?* works too. Several things **salen** or **cuestan**: *¿cuánto salen las zapatillas?* *To pay* and *to buy* follow one pattern: **pago**, **pagás**, **paga** and **compro**, **comprás**, **compra**. *¿Pagás vos o pago yo?* At the register: *¿tarjeta o efectivo?* — *card or cash?* **Mangos** is slang for pesos: *no tengo un mango* means *I don't have a peso*.

  After:

  > **¿Cuánto sale?** is the everyday Argentine way to ask a price; *¿cuánto cuesta?* works too. Several things **salen** or **cuestan**: *¿cuánto salen las zapatillas?*
  > 
  > *To pay* and *to buy* follow one pattern: **pago**, **pagás**, **paga** and **compro**, **comprás**, **compra**. At the register: *¿tarjeta o efectivo?* — *card or cash?*
  > 
  > **Mangos** is slang for pesos: *no tengo un mango* means *I don't have a peso*.

- `0f6658b9` cuantos-anos-tenes · Tengo, tenés, tiene

  Before:

  > *Tener* — to have — is how you give your age: Spanish *has* years.
  > | yo | **tengo** | Tengo veinte años. |
  > | vos | **tenés** | ¿Cuántos años tenés? |
  > | él, ella | **tiene** | Tiene doce años. |
  > The *e* of *tener* becomes *ie* in *tiene* — but never in the vos form: **tenés**. Before a noun, *uno* is **un**: *tiene un año*.
  > 
  > 16 to 19 are **dieci** + the number: *dieciséis*, *diecisiete*, *dieciocho*, *diecinueve*.

  After:

  > *Tener* — to have — is how you give your age: Spanish *has* years.
  > | yo | **tengo** | Tengo veinte años. |
  > | vos | **tenés** | ¿Cuántos años tenés? |
  > | él, ella | **tiene** | Tiene doce años. |
  > The *e* of *tener* becomes *ie* in *tiene* — but never in the vos form: **tenés**.
  > 
  > Before a noun, *uno* is **un**: *tiene un año*.
  > 
  > 16 to 19 are **dieci** + the number: *dieciséis*, *diecisiete*.

- `ae4f4e8b` cuarenta-y-cinco · Sesenta or setenta?

  Before:

  > **Sesenta** (60) and **setenta** (70) are the pair everyone mixes up: **sesenta** has the **s** of *six*, **setenta** has the **t** of *seven*. And a round hundred is just **cien** — never *un cien*.

  After:

  > **Sesenta** (60) and **setenta** (70) are easy to mix up: **sesenta** has the **s** of *six*, **setenta** has the **t** of *seven*.
  > 
  > A round hundred is just **cien** — never *un cien*.

- `82c92152` en-el-kiosco · More than one

  Before:

  > Add **-s** after a vowel (*medialuna → medialunas*) and **-es** after a consonant. The word for "a" disappears: *una empanada*, but *dos empanadas*. **¿Cuántos?** or **¿cuántas?** matches the thing: *¿cuántas medialunas?*, *¿cuántos abuelos?*

  After:

  > Add **-s** after a vowel (*medialuna → medialunas*) and **-es** after a consonant.
  > 
  > The word for "a" disappears: *una empanada*, but *dos empanadas*.
  > 
  > **¿Cuántos?** or **¿cuántas?** matches the thing: *¿cuántas medialunas?*, *¿cuántos abuelos?*

- `23fd59cf` encantado · When you get lost

  Before:

  > Stuck on a word: **¿cómo se dice…?** — *how do you say…?* — and **¿qué significa?** — *what does it mean?* Too fast: **¿me repetís?** or **más despacio, por favor**. **No entendí** is *I didn't get that*. And the line that buys you patience: **hablo un poco de castellano** — Argentines call the language *castellano*.

  After:

  > Stuck on a word: **¿cómo se dice…?** — *how do you say…?* — and **¿qué significa?** — *what does it mean?*
  > 
  > Too fast: **¿me repetís?** or **más despacio, por favor**. **No entendí** is *I didn't get that*.
  > 
  > To get patience, say **hablo un poco de castellano**. Argentines call the language *castellano*.

- `8f1d1346` encantado · De

  Before:

  > Spanish has no *'s*. Say the thing, then **de**, then the person: *Juan's teacher* is **profe de Juan**, *Marta's student* is **estudiante de Marta**. **De** also comes after *un poco*: *un poco de inglés* — *a little English*.

  After:

  > Spanish has no *'s*. Say the thing, then **de**, then the person: *Juan's teacher* is **profe de Juan**, *Marta's student* is **estudiante de Marta**.
  > 
  > **De** also comes after *un poco*: *un poco de inglés* — *a little English*.

- `95b906a1` encantado · Entendí, sabe

  Before:

  > **No entendí** — *I didn't understand* — is what you say when you missed something just said: *Perdón, no entendí.* It is the past of *no entiendo*. For another person, *knows* is **sabe**: *Juan sabe un poco de inglés.*

  After:

  > **No entendí** — *I didn't understand* — is for something you just missed: *Perdón, no entendí.* It is the past of *no entiendo*.
  > 
  > For another person, *knows* is **sabe**: *Juan sabe un poco de inglés.*

- `dce725f6` encantado · Encantado, encantada

  Before:

  > **Encantado** — *delighted* — is what you say when you meet someone, and it matches whoever is speaking: a man says *encantado*, a woman **encantada**. The reply: **igualmente** — *likewise*. Day to day, most porteños just say **un gusto** or *mucho gusto*: *Soy Sofi.* — *Un gusto, Sofi.*

  After:

  > **Encantado** — *delighted* — is what you say when you meet someone. A man says *encantado*, a woman **encantada**.
  > 
  > The reply: **igualmente** — *likewise*.
  > 
  > Day to day, most porteños just say **un gusto** or *mucho gusto*: *Soy Sofi.* — *Un gusto, Sofi.*

- `710a7b73` hola-che · ¿Cómo andás?

  Before:

  > **¿Cómo andás?** is the everyday porteño *how are you?* — you will hear it all day. The answer is short: *Bien.* or *Todo bien.* **¿Qué onda?** gets the same answer: *¿Qué onda? — Todo bien.*

  After:

  > **¿Cómo andás?** is the everyday porteño *how are you?* The answer is short: *Bien.* or *Todo bien.*
  > 
  > **¿Qué onda?** gets the same answer: *¿Qué onda? — Todo bien.*

- `8b938cde` hola-che · Che

  Before:

  > **Che** is how people in Buenos Aires get someone's attention — like "hey": *Che, ¿todo bien?* It comes first, often with a name: *Che, Juan, ¿un mate?* It's friendly and casual — not for formal moments.

  After:

  > **Che** is how people in Buenos Aires say "hey": *Che, ¿todo bien?*
  > 
  > It comes first, often with a name: *Che, Juan, ¿un mate?*
  > 
  > It's friendly and casual, not for formal moments.

- `bae799d6` la-familia · Hermano, hermana

  Before:

  > Most family words come in pairs:
  > 
  > | man | woman |
  > | herman**o** | herman**a** |
  > | hij**o** | hij**a** |
  > | abuel**o** | abuel**a** |
  > | novi**o** | novi**a** |
  > | viej**o** | viej**a** |
  > 
  > **Mi** — *my* — never changes: *mi hermano*, *mi hermana*. And *papá* ends in *-a* but is a man.

  After:

  > Most family words come in pairs:
  > 
  > | man | woman |
  > | herman**o** | herman**a** |
  > | hij**o** | hij**a** |
  > | abuel**o** | abuel**a** |
  > | novi**o** | novi**a** |
  > | viej**o** | viej**a** |
  > 
  > **Mi** — *my* — never changes: *mi hermano*, *mi hermana*.
  > 
  > And *papá* ends in *-a* but is a man.

- `f64b190f` la-familia · Mis viejos

  Before:

  > **Mi viejo** and **mi vieja** — literally "my old one" — are affectionate Argentine words for *my dad* and *my mom*. Together: **mis viejos** — *my parents*.

  After:

  > **Mi viejo** and **mi vieja** — literally "my old one" — are affectionate Argentine words for *my dad* and *my mom*.
  > 
  > Together: **mis viejos** — *my parents*.

- `1f4b9651` mas-despacio · Ask for help

  Before:

  > Stuck on a word: **¿cómo se dice…?** — *how do you say…?* — and **¿qué significa?** — *what does it mean?* Too fast: **más despacio, por favor**. And the line that makes people slow down and help: **hablo un poco** — *I speak a little*. Argentines call the language **castellano**.

  After:

  > Stuck on a word: **¿cómo se dice…?** — *how do you say…?* — and **¿qué significa?** — *what does it mean?*
  > 
  > Too fast: **más despacio, por favor**. To make people slow down and help: **hablo un poco** — *I speak a little*.
  > 
  > Argentines call the language **castellano**.

- `fe0d5efc` mi-mujer-y-mi-suegra · Mi mujer, mi marido

  Before:

  > Porteños say **mi mujer** for *my wife* and **mi marido** for *my husband* — two different words, not a pair. And **bebé** is one word for a baby boy or girl: *un bebé*.

  After:

  > Porteños say **mi mujer** for *my wife* and **mi marido** for *my husband* — two different words, not a pair.
  > 
  > And **bebé** is one word for a baby boy or girl: *un bebé*.

- `3ddb4cb9` no-entiendo · Hablo, hablás

  Before:

  > The *I* form ends in **-o**: **hablo**, **entiendo**. The vos form is stressed on the end: **hablás**, **entendés**, **repetís**. One verb is different: *I know* is just **sé**, and the vos form is **sabés**. **Me** (*me*) and **te** (*you*) go before the verb: *No te entiendo. ¿Me entendés?* *No entiendo. ¿Hablás inglés?* — *No sé.*

  After:

  > The *I* form ends in **-o**: **hablo**, **entiendo**. The vos form is stressed on the end: **hablás**, **entendés**, **repetís**.
  > 
  > One verb is different: *I know* is **sé**, and the vos form is **sabés**.
  > 
  > **Me** (*me*) and **te** (*you*) go before the verb: *No te entiendo. ¿Me entendés?*

- `25308c48` otro-cafe · Tostadas, un tostado

  Before:

  > In a café nobody orders one slice: ask for **tostadas** — *Un café y tostadas, por favor.* **Un tostado** is something else: a toasted ham-and-cheese sandwich, the classic café lunch. *Una tostada* is one slice, at home: *¿Otra tostada?*

  After:

  > In a café, ask for **tostadas**, not one slice: *Un café y tostadas, por favor.*
  > 
  > **Un tostado** is something else: a toasted ham-and-cheese sandwich.
  > 
  > *Una tostada* is one slice, at home: *¿Otra tostada?*

- `6620174b` practica-hola · Soy, sos

  Before:

  > Two forms of *ser* so far: **soy** for yourself, **sos** for the person in front of you. *Soy Lucía, ¿y vos?* — *¿Sos turista?* The words *yo* and *vos* are optional; the verb already says who.

  After:

  > Two forms of *ser* so far: **soy** for yourself, **sos** for the person in front of you. *Soy Lucía, ¿y vos?* — *¿Sos turista?*
  > 
  > The words *yo* and *vos* are optional; the verb already says who.

- `e2dfe789` practica-hola · The right hello

  Before:

  > **Buen día** in the morning, **buenas tardes** later, **buenas** any time. To leave: **chau** or **hasta luego**. And after every *gracias*, a **de nada**.

  After:

  > **Buen día** in the morning, **buenas tardes** later, **buenas** any time.
  > 
  > To leave: **chau** or **hasta luego**.
  > 
  > After every *gracias*, a **de nada**.

- `d244b49a` practica-tengo-hambre · -o, -a, or no change

  Before:

  > Words for people follow the person: *mi tío, mi tía*; *brasileño, brasileña*. A word in *-e* stays put: *canadiense*. A nationality ending in a consonant adds *-a*, and any accent mark goes: *francés → francesa*, *español → española*.

  After:

  > Words for people follow the person: *mi tío, mi tía*; *brasileño, brasileña*.
  > 
  > A word in *-e* stays put: *canadiense*.
  > 
  > A nationality ending in a consonant adds *-a*, and any accent mark goes: *francés → francesa*, *español → española*.

- `fde51102` quien-es · ¿Quién es?

  Before:

  > **Quién** asks *who*: *¿Quién es él?* The answer drops *él* or *ella*: *Es Martín, un vecino.* Whose? Use **de**: *una amiga de Juan* — *a friend of Juan's*. A **pibe** is a young guy and a **piba** a young woman — very porteño.

  After:

  > **Quién** asks *who*: *¿Quién es él?* The answer drops *él* or *ella*: *Es Martín, un vecino.*
  > 
  > Whose? Use **de**: *una amiga de Juan* — *a friend of Juan's*.
  > 
  > A **pibe** is a young guy, a **piba** a young woman.

- `2ee7b356` sos-turista · Disculpá, permiso

  Before:

  > **Disculpá** gets a stranger's attention: *Disculpá, ¿sos Martín?* **Permiso** is what you say to get past someone, on a full bus or in a doorway.

  After:

  > **Disculpá** gets a stranger's attention: *Disculpá, ¿sos Martín?*
  > 
  > **Permiso** is what you say to get past someone, on a full bus or in a doorway.

- `7578114a` sos-turista · Disculpá, permiso

  > **Disculpá** gets a stranger's attention: *Disculpá, ¿sos Martín?* **Permiso** is what you say to get past someone, on a full bus or in a doorway.

- `365922d8` soy-de-zona-norte · De, for any size of place

  Before:

  > *Ser de* works for a country, a city or one corner of it: **Soy de un pueblo de Córdoba.** **Soy de zona sur.** *Zona norte*, *zona sur* and *zona oeste* go straight after *de* — no *la*.

  After:

  > *Ser de* works for a country, a city or one corner of it: **Soy de un pueblo de Córdoba.** **Soy de zona sur.**
  > 
  > *Zona norte*, *zona sur* and *zona oeste* go straight after *de* — no *la*.

- `42534aab` soy-de-zona-norte · Capital

  Before:

  > Porteños call the city itself **Capital** — *Soy de Capital* — to set it apart from the suburbs around it. Not from here at all? **Soy de afuera.**

  After:

  > Porteños call the city itself **Capital**, to set it apart from the suburbs: *Soy de Capital*.
  > 
  > Not from here at all? **Soy de afuera.**

- `03c07d66` soy-sofi · Soy

  Before:

  > To say who you are, use **soy**: *Soy Sofi.* One word carries "I am". The word for "I" (**yo**) is usually left out; add it only to stress or contrast: *Yo soy Martín.*

  After:

  > To say who you are, use **soy**: *Soy Sofi.* One word carries "I am".
  > 
  > The word for "I" (**yo**) is usually left out. Add it only to stress or contrast: *Yo soy Martín.*

- `95422350` tengo-veinte-anos · Eleven to twenty

  Before:

  > **Once**, **doce**, **trece**, **catorce** and **quince** are words to learn one by one. After that it gets easy: **dieci** plus the number, as one word: *dieciséis*, *diecisiete*. Then comes **veinte**: *Tengo veinte años.*

  After:

  > **Once**, **doce**, **trece**, **catorce** and **quince** are words to learn one by one.
  > 
  > After that it is **dieci** plus the number, as one word: *dieciséis*, *diecisiete*.
  > 
  > Then comes **veinte**: *Tengo veinte años.*

- `ce23b5b6` tiene-ocho-anos · Cero to diez

  Before:

  > With **seis**, **siete**, **ocho**, **nueve** and **diez** you can count to ten. **Cero** is zero. The number goes before **años**: *Mi hijo tiene ocho años.*

  After:

  > With **seis**, **siete**, **ocho**, **nueve** and **diez** you can count to ten. **Cero** is zero.
  > 
  > The number goes before **años**: *Mi hijo tiene ocho años.*

- `0a165b34` treinta-y-cuatro · Veinticinco, treinta y cuatro

  Before:

  > From 21 to 29 the number is one word: **veintiuno**, **veintidós**, **veinticinco**. From **treinta** on it is three words joined by **y**: *Tengo treinta y cuatro años.* The same pattern works for **cuarenta**, **cincuenta** and all the way up to **noventa**. Before *años*, **uno** becomes **un**: *veintiún años*, *treinta y un años*.

  After:

  > From 21 to 29 the number is one word: **veintiuno**, **veintidós**, **veinticinco**.
  > 
  > From **treinta** on it is three words joined by **y**: *Tengo treinta y cuatro años.* The same goes for **cuarenta**, **cincuenta** and up to **noventa**.
  > 
  > Before *años*, **uno** becomes **un**: *veintiún años*, *treinta y un años*.

- `fbdb815c` una-pizza-y-un-helado · Un or una

  Before:

  > The little word matches the thing, not you: **un** té, **un** helado, but **una** torta, **una** pizza. Learn each new word with its *un* or *una* and you'll never have to guess. One twist: *agua* takes **un** — *un agua*. It is still an *una* word, though: *otra agua*.

  After:

  > The little word matches the thing, not you: **un** té, **un** helado, but **una** torta, **una** pizza.
  > 
  > Learn each new word with its *un* or *una*.
  > 
  > One twist: *agua* takes **un**: *un agua*. It is still an *una* word: *otra agua*.

- `975cf77f` vos-y-sos · Soy, sos

  Before:

  > *Ser* — to be — has a form for each person. So far:
  > 
  > | yo | **soy** | I am |
  > | vos | **sos** | you are |
  > 
  > Use **vos** with everyone: friends, the waiter, your boss. A question is the same words with a rising voice: *¿Sos Sofi?*

  After:

  > *Ser* — to be — has a form for each person. So far:
  > 
  > | yo | **soy** | I am |
  > | vos | **sos** | you are |
  > 
  > Use **vos** with everyone: friends, the waiter, your boss.
  > 
  > A question is the same words with a rising voice: *¿Sos Sofi?*

- `d6547b7a` vos-y-sos · Vos, not tú

  Before:

  > In Argentina the everyday word for "you" is **vos**, and it has its own verb forms: *vos sos* (you are). You'll hear *tú* in other countries. This course never uses it.

  After:

  > In Argentina the everyday word for "you" is **vos**, and it has its own verb forms: *vos sos* (you are).
  > 
  > You'll hear *tú* in other countries. This course never uses it.

- `73641093` a-que-hora-abre · Menos cuarto

  Before:

  > After half past, porteños count down to the next hour: **las ocho menos cuarto** is *7:45*, *las nueve menos diez* is *8:50*. On the hour exactly: **a las diez en punto**.

  After:

  > After half past, porteños count down to the next hour: **las ocho menos cuarto** is *7:45*, *las nueve menos diez* is *8:50*.
  > 
  > On the hour exactly: **a las diez en punto**.

- `7782b8c8` a-que-hora-abre · Desde las nueve hasta las seis

  Before:

  > Opening hours are **desde** … **hasta**: *abre desde las nueve hasta las seis*. Shorter, and more common: *abre **de** nueve **a** seis*. At twelve it's **el mediodía** — and plenty of small shops **cierran** for a while right after it. With **desde**, Spanish uses the present: *estoy acá desde las ocho* = I've been here since eight.

  After:

  > Opening hours are **desde** … **hasta**: *abre desde las nueve hasta las seis*. Shorter, and more common: *abre **de** nueve **a** seis*.
  > 
  > At twelve it's **el mediodía**, and many small shops **cierran** for a while after it.
  > 
  > With **desde**, Spanish uses the present: *estoy acá desde las ocho* = I've been here since eight.

- `29afb672` adonde-vas · Always with a

  Before:

  > Going somewhere takes **a** — *to* — every time: *Voy a la plaza.* The question word has that **a** built in: **¿adónde vas?** is *where are you going to?* English often drops the *to*; Spanish never drops the **a**. **A + el** becomes **al**: *voy al kiosco*, but *vas a la estación*.

  After:

  > Going somewhere takes **a** — *to* — every time: *Voy a la plaza.* English often drops the *to*; Spanish never drops the **a**.
  > 
  > The question word has that **a** built in: **¿adónde vas?** is *where are you going to?*
  > 
  > **A + el** becomes **al**: *voy al kiosco*, but *vas a la estación*.

- `6e364ee3` adonde-vas · Voy, vas, va

  Before:

  > **Ir** — *to go* — looks nothing like its forms: **voy**, **vas**, **va**, **vamos**, **van**. Spanish uses one form for *I go* and *I'm going*: *Voy a la plaza* is both. And **vamos** on its own also means *let's go*.

  After:

  > **Ir** — *to go* — looks nothing like its forms: **voy**, **vas**, **va**, **vamos**, **van**.
  > 
  > One form covers *I go* and *I'm going*: *Voy a la plaza* is both.
  > 
  > On its own, **vamos** also means *let's go*.

- `7d0417aa` altos-y-morochos · Morocho, rubio, flaco

  Before:

  > Porteños describe people by the obvious and nobody minds: **morocho** is dark-haired, **rubio** fair, **flaco** thin, **petiso** short. *Flaco* is even a nickname: *¿Todo bien, flaco?*

  After:

  > Porteños describe people by the obvious and nobody minds: **morocho** is dark-haired, **rubio** fair, **flaco** thin, **petiso** short.
  > 
  > *Flaco* is even a nickname: *¿Todo bien, flaco?*

- `eb578f7a` altos-y-morochos · Son altos, somos morochos

  Before:

  > Describing more than one person is *ser* plus a word with an *-s*:
  > 
  > | yo | soy alt**o** | soy alt**a** |
  > | vos | sos rubi**o** | sos rubi**a** |
  > | él, ella | es flac**o** | es flac**a** |
  > | nosotros | **somos** alt**os** | **somos** alt**as** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | **son** moroch**os** | **son** lind**as** |
  > 
  > A mixed group takes *-os*. A word in *-e* or a consonant just adds *-s* or *-es*: *joven* → **jóvenes**.

  After:

  > Describing more than one person is *ser* plus a word with an *-s*:
  > 
  > | yo | soy alt**o** | soy alt**a** |
  > | vos | sos rubi**o** | sos rubi**a** |
  > | él, ella | es flac**o** | es flac**a** |
  > | nosotros | **somos** alt**os** | **somos** alt**as** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | **son** moroch**os** | **son** lind**as** |
  > 
  > A mixed group takes *-os*.
  > 
  > A word in *-e* or a consonant just adds *-s* or *-es*: *joven* → **jóvenes**.

- `c2a5f3ac` busco-una-palabra · Buscar, and personal a

  Before:

  > **Buscar** is *to look for* — no *for* needed: *busco una palabra*. **Preguntar** is *to ask* a question: *pregunto dónde está la parada*. With a person, though, put **a** in front: *extraño a mi familia*, *ayudamos a mis abuelos*. Only for a specific person: *busco a Sofi*, but *busco una profe*.

  After:

  > **Buscar** is *to look for* — no *for* needed: *busco una palabra*.
  > 
  > **Preguntar** is *to ask* a question: *pregunto dónde está la parada*.
  > 
  > With a person, put **a** in front: *extraño a mi familia*. Only for a specific person: *busco a Sofi*, but *busco una profe*.

- `56d4a95b` comes-vivis · Every -er and -ir verb

  Before:

  > | | comer | vivir |
  > | yo | com**o** | viv**o** |
  > | vos | com**és** | viv**ís** |
  > | él, ella | com**e** | viv**e** |
  > | nosotros | com**emos** | viv**imos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | com**en** | viv**en** |
  > 
  > -er and -ir are the same except for **vos** and **nosotros**, where each keeps its own vowel. The present also covers *I'm eating* and a near future: *te escribo a la noche* — I'll write to you tonight.

  After:

  > | | comer | vivir |
  > | yo | com**o** | viv**o** |
  > | vos | com**és** | viv**ís** |
  > | él, ella | com**e** | viv**e** |
  > | nosotros | com**emos** | viv**imos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | com**en** | viv**en** |
  > 
  > -er and -ir are the same except for **vos** and **nosotros**, where each keeps its own vowel.
  > 
  > The present also covers *I'm eating* and a near future: *te escribo a la noche* — I'll write to you tonight.

- `23158cf0` como-estas · Estar, and ¿por qué?

  Before:

  > Use **estar** for how you are right now: *estoy cansado*, *¿estás nerviosa?* To ask why: **¿por qué?** — two words. The answer is **porque**, one word: *Porque Sofi está enferma.*

  After:

  > Use **estar** for how you are right now: *estoy cansado*, *¿estás nerviosa?*
  > 
  > To ask why: **¿por qué?** — two words. The answer is **porque**, one word: *Porque Sofi está enferma.*

- `5efb235d` como-estas · Estar, and four endings

  Before:

  > | yo | estoy | vos | estás |
  > | él, ella | está | nosotros | **estamos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | **están** | | |
  > 
  > The word for the feeling matches the people in number too:
  > 
  > | one man | cansad**o** | one woman | cansad**a** |
  > | men | cansad**os** | women | cansad**as** |
  > 
  > A word ending in *-e* just adds *-s*: *triste, tristes*. *Feliz* becomes **felices**. **¿Sos feliz?**, with *ser*, asks about your life in general, not just right now.

  After:

  > | yo | estoy | vos | estás |
  > | él, ella | está | nosotros | **estamos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | **están** | | |
  > 
  > The word for the feeling matches the people in number too:
  > 
  > | one man | cansad**o** | one woman | cansad**a** |
  > | men | cansad**os** | women | cansad**as** |
  > 
  > A word ending in *-e* just adds *-s*: *triste, tristes*. *Feliz* becomes **felices**.
  > 
  > **¿Sos feliz?**, with *ser*, asks about your life in general, not just right now.

- `5f609aca` compartimos-todo · Abre, recibe

  Before:

  > **Abrir** and **recibir** are regular: *abro, abrís, abre*; *recibo, recibís, recibe*. *¿El negocio abre a la noche?* **Recibir a** someone is to have them over: *recibo a mi suegra*.

  After:

  > **Abrir** and **recibir** are regular: *abro, abrís, abre*; *recibo, recibís, recibe*.
  > 
  > **Recibir a** someone is to have them over: *recibo a mi suegra*.

- `ff18c38c` compartimos-todo · -er and -ir: different only in vos and nosotros

  Before:

  > -er verbs keep **e** and -ir verbs take **i**, but only for *vos* and *nosotros*: *vend**és**, vend**emos*** but *abr**ís**, abr**imos***. Everywhere else they match: *vendo, abro*; *lee, comparte*; *leen, escriben*. *Recibir* and *correr* follow the same pattern.

  After:

  > -er verbs keep **e** and -ir verbs take **i**, but only for *vos* and *nosotros*: *vend**és**, vend**emos*** but *abr**ís**, abr**imos***.
  > 
  > Everywhere else they match: *vendo, abro*; *lee, comparte*.
  > 
  > *Recibir* and *correr* follow the same pattern.

- `ab90d735` de-que-laburas · ¿De qué laburás?

  Before:

  > *¿Qué hacés?* is mostly just *hi*. To ask about someone's job, say **¿De qué laburás?** or **¿De qué trabajás?** The answer takes *de* too: *Laburo de mozo.* Or with *ser*: *Soy abogada.*

  After:

  > *¿Qué hacés?* is mostly just *hi*. To ask about someone's job, say **¿De qué laburás?** or **¿De qué trabajás?**
  > 
  > The answer takes *de* too: *Laburo de mozo.* Or with *ser*: *Soy abogada.*

- `2aa3d8f0` donde-esta · Estar, and ser or estar

  Before:

  > | yo | **estoy** |
  > | vos | **estás** |
  > | él, ella | **está** |
  > 
  > Two verbs mean *to be*. **Ser** says who or what something is, and where it's from: *Soy de Rosario.* **Estar** says where it is right now: *Estoy en Buenos Aires.*

  After:

  > | yo | **estoy** |
  > | vos | **estás** |
  > | él, ella | **está** |
  > 
  > Two verbs mean *to be*. **Ser** says who or what something is, and where it's from: *Soy de Rosario.*
  > 
  > **Estar** says where it is right now: *Estoy en Buenos Aires.*

- `d8622385` en-el-primer-piso · Planta baja y pisos

  Before:

  > The ground floor is **la planta baja**; above it comes *el **primer** piso* (*primero* loses its *-o* before a noun). Then **segundo, tercero, cuarto, quinto**. Higher up, people often just say the number: *piso doce*. On the **timbre**, *4° B* is read **cuarto B**. **Piso** is also the floor you walk on.

  After:

  > The ground floor is **la planta baja**. Above it comes *el **primer** piso* (*primero* loses its *-o* before a noun).
  > 
  > Then **segundo, tercero, cuarto, quinto**. Higher up, people often just say the number: *piso doce*.
  > 
  > On the **timbre**, *4° B* is read **cuarto B**. **Piso** is also the floor you walk on.

- `c1c004e5` es-enfermera · No un, no una

  Before:

  > The same rule as *soy profesora*: **es enfermera**, **soy contador** — no *un*, no *una*. The article only comes back when you add something: *es una periodista argentina*.

  After:

  > The same rule as *soy profesora*: **es enfermera**, **soy contador** — no *un*, no *una*.
  > 
  > The article only comes back when you add something: *es una periodista argentina*.

- `0f108707` esta-arriba · Arriba, abajo, adentro

  Before:

  > Three words that need nothing after them: **arriba** (up, upstairs), **abajo** (down, downstairs), **adentro** (inside). *Estoy abajo* is what a porteño texts from the street when they're at your door. The fourth one is **afuera** — *outside*: *mi auto está afuera*.

  After:

  > Three words that need nothing after them: **arriba** (up, upstairs), **abajo** (down, downstairs), **adentro** (inside).
  > 
  > *Estoy abajo* is what a porteño texts when they're at your door.
  > 
  > The fourth one is **afuera** — *outside*: *mi auto está afuera*.

- `19fdd686` estas-listo · Caliente

  Before:

  > **Caliente** is for things: *el café está caliente*. Said about a person it means angry — or something ruder — so for yourself use *tengo calor*, and for the weather *hace calor*.

  After:

  > **Caliente** is for things: *el café está caliente*.
  > 
  > Said about a person it means angry, or something ruder. For yourself use *tengo calor*, and for the weather *hace calor*.

- `fc0b4b4e` estas-listo · ¿Listo? Listo.

  Before:

  > **Listo** is *ready* — *¿estás lista?* — and on its own it closes any deal: *Listo, dale.* **Apurado** is *in a hurry*, the normal state of anyone downtown.

  After:

  > **Listo** is *ready*: *¿estás lista?* On its own it closes any deal: *Listo, dale.*
  > 
  > **Apurado** is *in a hurry*.

- `9b8de7a7` estudio-a-la-noche · A la noche, ¿qué hacés?

  Before:

  > Porteños say **a la noche** for *at night* or *tonight*: *A la noche estudio inglés.* *Hablamos a la noche* — we'll talk tonight. **De noche** is at night in general: *laburo de noche* (I work nights). And **¿qué hacés?** — literally *what are you doing?* — is also the everyday *how's it going?*: *Hola, ¿qué hacés?*

  After:

  > Porteños say **a la noche** for *at night* or *tonight*: *A la noche estudio inglés.*
  > 
  > **De noche** is at night in general: *laburo de noche* (I work nights).
  > 
  > Literally *what are you doing?*, **¿qué hacés?** is also the everyday *how's it going?*: *Hola, ¿qué hacés?*

- `2375ef12` facu-y-laburo · Facu, and jobs without "a"

  Before:

  > **Facu** is short for *facultad* — university. For jobs, Spanish drops the article: **Soy profesora**, not *soy una profesora*. Jobs change for a woman: *profesor* → **profesora**, *médico* → **médica**, *jefe* → **jefa**.

  After:

  > **Facu** is short for *facultad* — university.
  > 
  > For jobs, Spanish drops the article: **Soy profesora**, not *soy una profesora*.
  > 
  > Jobs change for a woman: *profesor* → **profesora**, *médico* → **médica**, *jefe* → **jefa**.

- `ae41a407` facu-y-laburo · Tener que, and que

  Before:

  > **Tener que** + an infinitive (the *-ar, -er, -ir* form) is *to have to*: *tengo que laburar* — I have to work; *¿tenés que estudiar?* — do you have to study? A different **que** joins two ideas: *tengo una jefa que habla inglés* — a boss who speaks English.

  After:

  > **Tener que** + an infinitive (the *-ar, -er, -ir* form) is *to have to*: *tengo que laburar* — I have to work.
  > 
  > A different **que** joins two ideas: *tengo una jefa que habla inglés* — a boss who speaks English.

- `ec69da9b` hay-un-kiosco · Hay or está

  Before:

  > | something new | something you already know |
  > | **Hay** un kiosco. | **El** kiosco **está** en la esquina. |
  > | **Hay** una parada acá. | **La** parada **está** lejos. |
  > 
  > **Hay** goes with *un, una*, a number or nothing at all, and never changes: *hay dos kioscos*, *¿hay agua?* **Está** goes with *el, la*.

  After:

  > | something new | something you already know |
  > | **Hay** un kiosco. | **El** kiosco **está** en la esquina. |
  > | **Hay** una parada acá. | **La** parada **está** lejos. |
  > 
  > **Hay** goes with *un, una*, a number or nothing at all, and never changes: *hay dos kioscos*, *¿hay agua?*
  > 
  > **Está** goes with *el, la*.

- `c41cf955` hay-un-tren · ¿Hay … por acá?

  Before:

  > | asking | **¿Hay** una estación **por acá**? |
  > | yes | Sí, **hay** una en la avenida. |
  > | no | No, acá no **hay** tren. |
  > | where | La estación **está** a dos cuadras. |
  > 
  > **Por acá** is *around here*. **Hay** never changes: *hay un bar*, *hay dos plazas*, *hay gente*. 

  After:

  > | asking | **¿Hay** una estación **por acá**? |
  > | yes | Sí, **hay** una en la avenida. |
  > | no | No, acá no **hay** tren. |
  > | where | La estación **está** a dos cuadras. |
  > 
  > **Por acá** is *around here*.
  > 
  > **Hay** never changes: *hay un bar*, *hay dos plazas*.

- `b861d84d` hay-una-plaza · Hay gente

  Before:

  > **Hay** works for things you can't count, too: **hay gente** — there are people. *Gente* is singular in Spanish: *la gente está en la plaza*.

  After:

  > **Hay** works for things you can't count, too: **hay gente** — there are people.
  > 
  > *Gente* is singular in Spanish: *la gente está en la plaza*.

- `11dfb570` hoy-y-manana · Two mañanas

  Before:

  > **Mañana** on its own is *tomorrow*: *Hoy laburo, mañana no.* With **la** it is *the morning*, and porteños say **a la mañana** for *in the morning*. So *tomorrow morning* is **mañana a la mañana**.

  After:

  > **Mañana** on its own is *tomorrow*: *Hoy laburo, mañana no.*
  > 
  > With **la** it is *the morning*, and porteños say **a la mañana** for *in the morning*.
  > 
  > So *tomorrow morning* is **mañana a la mañana**.

- `5d601854` hoy-y-manana · Tarde: afternoon or late

  Before:

  > **Tarde** works the same way. **A la tarde** is *in the afternoon*; on its own **tarde** means *late*: *Es tarde.* Its opposite is **temprano** — *early*: *Es temprano.*

  After:

  > **Tarde** works the same way. **A la tarde** is *in the afternoon*; on its own **tarde** means *late*: *Es tarde.*
  > 
  > Its opposite is **temprano** — *early*: *Es temprano.*

- `040623f8` la-gente · Su and nuestro

  Before:

  > **Su** is *his*, *her* or *their* (and *your* for *ustedes*); with more than one it is **sus**: *su mamá*, *sus hijos*. *Our* matches the thing, not the people: **nuestro** papá, **nuestra** familia, **nuestros** abuelos, **nuestras** hermanas.

  After:

  > **Su** is *his*, *her* or *their* (and *your* for *ustedes*). With more than one it is **sus**: *su mamá*, *sus hijos*.
  > 
  > *Our* matches the thing, not the people: **nuestro** papá, **nuestra** familia, **nuestros** abuelos, **nuestras** hermanas.

- `97b6abce` la-hora · Telling the time

  Before:

  > *Es la una* (one o'clock), but *son las dos*, *son las siete*. Add **y media** for half past and **y cuarto** for quarter past. Other minutes go the same way: *las siete y veinte*. Past the half hour people often drop the **y**: *las ocho cuarenta*. To ask: *¿Qué hora es?* or *¿Tenés hora?* *At* a time is **a las**: *¿A qué hora?* — *A las nueve y media.* After a clock time say **de la mañana / de la tarde / de la noche**: *a las dos de la tarde*. With no clock time: **a la mañana**, *a la tarde*, *a la noche*.

  After:

  > *Es la una* (one o'clock), but *son las dos*. Add **y media** for half past and **y cuarto** for quarter past. Other minutes go the same way: *las siete y veinte*. Past the half hour people often drop the **y**: *las ocho cuarenta*.
  > 
  > To ask: *¿Qué hora es?* or *¿Tenés hora?* *At* a time is **a las**: *¿A qué hora?* — *A las nueve y media.*
  > 
  > After a clock time say **de la mañana / de la tarde / de la noche**: *a las dos de la tarde*. With no clock time: **a la mañana**, *a la tarde*, *a la noche*.

- `97723334` leo-y-escribo · Leés, escribís

  Before:

  > These follow *comer* and *vivir*. **Leer** and **aprender** are -er verbs: *leo, leés, lee*. **Escribir** is an -ir verb: *escribo, escribís, escribe*. Say both vowels in **leés**: *¿Leés muchos libros?*

  After:

  > These follow *comer* and *vivir*. **Leer** and **aprender** are -er verbs: *leo, leés, lee*. **Escribir** is an -ir verb: *escribo, escribís, escribe*.
  > 
  > Say both vowels in **leés**: *¿Leés muchos libros?*

- `afc27211` los-dias-de-la-semana · El lunes, los lunes

  Before:

  > Days take **el** for one day and **los** for every week: *el miércoles laburo* (on Wednesday), *los lunes laburo* (on Mondays). They have no capital letter. Dates work the same way, with a plain number: *el veintiuno tengo examen*. **Nos vemos** is *see you*; as a question it makes a plan: *¿Nos vemos el sábado?* — *Should we meet on Saturday?*

  After:

  > Days take **el** for one day and **los** for every week: *el miércoles laburo* (on Wednesday), *los lunes laburo* (on Mondays). They have no capital letter.
  > 
  > Dates work the same way, with a plain number: *el veintiuno tengo examen*.
  > 
  > **Nos vemos** is *see you*; as a question it makes a plan: *¿Nos vemos el sábado?* — *Should we meet on Saturday?*

- `1861263d` mate-y-facturas · Gusta or gustan

  Before:

  > The verb matches the thing you like, not you: *Me **gusta** el mate*, but *Me **gustan** las facturas*. The little word in front says who likes it: **me**, **te**, **le**. With a name, add **a** and keep **le**: *A Lucía le gusta el mate.*

  After:

  > The verb matches the thing you like, not you: *Me **gusta** el mate*, but *Me **gustan** las facturas*.
  > 
  > The little word in front says who likes it: **me**, **te**, **le**.
  > 
  > With a name, add **a** and keep **le**: *A Lucía le gusta el mate.*

- `96e834f8` me-ayudas · Más despacio

  Before:

  > Porteños talk **rápido**. The line that saves you: *¿Podés hablar más despacio?* — and most people will happily slow down and **ayudar**.

  After:

  > Porteños talk **rápido**. Ask: *¿Podés hablar más despacio?* Most people will slow down and **ayudar**.

- `5accb3e5` me-traes-un-cafe · El mozo

  Before:

  > In Argentina the waiter is **el mozo** (or *la moza*). To call one over, *perdón* is the gentle way; *¡mozo!* is heard too. When you're done, ask for **la cuenta**. 

  After:

  > In Argentina the waiter is **el mozo** (or *la moza*).
  > 
  > To call one over, *perdón* is the gentle way; *¡mozo!* is heard too.
  > 
  > When you're done, ask for **la cuenta**.

- `6c6949b3` me-traes-un-cafe · ¿Me traés…?

  Before:

  > The easy way to order is to ask the waiter to bring it: **¿Me traés un café?** — literally "do you bring me a coffee?". *Quiero un café* works too: **quiero**, **¿querés?** — no *ie* in the vos form. Or just name it: *Para mí, un té.* Add **por favor**.

  After:

  > To order, ask the waiter to bring it: **¿Me traés un café?** — literally "do you bring me a coffee?".
  > 
  > *Quiero un café* works too: **quiero**, **¿querés?** — no *ie* in the vos form.
  > 
  > Or just name it: *Para mí, un té.* Add **por favor**.

- `f9a1e455` mi-casa · Depto, pieza, living

  Before:

  > Argentines say **depto** for an apartment, **pieza** for a bedroom and **el living** for the living room. The fridge is **la heladera**. **Alquilo** on its own means *I rent my place*: *¿Dónde alquilás?*

  After:

  > Argentines say **depto** for an apartment, **pieza** for a bedroom and **el living** for the living room.
  > 
  > The fridge is **la heladera**.
  > 
  > **Alquilo** on its own means *I rent my place*: *¿Dónde alquilás?*

- `9acb02d2` mi-edificio · Tiene ascensor

  Before:

  > When you just say whether it has one, no *un*: *mi edificio **tiene** ascensor*, *no **tiene** terraza*. **Hay** for what's there: *en la planta baja **hay** un patio*.

  After:

  > When you just say whether it has one, no *un*: *mi edificio **tiene** ascensor*, *no **tiene** terraza*.
  > 
  > **Hay** for what's there: *en la planta baja **hay** un patio*.

- `52a41619` nos-gustan-los-fideos · Los fideos, la verdura

  Before:

  > Pasta is **los fideos**, always plural. Vegetables are **la verdura** or **las verduras** — both are everyday: *no le gusta la verdura*, *me gustan las verduras*.

  After:

  > Pasta is **los fideos**, always plural.
  > 
  > Vegetables are **la verdura** or **las verduras** — both are everyday: *no le gusta la verdura*, *me gustan las verduras*.

- `5cf8649b` practica-el-vecino · Es cocinero

  Before:

  > Jobs take no *un*: **es cocinero**, **soy contadora**. The word changes for a woman — *cocinera* — except the *-ista* ones: *periodista*.

  After:

  > Jobs take no *un*: **es cocinero**, **soy contadora**.
  > 
  > The word changes for a woman — *cocinera* — except the *-ista* ones: *periodista*.

- `63ff509a` practica-el-vecino · Everyone ends in -n

  Before:

  > The *ellos* and *ustedes* form of every verb you have ends in **-n**: *quieren, tienen, laburan, viven, escriben*. *¿Quieren café?* — do you guys want coffee? Hear the *-n* and you know it's more than one.

  After:

  > Every *ellos* and *ustedes* form you have ends in **-n**: *quieren, tienen, laburan, viven, escriben*.
  > 
  > *¿Quieren café?* — do you guys want coffee? Hear the *-n* and you know it's more than one.

- `1f297d17` practica-nos-gusta · Three verbs, three rules

  Before:

  > *Estar* and the describing word after it match the person: *estamos cansad**os***. An -ar verb takes the person's ending: *practic**amos***. *Gustar* matches the thing: *nos gust**an** los fideos*.

  After:

  > *Estar* and the describing word after it match the person: *estamos cansad**os***.
  > 
  > An -ar verb takes the person's ending: *practic**amos***.
  > 
  > *Gustar* matches the thing: *nos gust**an** los fideos*.

- `a7aca477` practica-nos-gusta · Who likes it

  Before:

  > The little word in front says who likes it: **me**, **te**, **le**, **nos**, **les**. The verb is only ever *gusta* (one thing) or *gustan* (several): *le gust**a** el pollo*, *me gust**an** las medialunas*.

  After:

  > The little word in front says who likes it: **me**, **te**, **le**, **nos**, **les**.
  > 
  > The verb is only ever *gusta* (one thing) or *gustan* (several): *le gust**a** el pollo*, *me gust**an** las medialunas*.

- `88ebae8e` practico-castellano · More -ar verbs, same endings

  Before:

  > | | practicar | necesitar | ayudar |
  > | yo | practic**o** | necesit**o** | ayud**o** |
  > | vos | practic**ás** | necesit**ás** | ayud**ás** |
  > | él, ella | practic**a** | necesit**a** | ayud**a** |
  > | nosotros | practic**amos** | necesit**amos** | ayud**amos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | practic**an** | necesit**an** | ayud**an** |
  > 
  > *Usar, buscar, preguntar, enseñar* work the same way. The vos form always carries the accent: *¿Qué buscás?*

  After:

  > | | practicar | necesitar | ayudar |
  > | yo | practic**o** | necesit**o** | ayud**o** |
  > | vos | practic**ás** | necesit**ás** | ayud**ás** |
  > | él, ella | practic**a** | necesit**a** | ayud**a** |
  > | nosotros | practic**amos** | necesit**amos** | ayud**amos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | practic**an** | necesit**an** | ayud**an** |
  > 
  > *Usar, buscar, preguntar, enseñar* work the same way.
  > 
  > The vos form always carries the accent: *¿Qué buscás?*

- `3df4bd97` que-haces · Every -ar verb

  Before:

  > Take off *-ar* and add the ending for the person:
  > 
  > | yo | labur**o** |
  > | vos | labur**ás** |
  > | él, ella | labur**a** |
  > | nosotros | labur**amos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | labur**an** |
  > 
  > It works for *hablar, tomar, estudiar, caminar* — every regular -ar verb. **Hacer** is not an -ar verb, so learn its three forms as they are: **hago**, *hacés, hace*.

  After:

  > Take off *-ar* and add the ending for the person:
  > 
  > | yo | labur**o** |
  > | vos | labur**ás** |
  > | él, ella | labur**a** |
  > | nosotros | labur**amos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | labur**an** |
  > 
  > It works for *hablar, tomar, estudiar, caminar* — every regular -ar verb.
  > 
  > **Hacer** is not an -ar verb, so learn its three forms as they are: **hago**, *hacés, hace*.

- `899d348a` que-quieren-tomar · Soda y picada

  Before:

  > **Soda** is fizzy water, still served from a siphon in the old cafés. It is not a soft drink — that is **gaseosa**. On a menu you will see **agua con gas** (sparkling) and **agua sin gas** (still). A **picada** is a board of cheese and cold cuts to share before dinner.

  After:

  > **Soda** is fizzy water, served from a siphon in the old cafés. It is not a soft drink — that is **gaseosa**.
  > 
  > On a menu you will see **agua con gas** (sparkling) and **agua sin gas** (still).
  > 
  > A **picada** is a board of cheese and cold cuts to share before dinner.

- `bce85693` que-quieren-tomar · Quiere, queremos, quieren

  Before:

  > Ordering for more than one: **queremos** dos cafés. The mozo asks the table **¿qué quieren?** And for someone else: *ella **quiere** un té*. The *ie* is everywhere except *querés* and *queremos*.

  After:

  > Ordering for more than one: **queremos** dos cafés. The mozo asks the table **¿qué quieren?**
  > 
  > For someone else: *ella **quiere** un té*.
  > 
  > The *ie* is everywhere except *querés* and *queremos*.

- `bb1bfe5d` siempre-camino · Siempre, nunca

  Before:

  > **Siempre** (always) and **nunca** (never) go in front of the verb: *siempre camino al laburo*. With **nunca** there you need no *no*: *nunca tomo café*.

  After:

  > **Siempre** (always) and **nunca** (never) go in front of the verb: *siempre camino al laburo*.
  > 
  > With **nunca** there you need no *no*: *nunca tomo café*.

- `3910aabd` tiene-que-ayudar · Buscar

  Before:

  > **Buscar** is *to look for* — *tengo que buscar laburo* — and also *to pick someone up*: *tengo que buscar a mi hijo*, *tienen que buscar a sus hijos*.

  After:

  > **Buscar** is *to look for*: *tengo que buscar laburo*.
  > 
  > It is also *to pick someone up*: *tengo que buscar a mi hijo*.

- `9769b812` vendo-diarios · El negocio

  Before:

  > **Negocio** is any shop, not just a business: *el negocio de la esquina*. And porteños **comparten** everything, from an apartment to a mate. *Con mi hermano vendemos empanadas* means *my brother and I sell* — two people, not three.

  After:

  > **Negocio** is any shop, not just a business: *el negocio de la esquina*.
  > 
  > Porteños **comparten** everything, from an apartment to a mate.
  > 
  > *Con mi hermano vendemos empanadas* means *my brother and I sell* — two people, not three.

- `157ca385` a-la-vuelta · A la vuelta

  Before:

  > **A la vuelta** is *around the corner* — on the next street, not this one. **En la otra vereda** is *across the street*: *la panadería está en la otra vereda*.

  After:

  > **A la vuelta** is *around the corner* — on the next street, not this one.
  > 
  > **En la otra vereda** is *across the street*: *la panadería está en la otra vereda*.

- `a3090b0a` a-la-vuelta · A la derecha, a la izquierda

  Before:

  > Left and right both take **a la**: **a la derecha**, **a la izquierda**. To say what something is left or right of, add **de**: *la farmacia está a la derecha del banco*.

  After:

  > Left and right both take **a la**: **a la derecha**, **a la izquierda**.
  > 
  > To say what something is left or right of, add **de**: *la farmacia está a la derecha del banco*.

- `00f64f36` ayuda · Me perdí, me robaron

  Before:

  > Learn these two whole — they are in the past, which the course teaches later. **Me perdí** — *I got lost*; right now you are **perdido** or **perdida**. **Me robaron** — *I got robbed*: *me robaron el celu*.

  After:

  > Learn these two whole. They are in the past, which the course teaches later.
  > 
  > **Me perdí** — *I got lost*; right now you are **perdido** or **perdida**.
  > 
  > **Me robaron** — *I got robbed*: *me robaron el celu*.

- `36daec09` ayuda · The numbers to call

  Before:

  > In Buenos Aires **911** is the police and **107** is the ambulance. In the street, shout **¡ayuda!** and tell someone what to do: **llamá a la policía**, *llamá una ambulancia*. *Es urgente.*

  After:

  > In Buenos Aires **911** is the police and **107** is the ambulance.
  > 
  > In the street, shout **¡ayuda!** and tell someone what to do: **llamá a la policía**, *llamá una ambulancia*. *Es urgente.*

- `c61da1fd` ayuda · ¡Cuidado!

  Before:

  > **¡Cuidado!** is *watch out!* — a car, a step, a wet floor. To get help fast, say what you need: *necesito un médico*, *¿hay un hospital cerca?*

  After:

  > **¡Cuidado!** is *watch out!* — a car, a step, a wet floor.
  > 
  > To get help fast, say what you need: *necesito un médico*, *¿hay un hospital cerca?*

- `53370f9c` celeste-y-blanco · Celeste, rosa, naranja

  Before:

  > **Celeste** is the light blue of the flag — for porteños a different color from *azul*: *No son azules, son celestes.* **Rosa** and **naranja** keep the *-a* for masculine too: *un pantalón rosa*, *un jean naranja*.

  After:

  > **Celeste** is the light blue of the flag — for porteños a different color from *azul*: *No son azules, son celestes.*
  > 
  > **Rosa** and **naranja** keep the *-a* for masculine too: *un pantalón rosa*, *un jean naranja*.

- `a807dff0` chip-y-datos · Chip y datos

  Before:

  > For a local number you buy a **chip** — a SIM card — at a phone shop or a kiosco (take your passport: they may ask for it), and then you load **datos**: *cargar datos*, the same verb as loading the SUBE. *No tengo datos* means no internet until the next wifi.

  After:

  > For a local number you buy a **chip** — a SIM card — at a phone shop or a kiosco. Take your passport: they may ask for it.
  > 
  > Then you load **datos**: *cargar datos*, the same verb as loading the SUBE.
  > 
  > *No tengo datos* means no internet until the next wifi.

- `d48b1ff9` clima · Weather with hacer

  Before:

  > **Hace calor**, **hace frío** — it's hot, it's cold. But **hay sol**, **hay viento**, **está nublado**, and **llueve** alone (for right now you'll also hear *está lloviendo*). You yourself **tenés frío**. Seasons are flipped: *el verano* runs from late December to March. **Bárbaro** means *great*, not barbaric: *¡Bárbaro!* — Great! *Hay un sol bárbaro* — it's really sunny.

  After:

  > **Hace calor**, **hace frío** — it's hot, it's cold. But **hay sol**, **hay viento**, **está nublado**, and **llueve** alone (for right now you'll also hear *está lloviendo*).
  > 
  > You yourself **tenés frío**. Seasons are flipped: *el verano* runs from late December to March.
  > 
  > **Bárbaro** means *great*, not barbaric: *Hay un sol bárbaro* — it's really sunny.

- `cf20bac1` dale-veni · How a vos command is made

  Before:

  > Take the infinitive, drop the *-r*, and stress the last vowel:
  > 
  > | mirar | **mirá** |
  > | esperar | **esperá** |
  > | venir | **vení** |
  > | andar | **andá** |
  > 
  > A pronoun joins the end, and the accent goes, because the stress doesn't move: *esperá* → **esperame**. *Decir* → **decime**, *sentarse* → **sentate**. To tell someone to *go*, use **andá** — *ir* has no vos command of its own: *andá al médico*.

  After:

  > Take the infinitive, drop the *-r*, and stress the last vowel:
  > 
  > | mirar | **mirá** |
  > | esperar | **esperá** |
  > | venir | **vení** |
  > | andar | **andá** |
  > 
  > A pronoun joins the end, and the accent goes, because the stress doesn't move: *esperá* → **esperame**. *Decir* → **decime**, *sentarse* → **sentate**.
  > 
  > To tell someone to *go*, use **andá** — *ir* has no vos command of its own: *andá al médico*.

- `5670c80f` debito-o-credito · ¿Débito o crédito?

  Before:

  > The question at every till. With **crédito** comes a second one: *¿en cuántas cuotas?* — how many monthly payments. *En una cuota* is all at once; **sin interés** means no extra charge. Not sure they take cards? *¿Puedo pagar con tarjeta?*

  After:

  > The question at every till. With **crédito** comes a second one: *¿en cuántas cuotas?* — how many monthly payments.
  > 
  > *En una cuota* is all at once; **sin interés** means no extra charge.
  > 
  > Not sure they take cards? *¿Puedo pagar con tarjeta?*

- `b197e033` debito-o-credito · Lucas and dólares

  Before:

  > Prices run to thousands, so a thousand pesos has a short name: **una luca**, *diez lucas*. To **cambiar dólares**, first ask the rate: *¿A cuánto está el dólar?* And cash often gets a better price: *¿Hay descuento en efectivo?*

  After:

  > A thousand pesos has a short name: **una luca**, *diez lucas*.
  > 
  > To **cambiar dólares**, first ask the rate: *¿A cuánto está el dólar?*
  > 
  > Cash often gets a better price: *¿Hay descuento en efectivo?*

- `bea55b21` debito-o-credito · Te transfiero

  Before:

  > Paying by bank transfer is normal, in shops and between friends: **te transfiero**. You ask for the **alias**, the short name of the other person's account. Or you scan a **QR** with your phone — most people use the Mercado Pago app for it.

  After:

  > Paying by bank transfer is normal, in shops and between friends: **te transfiero**.
  > 
  > You ask for the **alias**, the short name of the other person's account.
  > 
  > Or you scan a **QR** with your phone — most people use the Mercado Pago app for it.

- `5c1d67c6` dejame-aca · Bajate, subite

  Before:

  > On the bondi, getting on and off carries a *te* on the end: **subite** (get on), **bajate en la esquina** (get off at the corner). And **pará** stops anyone: *¡pará, pará!* In a car, **dejar** is *to drop off*: **dejame acá**, *te dejo en la esquina*.

  After:

  > On the bondi, getting on and off carries a *te* on the end: **subite** (get on), **bajate en la esquina** (get off at the corner).
  > 
  > And **pará** stops anyone: *¡pará, pará!*
  > 
  > In a car, **dejar** is *to drop off*: **dejame acá**, *te dejo en la esquina*.

- `6b5e4dd1` dejame-aca · ¿A qué altura?

  Before:

  > Porteños give an address as street and number — *Corrientes 1500* — and ask **¿a qué altura?** to know which block. The number goes up about a hundred every **cuadra**. To ask the way: **¿Cómo llego a la estación?** — *how do I get to the station?*

  After:

  > Porteños give an address as street and number — *Corrientes 1500* — and ask **¿a qué altura?** to know which block. The number goes up about a hundred every **cuadra**.
  > 
  > To ask the way: **¿Cómo llego a la estación?** — *how do I get to the station?*

- `c46ddd6d` duermo-la-siesta · La siesta

  Before:

  > **Dormir** stretches like *poder*: **duermo**, but **dormís**. And **la siesta** after lunch is still sacred in much of the country — less so in Buenos Aires. Around five comes the fourth meal: to **merendar** — mate or coffee with something sweet.

  After:

  > **Dormir** stretches like *poder*: **duermo**, but **dormís**.
  > 
  > After lunch, **la siesta** is still sacred in much of the country — less so in Buenos Aires.
  > 
  > Around five comes the fourth meal: to **merendar** — mate or coffee with something sweet.

- `422fa950` el-barrio · Súper, verdulería

  Before:

  > Nobody says the long word: the **supermercado** is just **el súper**. For fruit and vegetables you go to the **verdulería**, a small shop you find on almost every block: *Voy a la verdulería.*

  After:

  > Nobody says the long word: the **supermercado** is just **el súper**.
  > 
  > For fruit and vegetables you go to the **verdulería**, a small shop on almost every block: *Voy a la verdulería.*

- `f30ace7e` en-febrero · Months and degrees

  Before:

  > Months have no capital: **en marzo**, **en diciembre**. Temperature is **grados**: *hace treinta grados* is a normal January day, and porteños blame everything on **la humedad**. Argentina uses Celsius: *35 grados* is very hot (95°F), and *1 grado* is almost freezing. With **desde** or **hace** + a time, Spanish uses the present: *vivo acá hace dos meses* — I've been living here for two months.

  After:

  > Months have no capital: **en marzo**, **en diciembre**.
  > 
  > Temperature is **grados**, in Celsius: *35 grados* is very hot (95°F), and *1 grado* is almost freezing. Porteños blame everything on **la humedad**.
  > 
  > With **desde** or **hace** + a time, Spanish uses the present: *vivo acá hace dos meses* — I've been living here for two months.

- `591fcf6e` en-taxi · Two streets, not a number

  Before:

  > Porteños give the driver a corner, not a house number: *Voy a Thames y Gorriti.* Near the end you guide: *acá a la derecha*, **dejame acá**, *en la esquina está bien*.

  After:

  > Porteños give the driver a corner, not a house number: *Voy a Thames y Gorriti.*
  > 
  > Near the end you guide: *acá a la derecha*, **dejame acá**.

- `7e126434` en-taxi · Taxi, remis, app

  Before:

  > A taxi is black and yellow; you stop it in the street when the red *libre* sign is lit: *¿Está libre?* A **remis** is a car you book by phone at a fixed price. Most people now use an app: *pido un auto por la app*. A taxi driver is a **tachero**.

  After:

  > A taxi is black and yellow. You stop it in the street when the red *libre* sign is lit: *¿Está libre?* A taxi driver is a **tachero**.
  > 
  > A **remis** is a car you book by phone at a fixed price.
  > 
  > Most people now use an app: *pido un auto por la app*.

- `a5722513` en-taxi · ¿Cuánto sale hasta…?

  Before:

  > With a remis, ask the price before you get in: *¿Cuánto sale hasta el aeropuerto?* In a taxi the meter decides. Paying cash, people round up: **quedate con el vuelto** — *keep the change*.

  After:

  > With a remis, ask the price before you get in: *¿Cuánto sale hasta el aeropuerto?* In a taxi the meter decides.
  > 
  > Paying cash, people round up: **quedate con el vuelto** — *keep the change*.

- `8836c476` en-verano · Seasons are flipped

  Before:

  > Argentina is in the south, so the seasons are the other way round. **El verano** runs from late December to March, and **julio** is the middle of **el invierno**: *En enero hace calor.* Months have no capital letter.

  After:

  > Argentina is in the south, so the seasons are the other way round.
  > 
  > **El verano** runs from late December to March, and **julio** is the middle of **el invierno**: *En enero hace calor.*
  > 
  > Months have no capital letter.

- `5f0726c3` es-muy-chico · Nuevo, nueva

  Before:

  > The adjective copies its noun: *una campera nueva*, *un jean nuevo*, *los pantalones chicos*. **Grande** has one form for both: *una remera grande*, *las zapatillas grandes*. Adjectives usually come after the noun, not before as in English. With *¡Qué…!* both orders work: *¡Qué linda remera!*, *¡Qué campera fea!*

  After:

  > The adjective copies its noun: *una campera nueva*, *un jean nuevo*. **Grande** has one form for both: *una remera grande*, *las zapatillas grandes*.
  > 
  > Adjectives usually come after the noun, not before as in English.
  > 
  > With *¡Qué…!* both orders work: *¡Qué linda remera!*, *¡Qué campera fea!*

- `e9b5fcfc` esta-enfrente · Where things are

  Before:

  > **al lado de** (next to), **enfrente de** (across from), **atrás de** (behind), **adelante de** (in front of), **entre** (between). Careful: *enfrente* is across the street, not *in front*. Remember *de + el* = *del*: *al lado del kiosco*. With no place after them, drop the *de*: *está enfrente*, *vivo al lado*.

  After:

  > **al lado de** (next to), **enfrente de** (across from), **atrás de** (behind), **adelante de** (in front of), **entre** (between).
  > 
  > Careful: *enfrente* is across the street, not *in front*.
  > 
  > Remember *de + el* = *del*: *al lado del kiosco*. With no place after them, drop the *de*: *está enfrente*, *vivo al lado*.

- `c584c6c4` esta-incluido · ¿Está incluido?

  Before:

  > Ask before you pay: *¿El desayuno está incluido?* *¿Hay wifi?* *¿Hay toallas?* An **alquiler temporario** is an apartment you rent for a few days or weeks — what the booking apps offer all over Buenos Aires.

  After:

  > Ask before you pay: *¿El desayuno está incluido?* *¿Hay wifi?*
  > 
  > An **alquiler temporario** is an apartment you rent for a few days or weeks.

- `790b188b` este-buzo · Colors that don't change

  Before:

  > *Amarillo* works like *rojo*: **amarilla, amarillos**. But **gris** and **marrón** are the same for *el* and *la* — *un buzo gris*, *una campera gris* — and only add *-es* for more than one: *unas zapatillas grises*.

  After:

  > *Amarillo* works like *rojo*: **amarilla, amarillos**.
  > 
  > But **gris** and **marrón** are the same for *el* and *la*: *un buzo gris*, *una campera gris*. They only add *-es* for more than one: *unas zapatillas grises*.

- `ccecffed` este-y-ese · Este here, ese there

  Before:

  > **Este** is *this*, near you; **ese** is *that*, further away. Both match the noun: **este** café, **esa** mochila, and **estos**, **estas**, **esos**, **esas** for more than one. *Esa mochila es re linda* — **re** before an adjective is the porteño *really*.

  After:

  > **Este** is *this*, near you; **ese** is *that*, further away.
  > 
  > Both match the noun: **este** café, **esa** mochila, and **estos**, **estas**, **esos**, **esas** for more than one.
  > 
  > *Esa mochila es re linda* — **re** before an adjective is the porteño *really*.

- `3780b464` la-campera-nueva · Un pantalón, un jean

  Before:

  > One pair of pants is **un pantalón** — singular: *Mi pantalón es nuevo.* *Los pantalones* works too. The same goes for **un jean**. And **ropa** — *clothes* — is always singular: *Mi ropa es nueva.*

  After:

  > One pair of pants is **un pantalón** — singular: *Mi pantalón es nuevo.* *Los pantalones* works too. The same goes for **un jean**.
  > 
  > And **ropa** — *clothes* — is always singular: *Mi ropa es nueva.*

- `eeed7cdf` la-carniceria · The shops of a barrio

  Before:

  > Many shops are their product plus **-ería**: *carne* → **carnicería**, *pan* → *panadería*. The **almacén** is the old corner shop that sells a bit of everything.

  After:

  > Many shops are their product plus **-ería**: *carne* → **carnicería**, *pan* → *panadería*.
  > 
  > The **almacén** is the old corner shop that sells a bit of everything.

- `8eaa2931` la-rutina · Me levanto, te levantás

  Before:

  > These verbs carry a pronoun that matches the person:
  > | yo | **me** levanto | **me** acuesto |
  > | vos | **te** levantás | **te** acostás |
  > | él, ella | **se** levanta | **se** acuesta |
  > | nosotros | **nos** levantamos | |
  > The pronoun goes before the verb. *Acostarse* and *almorzar* change *o → ue*, but not with vos: **me acuesto**, **almuerzo** — **te acostás**, **almorzás**. 

  After:

  > These verbs carry a pronoun that matches the person:
  > | yo | **me** levanto | **me** acuesto |
  > | vos | **te** levantás | **te** acostás |
  > | él, ella | **se** levanta | **se** acuesta |
  > | nosotros | **nos** levantamos | |
  > The pronoun goes before the verb.
  > 
  > *Acostarse* and *almorzar* change *o → ue*, but not with vos: **me acuesto**, **almuerzo** — **te acostás**, **almorzás**.

- `1f894de9` lo-llevo · Me queda bien

  Before:

  > To say how clothes fit, use **me queda**: *Esta camisa me queda bien* — *this shirt fits me well*. *Me queda chico* — *it's too small for me*. *El vestido me queda grande* — *the dress is too big for me*.

  After:

  > To say how clothes fit, use **me queda**: *Esta camisa me queda bien* — *this shirt fits me well*. *Me queda chico* — *it's too small for me*.

- `834d42dc` lo-llevo · Talle

  Before:

  > Your clothing size is your **talle**: *¿Qué talle usás?* (you'll also hear *¿Qué talle sos?*) Argentine sizes run small, so ask to try it on before you pay: **¿Puedo probarme esto?**

  After:

  > Your clothing size is your **talle**: *¿Qué talle usás?* (you'll also hear *¿Qué talle sos?*)
  > 
  > Argentine sizes run small, so ask to try it on before you pay: **¿Puedo probarme esto?**

- `34a36300` me-cobras · Thousands

  Before:

  > Prices in Argentina run to thousands: **dos mil**, **quince mil**, **cien mil** — *mil* never takes an *s*. A **millón** does: *dos millones* — and takes **de** before the thing: *un millón de pesos*.

  After:

  > Prices in Argentina run to thousands: **dos mil**, **quince mil**, **cien mil** — *mil* never takes an *s*.
  > 
  > A **millón** does: *dos millones*. It takes **de** before the thing: *un millón de pesos*.

- `cb4863d6` me-cobras · ¿Me cobrás?

  Before:

  > To pay, you don't say *I want to pay*: you ask them to charge you — **¿me cobrás?** The change they give back is **el vuelto**; small coins and bills are **cambio**. **Cobrar** is also *to get paid*: *¿Cuándo cobrás?* — *when do you get paid?*

  After:

  > To pay, you ask them to charge you: **¿me cobrás?**
  > 
  > The change they give back is **el vuelto**; small coins and bills are **cambio**.
  > 
  > **Cobrar** is also *to get paid*: *¿Cuándo cobrás?* — *when do you get paid?*

- `63f1c92c` me-despierto-temprano · Me despierto, se despiertan

  Before:

  > Waking up and getting up are two verbs: **me despierto** at seven, **me levanto** at seven-thirty. With vos the stem doesn't change: **te despertás**. The little word follows the person: *me, te, se, nos* — and **se** again for *ellos*: **se levantan**, **se acuestan**.

  After:

  > Waking up and getting up are two verbs: **me despierto** at seven, **me levanto** at seven-thirty.
  > 
  > With vos the stem doesn't change: **te despertás**.
  > 
  > The little word follows the person: *me, te, se, nos* — and **se** again for *ellos*: **se levantan**, **se acuestan**.

- `edcf0fa6` me-despierto-temprano · Me voy

  Before:

  > *Voy* is where you're going; **me voy** is that you're leaving: *me voy a las ocho*. *¿Te vas?* — *Sí, me voy.* And when someone is slow to leave: **¡Dale, apurate!** — *come on, hurry up!*

  After:

  > *Voy* is where you're going; **me voy** is that you're leaving: *me voy a las ocho*. *¿Te vas?* — *Sí, me voy.*
  > 
  > When someone is slow to leave: **¡Dale, apurate!** — *come on, hurry up!*

- `62e81a46` me-encanta-viajar · Salir a pasear

  Before:

  > **Pasear** is to go out with no hurry and no plan, just to walk around and look: *Los domingos salimos a pasear.* With a dog it means to walk it: *Voy a pasear al perro.*

  After:

  > **Pasear** is to go out with no hurry and no plan: *Los domingos salimos a pasear.*
  > 
  > With a dog it means to walk it: *Voy a pasear al perro.*

- `66c19e01` me-interesa · Le gusta, les interesa

  Before:

  > The thing liked is the subject; the pronoun says who likes it:
  > | a mí | **me** gusta nadar | **me** interesa el tenis |
  > | a vos | **te** gusta viajar | **te** interesan los deportes |
  > | a él, a ella | **le** gusta cantar | **le** interesa la música |
  > | a nosotros | **nos** gusta pasear | **nos** encanta la playa |
  > | a ellos, a ustedes | **les** gusta el fútbol | **les** encantan las series |
  > With a verb it stays singular: *me gusta nadar y cantar*. To name the person, put **a** in front and keep **le**: *A mi hermano le gusta nadar.* **Odiar** is a normal verb: *odio, odiás*.

  After:

  > The thing liked is the subject; the pronoun says who likes it:
  > | a mí | **me** gusta nadar | **me** interesa el tenis |
  > | a vos | **te** gusta viajar | **te** interesan los deportes |
  > | a él, a ella | **le** gusta cantar | **le** interesa la música |
  > | a nosotros | **nos** gusta pasear | **nos** encanta la playa |
  > | a ellos, a ustedes | **les** gusta el fútbol | **les** encantan las series |
  > With a verb it stays singular: *me gusta nadar y cantar*.
  > 
  > To name the person, put **a** in front and keep **le**: *A mi hermano le gusta nadar.*
  > 
  > **Odiar** is a normal verb: *odio, odiás*.

- `dc9f86b5` pasa-toma · The short ones

  Before:

  > Three short commands you will hear all day: *hacer* gives **hacé**, *poner* gives **poné**, and *dar* gives **dame** — *give me*: *dame otro*. Never *haz* or *pon* — those belong to another Spanish.

  After:

  > Three short commands: *hacer* gives **hacé**, *poner* gives **poné**, and *dar* gives **dame** — *give me*: *dame otro*.
  > 
  > Never *haz* or *pon* — those belong to another Spanish.

- `440071f8` pasame-tu-numero · ¿Me pasás tu número?

  Before:

  > This is the normal way to ask for someone's number — friendly, not pushy. They may say **agendame** — *save my number* — and read it out. You answer: *te agendo*.

  After:

  > This is the normal way to ask for someone's number — friendly, not pushy.
  > 
  > They may say **agendame** — *save my number* — and read it out. You answer: *te agendo*.

- `57dbf66a` pasame-tu-numero · Te escribo

  Before:

  > Everything runs on WhatsApp: plans, shops, even the doctor. **Te escribo** — *I'll text you*; **mandame un mensaje**; **llamame**. On Instagram: **seguime** — *follow me*.

  After:

  > Everything runs on WhatsApp: plans, shops, even the doctor.
  > 
  > **Te escribo** — *I'll text you*; **mandame un mensaje**; **llamame**.
  > 
  > On Instagram: **seguime** — *follow me*.

- `034163a3` practica-me-cobras · Esa campera, ese buzo

  Before:

  > The word for *that* matches the thing: **esa campera**, **ese buzo**, **esas zapatillas**. When you point without naming it, **eso** never changes.

  After:

  > The word for *that* matches the thing: **esa campera**, **ese buzo**, **esas zapatillas**.
  > 
  > When you point without naming it, **eso** never changes.

- `a9c6a548` practica-me-cobras · Commands in the shop

  Before:

  > The vos command is everywhere in a shop: **mirá**, **fijate**, **esperame**, **tomá** — *tomá, tu vuelto*. Drop the *-r* and stress the last vowel of the verb: *mirá*, *tomá*. With *me* or *te* added, the stress stays there: *fijate* (fi-JA-te), *esperame* (es-pe-RA-me).

  After:

  > The vos command is everywhere in a shop: **mirá**, **fijate**, **esperame**, **tomá** — *tomá, tu vuelto*.
  > 
  > Drop the *-r* and stress the last vowel of the verb: *mirá*, *tomá*.
  > 
  > With *me* or *te* added, the stress stays there: *fijate* (fi-JA-te), *esperame* (es-pe-RA-me).

- `4dbef0fb` preferis-salir · Prefiero, preferís

  Before:

  > More verbs that stretch their stem — but never with vos:
  > | | preferir | volver | empezar |
  > | yo | pref**ie**ro | v**ue**lvo | emp**ie**zo |
  > | vos | **preferís** | **volvés** | **empezás** |
  > | él, ella | pref**ie**re | v**ue**lve | emp**ie**za |
  > | ellos | pref**ie**ren | v**ue**lven | emp**ie**zan |
  > The same as *quiero, querés* and *puedo, podés*. **Nosotros** stays short too: *preferimos, volvemos, empezamos*. Add an infinitive for the plan: *¿Preferís ir al teatro?*

  After:

  > More verbs that stretch their stem — but never with vos:
  > | | preferir | volver | empezar |
  > | yo | pref**ie**ro | v**ue**lvo | emp**ie**zo |
  > | vos | **preferís** | **volvés** | **empezás** |
  > | él, ella | pref**ie**re | v**ue**lve | emp**ie**za |
  > | ellos | pref**ie**ren | v**ue**lven | emp**ie**zan |
  > The same as *quiero, querés* and *puedo, podés*. **Nosotros** stays short too: *preferimos, volvemos, empezamos*.
  > 
  > Add an infinitive for the plan: *¿Preferís ir al teatro?*

- `f255cb1b` que-te-gusta-hacer · Me gusta + doing something

  Before:

  > With a verb, **gusta** and **encanta** stay singular, even for two activities: *Me gusta leer y cocinar.* With things they follow the thing: *Me encanta el fútbol*, *me encantan las series*. To agree: **A mí también** after a *yes*, **A mí tampoco** after a *no*. With a normal verb it's **yo**: *No bailo.* — *Yo tampoco.*

  After:

  > With a verb, **gusta** and **encanta** stay singular, even for two activities: *Me gusta leer y cocinar.*
  > 
  > With things they follow the thing: *Me encanta el fútbol*, *me encantan las series*.
  > 
  > To agree: **A mí también** after a *yes*, **A mí tampoco** after a *no*. With a normal verb it's **yo**: *No bailo.* — *Yo tampoco.*

- `78286e2b` queres-podes-vas · Querer, poder, ir

  Before:

  > | | querer | poder | ir |
  > | yo | quiero | puedo | voy |
  > | vos | **querés** | **podés** | vas |
  > | él, ella | quiere | puede | va |
  > | nosotros | **queremos** | **podemos** | vamos |
  > | ellos, ustedes | quieren | pueden | van |
  > 
  > *e → ie* and *o → ue* everywhere except **vos** and **nosotros**. *Ir* is its own thing, and takes **a** + place (*a + el* = **al**): *voy al súper*, *voy a la facu*. **Salir** only bends in *yo*: **salgo**, *salís*, *sale*. Add an infinitive for plans: *¿Querés salir?*

  After:

  > | | querer | poder | ir |
  > | yo | quiero | puedo | voy |
  > | vos | **querés** | **podés** | vas |
  > | él, ella | quiere | puede | va |
  > | nosotros | **queremos** | **podemos** | vamos |
  > | ellos, ustedes | quieren | pueden | van |
  > 
  > *e → ie* and *o → ue* everywhere except **vos** and **nosotros**.
  > 
  > *Ir* is its own thing, and takes **a** + place (*a + el* = **al**): *voy al súper*, *voy a la facu*.
  > 
  > **Salir** only bends in *yo*: **salgo**, *salís*, *sale*. Add an infinitive for plans: *¿Querés salir?*

- `14ab3922` ropa-y-colores · Colors match the noun

  Before:

  > Colors match their noun like any adjective: *un pantalón negro*, *una remera negra*, *zapatillas negras*. Ones ending in *-e* or a consonant only change for more than one: *verde* → **verdes**, *azul* → **azules**. The color comes after the noun.

  After:

  > Colors match their noun like any adjective: *un pantalón negro*, *una remera negra*, *zapatillas negras*. The color comes after the noun.
  > 
  > Ones ending in *-e* or a consonant only change for more than one: *verde* → **verdes**, *azul* → **azules**.

- `7125b8a8` ropa-y-colores · The black one

  Before:

  > To say *the black one*, drop the noun and keep **el** or **la**: *¿El negro o el azul?* — *the black one or the blue one?* *Quiero la roja* — *I want the red one*. The same works with *este* and *ese*: *Quiero este* — *I want this one*.

  After:

  > To say *the black one*, drop the noun and keep **el** or **la**: *¿El negro o el azul?* — *the black one or the blue one?* *Quiero la roja* — *I want the red one*.
  > 
  > The same works with *este* and *ese*: *Quiero este* — *I want this one*.

- `c51b5304` segui-derecho · Directions in vos

  Before:

  > Every direction is a vos command: drop the *-r*, stress the last vowel.
  > | doblar | **doblá** | Doblá a la derecha. |
  > | cruzar | **cruzá** | Cruzá la avenida. |
  > | caminar | **caminá** | Caminá dos cuadras. |
  > | seguir | **seguí** | Seguí derecho. |
  > | parar | **pará** | ¡Pará! |
  > *Seguir* says *sigo*, but its command is regular: **seguí**. With a pronoun it joins the end: *bajarse* → **bajate**, *subirse* → **subite**.

  After:

  > Every direction is a vos command: drop the *-r*, stress the last vowel.
  > | doblar | **doblá** | Doblá a la derecha. |
  > | cruzar | **cruzá** | Cruzá la avenida. |
  > | caminar | **caminá** | Caminá dos cuadras. |
  > | seguir | **seguí** | Seguí derecho. |
  > | parar | **pará** | ¡Pará! |
  > *Seguir* says *sigo*, but its command is regular: **seguí**.
  > 
  > With a pronoun it joins the end: *bajarse* → **bajate**, *subirse* → **subite**.

- `6111b576` series-y-musica · Una peli

  Before:

  > **Peli** is short for **película**, and it is what people really say: *¿Querés mirar una peli?* A show with many episodes is a **serie**: *Me encantan las series.*

  After:

  > **Peli** is short for **película**: *¿Querés mirar una peli?*
  > 
  > A show with many episodes is a **serie**: *Me encantan las series.*

- `52586356` tengo-una-reserva · Tengo una reserva

  Before:

  > At the front desk one line does it: **tengo una reserva a nombre de** … and your name. They will ask for your **pasaporte**. Staying two nights? *Dos noches.*

  After:

  > At the front desk, say **tengo una reserva a nombre de** … and your name.
  > 
  > They will ask for your **pasaporte**.
  > 
  > Staying two nights? *Dos noches.*

- `4e70b774` todos-los-dias · Los sábados, todos los días

  Before:

  > **Los sábados** means *on Saturdays* — every Saturday: *Los sábados me levanto tarde.* *Every day* is **todos los días**, and *once a week* is **una vez por semana**. **El finde** is the short way to say the weekend.

  After:

  > **Los sábados** means *on Saturdays* — every Saturday: *Los sábados me levanto tarde.*
  > 
  > *Every day* is **todos los días**, and *once a week* is **una vez por semana**.
  > 
  > **El finde** is the short way to say the weekend.

- `621d77e5` tomar-el-bondi · Me bajo en la próxima

  Before:

  > **Me bajo en la próxima** — *I'm getting off at the next stop*. Don't know the stop? *¿Cuántas paradas faltan?* or simply *¿Me avisás?* In the subte, changing lines is a **combinación**, and you wait on the **andén**.

  After:

  > **Me bajo en la próxima** — *I'm getting off at the next stop*.
  > 
  > Don't know the stop? *¿Cuántas paradas faltan?* or simply *¿Me avisás?*
  > 
  > In the subte, changing lines is a **combinación**, and you wait on the **andén**.

- `e03a678a` tomar-el-bondi · Tell the driver where you're going

  Before:

  > On a colectivo the **boleto** — the fare — depends on how far you go, so you tell the driver first (*hasta Palermo*) and then tap. Not sure it's the right bus? *¿Va a Palermo?*

  After:

  > On a colectivo the **boleto** — the fare — depends on how far you go. Tell the driver first (*hasta Palermo*), then tap.
  > 
  > Not sure it's the right bus? *¿Va a Palermo?*

- `f8d95867` tomar-el-bondi · No cash on the bondi

  Before:
