  > You can't pay the driver in cash. You tap a **SUBE** card on the reader — on buses, the subte and trains (many now take a bank card or phone too). You load it at a kiosco or a subte station: *¿Me cargás la SUBE?* When the **saldo** runs out, load it again.

  After:

  > You can't pay the driver in cash. You tap a **SUBE** card on buses, the subte and trains (many now take a bank card or phone too).
  > 
  > You load it at a kiosco or a subte station: *¿Me cargás la SUBE?* When the **saldo** runs out, load it again.

- `eb18db97` vuelvo-temprano · Pienso ir, creo que…

  Before:

  > **Pienso** + verb is *I plan to*: *pienso ir al cine*, *¿pensás salir hoy?* **Creo que** … is *I think that* …: *creo que está cerrado*, *¿creés que abre hoy?* And **no creo** is *I don't think so*.

  After:

  > **Pienso** + verb is *I plan to*: *pienso ir al cine*, *¿pensás salir hoy?*
  > 
  > **Creo que** … is *I think that* …: *creo que está cerrado*, *¿creés que abre hoy?*
  > 
  > And **no creo** is *I don't think so*.

- `54719e83` ahora-y-planes · El próximo, la próxima

  Before:

  > **El próximo viernes** is *next Friday* — the same as *el viernes que viene*; **el próximo finde** is *next weekend*. **La próxima** alone is *next time*: *La próxima invito yo.* **La semana que viene** — *next week*, the week that comes — is the form you will hear most.

  After:

  > **El próximo viernes** is *next Friday*, the same as *el viernes que viene*; **el próximo finde** is *next weekend*.
  > 
  > **La próxima** alone is *next time*: *La próxima invito yo.*
  > 
  > **La semana que viene** — *next week* — is the form you will hear most.

- `aff1bd39` barri-y-planche · El lavarropas

  Before:

  > Clothes go in the **lavarropas**; to **planchar** them is optional for most porteños. The floor: **barrer** with the **escoba**, then you **pasás el trapo** — the wet *trapo de piso*.

  After:

  > Clothes go in the **lavarropas**; to **planchar** them is optional for most porteños.
  > 
  > The floor: **barrer** with the **escoba**, then you **pasás el trapo** — the wet *trapo de piso*.

- `9e653eb3` cenamos-afuera · Pagué, llegué

  Before:

  > In *yo* the spelling bends to keep the sound: *pagar* → **pagué**, *llegar* → **llegué**. At the end of the night someone says **pago yo** — and the next day, **ayer pagué yo, hoy te toca a vos**.

  After:

  > In *yo* the spelling bends to keep the sound: *pagar* → **pagué**, *llegar* → **llegué**.
  > 
  > At the end of the night someone says **pago yo** — and the next day, **ayer pagué yo, hoy te toca a vos**.

- `0a6e13e7` comi-y-sali · The past of -er and -ir

  Before:

  > | | -ar | -er, -ir |
  > | yo | labur**é** | com**í**, sal**í** |
  > | vos | labur**aste** | com**iste**, sal**iste** |
  > | él, ella | labur**ó** | com**ió**, sal**ió** |
  > | nosotros | labur**amos** | com**imos**, sal**imos** |
  > -er and -ir share one set of endings. *Salimos* is both *we go out* and *we went out* — *anoche* or *ayer* tells you which.

  After:

  > | | -ar | -er, -ir |
  > | yo | labur**é** | com**í**, sal**í** |
  > | vos | labur**aste** | com**iste**, sal**iste** |
  > | él, ella | labur**ó** | com**ió**, sal**ió** |
  > | nosotros | labur**amos** | com**imos**, sal**imos** |
  > 
  > -er and -ir share one set of endings.
  > 
  > *Salimos* is both *we go out* and *we went out* — *anoche* or *ayer* tells you which.

- `79ace8ff` conoci-a-alguien · Conocer, saber

  Before:

  > **Saber** is to know a fact or how to do something: *sé dónde vive*. **Conocer** is to know a person or a place: **conozco a Juan**, *¿conocés Córdoba?* In the past it changes meaning: **conocí a Juan** — *I met Juan* (for the first time).

  After:

  > **Saber** is to know a fact or how to do something: *sé dónde vive*.
  > 
  > **Conocer** is to know a person or a place: **conozco a Juan**, *¿conocés Córdoba?*
  > 
  > In the past it changes meaning: **conocí a Juan** — *I met Juan* (for the first time).

- `5877b8a4` de-viaje · Fui a, me fui a

  Before:

  > For where you went, porteños mostly say **fui a** or **me fui a**: *me fui a Bariloche*, *¿adónde te fuiste en enero?* **Viajar** is more about the trip itself: *viajé en micro*, *viajé sola*, *viajo mañana*. *Viajé a Bariloche* is fine too, just less everyday.

  After:

  > For where you went, porteños mostly say **fui a** or **me fui a**: *me fui a Bariloche*, *¿adónde te fuiste en enero?*
  > 
  > **Viajar** is more about the trip itself: *viajé en micro*, *viajé sola*. *Viajé a Bariloche* is fine too, just less everyday.

- `bd699582` de-viaje · January, and e for y

  Before:

  > Porteños go on **vacaciones** in January and February, and *la costa* — Mar del Plata, Pinamar — is where half of Buenos Aires ends up. A small detail: **y** turns into **e** before an *i* sound — *fuimos a la montaña e hizo mucho frío*.

  After:

  > Porteños go on **vacaciones** in January and February. Half of Buenos Aires ends up on *la costa* — Mar del Plata, Pinamar.
  > 
  > A small detail: **y** turns into **e** before an *i* sound — *fuimos a la montaña e hizo mucho frío*.

- `3983d478` el-cumple · -ando, -iendo

  Before:

  > Right now: **estar** + the verb in *-ando* (for -ar verbs) or *-iendo* (for -er and -ir): *estoy cocin**ando***, *está escrib**iendo***. Two to learn by ear: **leyendo**, **durmiendo**.

  After:

  > Right now: **estar** + the verb in *-ando* (for -ar verbs) or *-iendo* (for -er and -ir): *estoy cocin**ando***, *está escrib**iendo***.
  > 
  > Two to learn by ear: **leyendo**, **durmiendo**.

- `4ff7f7af` el-finde · Después, entonces

  Before:

  > Tell a story in order with **primero**, **después**, **al final**: *Primero comimos, después vimos una película.* **Entonces** is mostly *so*: *No pude ir, entonces vi el partido en casa.*

  After:

  > Tell a story in order with **primero**, **después**, **al final**: *Primero comimos, después vimos una película.*
  > 
  > **Entonces** is mostly *so*: *No pude ir, entonces vi el partido en casa.*

- `9e470499` el-finde · ¿Viste?

  Before:

  > **Viste** is *you saw*: **¿Viste el partido?** — *Did you see the game?* Porteños also add **¿viste?** at the end of a sentence to mean *you know?*: *Es re caro, ¿viste?*

  After:

  > **Viste** is *you saw*: **¿Viste el partido?** — *Did you see the game?*
  > 
  > Porteños also add **¿viste?** at the end of a sentence to mean *you know?*: *Es re caro, ¿viste?*

- `56a5e066` el-mas-tranquilo · Lejísimos

  Before:

  > *-ísimo* works on almost anything: **baratísimo**, **lindísima**, and even on *lejos*: **queda lejísimos** — *it's really far away*. It's Spanish everywhere; the everyday porteño *really* is still **re**: *re lindo*.

  After:

  > *-ísimo* works on almost anything: **baratísimo**, **lindísima**. Even on *lejos*: **queda lejísimos** — *it's really far away*.
  > 
  > It's Spanish everywhere; the everyday porteño *really* is still **re**: *re lindo*.

- `f81f6472` estoy-llegando · -ando, -iendo

  Before:

  > Verbs in *-ar* take **-ando**: **laburando**, **esperando**, **tomando**. Verbs in *-er* and *-ir* take **-iendo**: **comiendo**, **haciendo**. That ending never changes — only *estar* does: *Estoy comiendo.* *Está tomando un café.*

  After:

  > Verbs in *-ar* take **-ando**: **laburando**, **esperando**, **tomando**. Verbs in *-er* and *-ir* take **-iendo**: **comiendo**, **haciendo**.
  > 
  > That ending never changes — only *estar* does: *Estoy comiendo.* *Está tomando un café.*

- `ffce8d24` estoy-llegando · Ya, todavía, casi

  Before:

  > **Ya** is *already* or *right now*; **todavía** is *still*: *Todavía estoy laburando.* *Todavía no* is *not yet*. **Casi** is *almost*. One warning: in Buenos Aires **ya estoy llegando** can mean you are still ten minutes away.

  After:

  > **Ya** is *already* or *right now*; **todavía** is *still*: *Todavía estoy laburando.* *Todavía no* is *not yet*.
  > 
  > **Casi** is *almost*.
  > 
  > In Buenos Aires **ya estoy llegando** can mean you are still ten minutes away.

- `9bbe1671` feliz-cumple · El cumple

  Before:

  > Nobody says *cumpleaños* in a hurry: it's **el cumple** — *¡Feliz cumple!* The verb is **cumplir**, to turn an age: *¿Cuántos años cumplís?* — *Cumplo treinta.* And *cumplís años el domingo* means your birthday is on Sunday.

  After:

  > People shorten *cumpleaños* to **el cumple**: *¡Feliz cumple!*
  > 
  > The verb is **cumplir**, to turn an age: *¿Cuántos años cumplís?* — *Cumplo treinta.*
  > 
  > And *cumplís años el domingo* means your birthday is on Sunday.

- `d848ca5a` feliz-cumple · Voy a + verb

  Before:

  > For plans, use **ir a** + a verb: *Vamos a organizar el cumple.* *¿Quién va a preparar el café?* A little word like *te* or *me* goes before it: **te voy a esperar**, *¿me vas a ayudar?* You will also hear *voy a esperarte*.

  After:

  > For plans, use **ir a** + a verb: *Vamos a organizar el cumple.* *¿Quién va a preparar el café?*
  > 
  > A little word like *te* or *me* goes before it: **te voy a esperar**, *¿me vas a ayudar?* You will also hear *voy a esperarte*.

- `09e2149a` ganamos-el-partido · Ganamos, perdimos

  Before:

  > **Ganamos** is both *we win* and *we won*; the rest of the sentence tells you which: *Ayer ganamos el partido*. With **perder** the two are different: *perdemos* is *we lose* and **perdimos** is *we lost*.

  After:

  > **Ganamos** is both *we win* and *we won*; the rest of the sentence tells you which: *Ayer ganamos el partido*.
  > 
  > With **perder** the two are different: *perdemos* is *we lose* and **perdimos** is *we lost*.

- `e6e2d7cb` ida-y-vuelta · Micro, not colectivo

  Before:

  > The *colectivo* crosses the city; to go to another city you take the **micro** (the long-distance bus). You buy the **pasaje** at the terminal or online. A round-trip ticket is **ida y vuelta**. In Buenos Aires the micros leave from the **terminal** of Retiro, and the airports are Ezeiza (international) and Aeroparque (in the city).

  After:

  > The *colectivo* crosses the city. To go to another city you take the **micro** (the long-distance bus).
  > 
  > You buy the **pasaje** at the terminal or online. A round-trip ticket is **ida y vuelta**.
  > 
  > In Buenos Aires the micros leave from the **terminal** of Retiro. The airports are Ezeiza (international) and Aeroparque (in the city).

- `9f882e99` la-pasamos-barbaro · Estuvimos, tuvimos, la pasamos

  Before:

  > | | estar | tener | pasarla |
  > | yo | estuve | tuve | **la pasé** |
  > | vos | estuviste | tuviste | **la pasaste** |
  > | él, ella | estuvo | tuvo | **la pasó** |
  > | nosotros | **estuvimos** | **tuvimos** | **la pasamos** |
  > | ellos | **estuvieron** | **tuvieron** | **la pasaron** |
  > *Estar* and *tener* get an odd stem (*estuv-*, *tuv-*) for every person, with no accent. *Pasarla* is regular: **la pasé**, **la pasó** keep the accent. **¿Cómo estuvo?** — *how was it?* — **Estuvo increíble, la pasamos bárbaro.**

  After:

  > | | estar | tener | pasarla |
  > | yo | estuve | tuve | **la pasé** |
  > | vos | estuviste | tuviste | **la pasaste** |
  > | él, ella | estuvo | tuvo | **la pasó** |
  > | nosotros | **estuvimos** | **tuvimos** | **la pasamos** |
  > | ellos | **estuvieron** | **tuvieron** | **la pasaron** |
  > 
  > *Estar* and *tener* get an odd stem (*estuv-*, *tuv-*) for every person, with no accent. *Pasarla* is regular: **la pasé**, **la pasó** keep the accent.
  > 
  > **¿Cómo estuvo?** — *how was it?* — **Estuvo increíble, la pasamos bárbaro.**

- `fc227694` la-pasamos-barbaro · La pasé bárbaro

  Before:

  > A porteño doesn't *have fun*, he **la pasa**: **la pasé bárbaro**, **la pasamos re bien**, **¿la pasaste bien?** The *la* never changes and never means anything — it's just part of the phrase.

  After:

  > A porteño doesn't *have fun*, he **la pasa**: **la pasé bárbaro**, **la pasamos re bien**, **¿la pasaste bien?**
  > 
  > The *la* never changes. It's just part of the phrase.

- `35b971dd` las-tareas · Me toca

  Before:

  > **Me toca a mí** — *it's my turn*. **¿A quién le toca?** — *whose turn is it?* The chore follows in its basic form (*lavar*, *sacar*, *limpiar*): **te toca lavar los platos**. Add *a mí* / *a vos* for stress: **hoy te toca a vos**.

  After:

  > **Me toca a mí** — *it's my turn*. **¿A quién le toca?** — *whose turn is it?*
  > 
  > The chore follows in its basic form (*lavar*, *sacar*, *limpiar*): **te toca lavar los platos**.
  > 
  > Add *a mí* / *a vos* for stress: **hoy te toca a vos**.

- `3e0c5f85` las-vacaciones · La costa, las sierras

  Before:

  > For a porteño **la costa** is the Atlantic beaches south of the city, where you rent a **carpa** — a beach tent by the day. The other classic is **las sierras** of Córdoba: a **río** instead of the sea. A **carpa** is also a normal camping tent: *me fui al río con la carpa*.

  After:

  > For a porteño **la costa** is the Atlantic beaches south of the city. There you rent a **carpa** — a beach tent by the day.
  > 
  > The other option is **las sierras** of Córdoba: a **río** instead of the sea.
  > 
  > A **carpa** is also a normal camping tent: *me fui al río con la carpa*.

- `7e1f82d2` llegaron-tarde · The -ar past for everyone

  Before:

  > | | cenar | pagar | llegar |
  > | yo | cen**é** | pag**ué** | lleg**ué** |
  > | vos | cen**aste** | pag**aste** | lleg**aste** |
  > | él, ella | cen**ó** | pag**ó** | lleg**ó** |
  > | nosotros | cen**amos** | pag**amos** | lleg**amos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | cen**aron** | pag**aron** | lleg**aron** |
  > *-amos* looks like the present, so the time says which: **anoche cenamos afuera**. *-aron* is only ever the past: *hablaron, tomaron*. In *yo*, *-gar* verbs add a *u* to keep the hard *g*: **pagué**, **llegué**.

  After:

  > | | cenar | pagar | llegar |
  > | yo | cen**é** | pag**ué** | lleg**ué** |
  > | vos | cen**aste** | pag**aste** | lleg**aste** |
  > | él, ella | cen**ó** | pag**ó** | lleg**ó** |
  > | nosotros | cen**amos** | pag**amos** | lleg**amos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | cen**aron** | pag**aron** | lleg**aron** |
  > 
  > *-amos* looks like the present, so the time says which: **anoche cenamos afuera**. *-aron* is only ever the past: *hablaron, tomaron*.
  > 
  > In *yo*, *-gar* verbs add a *u* to keep the hard *g*: **pagué**, **llegué**.

- `8413be7f` mas-alto-que · Comparing

  Before:

  > | more than | **más** alto **que** yo |
  > | less than | **menos** caro **que** |
  > | as … as | **tan** lindo **como** |
  > | better, worse | **mejor**, **peor** |
  > | older, younger | **más grande**, **más chico** |
  > | really | rico → **riquísimo**, caro → **carísimo** |
  > The describing word still matches the person: *mi hermana es más alta que yo*. Say **mejor** for *better*; about a person, **más buena** means *nicer*. **El más chico** is *the youngest*, **el peor** *the worst*.

  After:

  > | more than | **más** alto **que** yo |
  > | less than | **menos** caro **que** |
  > | as … as | **tan** lindo **como** |
  > | better, worse | **mejor**, **peor** |
  > | older, younger | **más grande**, **más chico** |
  > | really | rico → **riquísimo**, caro → **carísimo** |
  > 
  > The describing word still matches the person: *mi hermana es más alta que yo*.
  > 
  > Say **mejor** for *better*; about a person, **más buena** means *nicer*. **El más chico** is *the youngest*, **el peor** *the worst*.

- `1ef9dc5b` me-dolio · Me dolió

  Before:

  > *Doler* has a past too, and it stays glued to its *me*: **me dolió la cabeza** — *my head hurt*, **¿te dolió?**, **a mi viejo le dolió la rodilla**. When more than one thing hurt, it's **dolieron**, just like *me duelen*: **me dolieron los ojos** — *my eyes hurt*.

  After:

  > *Doler* has a past too, and it keeps its *me*: **me dolió la cabeza** — *my head hurt*; **¿te dolió?**; **a mi viejo le dolió la rodilla**.
  > 
  > When more than one thing hurt, it's **dolieron**, just like *me duelen*: **me dolieron los ojos** — *my eyes hurt*.

- `e09dc1af` me-duele · Doler works like gustar

  Before:

  > The thing that hurts is the subject: **me duele la cabeza** — *my head hurts*, **me duelen los pies** — *my feet hurt*. The *me* says whose, so Spanish uses *la*, not *mi*. For someone else, name them with *a*: **a Juan le duele la espalda** — *Juan's back hurts*.

  After:

  > The thing that hurts is the subject: **me duele la cabeza** — *my head hurts*; **me duelen los pies** — *my feet hurt*.
  > 
  > The *me* says whose, so Spanish uses *la*, not *mi*.
  > 
  > For someone else, name them with *a*: **a Juan le duele la espalda** — *Juan's back hurts*.

- `ef4a4d49` naci-en · Your story: nací, me crié, viví

  Before:

  > Tell your life story with the past you already know: **nací** (*I was born*), **viví**, **volví**. **Me crié** is *I grew up*, and it always takes **me**: *Nací en Italia, pero me crié en Buenos Aires.* To ask: *¿Te criaste acá?*

  After:

  > Tell your life story with the past you already know: **nací** (*I was born*), **viví**, **volví**.
  > 
  > **Me crié** is *I grew up*, and it always takes **me**: *Nací en Italia, pero me crié en Buenos Aires.* To ask: *¿Te criaste acá?*

- `146ce785` no-pude-ir · No pude

  Before:

  > **No pude** means you wanted to or tried, and it did not happen: *No pude ir al asado*. **Dije**, **vine** and **pude** have no accent, but **leer** is regular and keeps it: *leí, leíste*.

  After:

  > **No pude** means you wanted to or tried, and it did not happen: *No pude ir al asado*.
  > 
  > **Dije**, **vine** and **pude** have no accent, but **leer** is regular and keeps it: *leí, leíste*.

- `717a288c` no-pudieron · More of the irregular past

  Before:

  > | | traer | querer | venir | poder |
  > | yo | traje | quise | vine | pude |
  > | vos | trajiste | quisiste | viniste | pudiste |
  > | él, ella | trajo | quiso | vino | pudo |
  > | nosotros | | | vinimos | pudimos |
  > | ellos | trajeron | | vinieron | pudieron |
  > Same endings as *hice*: *-e, -iste, -o, -imos, -ieron* — after a *j*, just *-eron*: **dijeron, trajeron**. **No vino nadie** — *nobody came*: after the verb, *nadie* still needs the *no*; in front, it doesn't: *nadie trajo hielo*.

  After:

  > | | traer | querer | venir | poder |
  > | yo | traje | quise | vine | pude |
  > | vos | trajiste | quisiste | viniste | pudiste |
  > | él, ella | trajo | quiso | vino | pudo |
  > | nosotros | | | vinimos | pudimos |
  > | ellos | trajeron | | vinieron | pudieron |
  > 
  > Same endings as *hice*: *-e, -iste, -o, -imos, -ieron*. After a *j*, just *-eron*: **dijeron, trajeron**.
  > 
  > **No vino nadie** — *nobody came*. After the verb, *nadie* still needs the *no*; in front, it doesn't: *nadie trajo hielo*.

- `c585fa33` no-se · Ni idea

  Before:

  > **Ni idea** — *no idea* — is the everyday short answer when you don't know: *—¿Dónde está Nico? —Ni idea.* The full form is **no tengo idea** or **no tengo ni idea**. To say what about: *Ni idea de dónde está el cargador.*

  After:

  > **Ni idea** — *no idea* — is the short answer when you don't know: *—¿Dónde está Nico? —Ni idea.*
  > 
  > The full form is **no tengo idea** or **no tengo ni idea**.
  > 
  > To say what about: *Ni idea de dónde está el cargador.*

- `bd5cb236` paseamos-por-las-sierras · The holiday is a we

  Before:

  > Holidays are told in *nosotros*: **alquilamos**, **nadamos**, **paseamos**, **sacamos mil fotos**. And the weather that ruined it: **llovió toda la semana**. In -ar verbs *nosotros* is the same now and in the past: **nadamos** is *we swim* or *we swam*; *ayer* or *en enero* tells you which.

  After:

  > Holidays are told in *nosotros*: **alquilamos**, **nadamos**, **paseamos**, **sacamos mil fotos**.
  > 
  > The weather that ruined it: **llovió toda la semana**.
  > 
  > In -ar verbs *nosotros* is the same now and in the past: **nadamos** is *we swim* or *we swam*; *ayer* or *en enero* tells you which.

- `22eec0b9` practica-como-estuvo · One past for everyone

  Before:

  > **Cené, cenaste, cenó, cenamos, cenaron**; **viví, viviste, vivió, vivimos, vivieron**. Two sets of endings, and every regular verb takes one of them: -ar verbs go like *cenar*; -er and -ir verbs go like *vivir*.

  After:

  > **Cené, cenaste, cenó, cenamos, cenaron**; **viví, viviste, vivió, vivimos, vivieron**.
  > 
  > Every regular verb takes one of these two sets of endings: -ar verbs go like *cenar*; -er and -ir verbs go like *vivir*.

- `9f2c679d` practica-como-estuvo · We: now or then?

  Before:

  > **Cenamos** and **salimos** look the same in the present and the past: *we have dinner* or *we had dinner*, *we go out* or *we went out*. Only -ar and -ir verbs do this; -er verbs change: **comemos** (now), **comimos** (then). A porteño never gets lost: **anoche**, **ayer**, **el sábado** — the time word does the work.

  After:

  > **Cenamos** and **salimos** look the same in the present and the past. They are *we have dinner* or *we had dinner*, *we go out* or *we went out*.
  > 
  > Only -ar and -ir verbs do this; -er verbs change: **comemos** (now), **comimos** (then).
  > 
  > The time word tells you which: **anoche**, **ayer**, **el sábado**.

- `a160a37a` practica-las-vacaciones · Me tocó, llovió

  Before:

  > **Me tocó** works like *gustar*: the thing is the subject, so with two things it is **me tocaron**: *me tocaron los platos y el baño*. **Llovió** has no person at all — just *it rained*.

  After:

  > **Me tocó** works like *gustar*: the thing is the subject. With two things it is **me tocaron**: *me tocaron los platos y el baño*.
  > 
  > **Llovió** has no person at all — just *it rained*.

- `a2908aec` que-decis · No veo nada

  Before:

  > English says *I can't see anything*; Spanish just says **no veo nada** — no word for *can*. The same with **ves**: *¿Ves?* is *can you see?* And the double negative is correct Spanish: *no* before the verb, *nada* after it.

  After:

  > English says *I can't see anything*; Spanish just says **no veo nada** — no word for *can*. The same with **ves**: *¿Ves?* is *can you see?*
  > 
  > The double negative is correct Spanish: *no* before the verb, *nada* after it.

- `ba82ffe4` que-decis · Digo, pongo, veo

  Before:

  > Like *vengo* and *traigo*, these verbs have an odd *I* form: **digo**, **pongo** and **veo**. With vos they are regular: **decís**, **ponés**, **ves**. *Decir* also changes **e** to **i** in **dice** and **dicen**. *—¿Qué decís? —Yo no digo nada.* To tell someone, add **le**: *¿Qué le digo a Ana?*

  After:

  > Like *vengo* and *traigo*, these verbs have an odd *I* form: **digo**, **pongo** and **veo**. With vos they are regular: **decís**, **ponés**, **ves**. *—¿Qué decís? —Yo no digo nada.*
  > 
  > *Decir* also changes **e** to **i** in **dice** and **dicen**.
  > 
  > To tell someone, add **le**: *¿Qué le digo a Ana?*

- `01e1a3a8` que-llevo-al-asado · Boludo and quilombo

  Before:

  > **Boludo** is common between close friends and an insult to anyone else — say it only to friends you know well. **Quilombo** means a mess; it's informal, not rude, but keep it out of formal settings.

  After:

  > **Boludo** is common between close friends and an insult to anyone else. Say it only to friends you know well.
  > 
  > **Quilombo** means a mess. It's informal, not rude, but keep it out of formal settings.

- `d5a4acc0` que-llevo-al-asado · Llevar or traer

  Before:

  > English *bring* is two verbs here. **Llevar** is to a place where you are not yet: *¿Qué llevo al asado?* **Traer** is to the place where you are now: *¿Podés traer hielo?* Going to a friend's place, always ask **¿qué llevo?** **Venir** works like *traer*: on your way to someone, say **ya voy**, not *vengo*.

  After:

  > English *bring* is two verbs here. **Llevar** is to a place where you are not yet: *¿Qué llevo al asado?* **Traer** is to the place where you are now: *¿Podés traer hielo?*
  > 
  > Going to a friend's place, always ask **¿qué llevo?**
  > 
  > **Venir** works like *traer*: on your way to someone, say **ya voy**, not *vengo*.

- `bd066d7e` que-paso-anoche · Hace = ago

  Before:

  > **Hace** + a time is *ago*: **llegué hace una hora** — *I got here an hour ago*; **hablé con Juan hace dos días**. *Not long ago* is **hace poco**, and the question is **¿hace cuánto?** — *how long ago?*

  After:

  > **Hace** + a time is *ago*: **llegué hace una hora** — *I got here an hour ago*; **hablé con Juan hace dos días**.
  > 
  > *Not long ago* is **hace poco**. The question is **¿hace cuánto?** — *how long ago?*

- `acd8e9ee` quien-lavo · Me tocó

  Before:

  > The turn has a past: **ayer me tocó a mí, hoy te toca a vos**. **¿A quién le tocó?** Most chores are plain -ar verbs: **lavé, limpiaste, lavó**. *Sacar* changes in *yo*: **saqué** (like *busqué*). *Barrer* is an -er verb: **barrí, barriste**.

  After:

  > The turn has a past: **ayer me tocó a mí, hoy te toca a vos**. **¿A quién le tocó?**
  > 
  > Most chores are plain -ar verbs: **lavé, limpiaste, lavó**. *Sacar* changes in *yo*: **saqué** (like *busqué*). *Barrer* is an -er verb: **barrí, barriste**.

- `d389c9c5` saco-turno · At the doctor's

  Before:

  > You ask for a **turno** (an appointment), and the verb is **sacar turno**: *tengo que sacar turno con el médico*. The **guardia** is the emergency room. **Estoy engripado** — *I've got the flu* — is what gets you the day off.

  After:

  > You ask for a **turno** (an appointment). The verb is **sacar turno**: *tengo que sacar turno con el médico*.
  > 
  > The **guardia** is the emergency room.
  > 
  > **Estoy engripado** — *I've got the flu*.

- `7fd0a81b` te-di-la-llave · Di, busqué

  Before:

  > **Dar** has a short past with no accents: **di, diste, dio** — *le di la llave*. And *buscar* bends in *yo* to keep the hard *c* sound: **busqué** (not *buscé*). **Buscar** is *to look for* and also *to pick someone up*: *busqué la billetera*, *la busqué a Mica en la estación*.

  After:

  > **Dar** has a short past with no accents: **di, diste, dio** — *le di la llave*.
  > 
  > And *buscar* bends in *yo* to keep the hard *c* sound: **busqué** (not *buscé*).
  > 
  > **Buscar** is *to look for* and also *to pick someone up*: *busqué la billetera*, *la busqué a Mica en la estación*.

- `89462fe8` te-di-la-llave · Le di la llave a Sofi

  Before:

  > Even when you name the person, the **le** stays: **le dije a Juan que no**, **le di la llave a Sofi**, **les mandé las fotos a mis viejos**. English says it once; a porteño says it twice. With *lo* or *la* next to it, *le* turns into **se**: *¿La foto? Ya se la mandé a Juan.*

  After:

  > Even when you name the person, the **le** stays: **le dije a Juan que no**, **le di la llave a Sofi**, **les mandé las fotos a mis viejos**. English says it once; a porteño says it twice.
  > 
  > With *lo* or *la* next to it, *le* turns into **se**: *¿La foto? Ya se la mandé a Juan.*

- `863cc68c` te-llamo · Lo, la, le — where they go

  Before:

  > | him, or a masculine thing | **lo**, **los** | ¿El audio? **Lo** mando ahora. |
  > | her, or a feminine thing | **la**, **las** | ¿La tarjeta? **La** tengo. |
  > | to him, her, them | **le**, **les** | **Le** doy la llave. |
  > | me, you, us | **me**, **te**, **nos** | **Te** llamo mañana. |
  > Before a normal verb: *la tengo*. With a name you often say both: *lo llamo a Juan*, *les mando un audio a mis viejos*. Stuck to the end of a command, with no accent: **llamame**, **dame**, **mandame**, **prestame**. **Buscar** is *to look for*, and also *to pick someone up*: *te busco a las ocho* = *te paso a buscar*.

  After:

  > | him, or a masculine thing | **lo**, **los** | ¿El audio? **Lo** mando ahora. |
  > | her, or a feminine thing | **la**, **las** | ¿La tarjeta? **La** tengo. |
  > | to him, her, them | **le**, **les** | **Le** doy la llave. |
  > | me, you, us | **me**, **te**, **nos** | **Te** llamo mañana. |
  > 
  > Before a normal verb: *la tengo*. Stuck to the end of a command, with no accent: **llamame**, **dame**, **mandame**, **prestame**. With a name you often say both: *lo llamo a Juan*.
  > 
  > **Buscar** is *to look for*, and also *to pick someone up*: *te busco a las ocho* = *te paso a buscar*.

- `b1798c9a` te-llamo · Se lo, se la

  Before:

  > Two pronouns together go person first, thing second: **me lo** mandás, **te la** doy. And *le* or *les* in front of *lo, la, los, las* turns into **se**: *¿le mandás el audio?* → **¿se lo mandás?**; *le doy la llave* → **se la doy**.

  After:

  > Two pronouns together go person first, thing second: **me lo** mandás, **te la** doy.
  > 
  > And *le* or *les* in front of *lo, la, los, las* turns into **se**: *¿le mandás el audio?* → **¿se lo mandás?**; *le doy la llave* → **se la doy**.

- `be9c362e` una-pastilla · At the farmacia

  Before:

  > Half the time the pharmacist is your doctor: **estoy resfriado**, **tengo alergia**, **me duele el oído** — and you leave with **una curita** or **unas pastillas**. To ask: **¿Tenés algo para la alergia?** — **Necesito algo para la garganta.** A broken bone is **quebrado**: *tiene el brazo quebrado*. And when you hurt yourself: **me lastimé la rodilla**.

  After:

  > Tell the pharmacist what's wrong: **estoy resfriado**, **tengo alergia**, **me duele el oído**. You leave with **una curita** or **unas pastillas**.
  > 
  > To ask: **¿Tenés algo para la alergia?** — **Necesito algo para la garganta.**
  > 
  > A broken bone is **quebrado**: *tiene el brazo quebrado*. When you hurt yourself: **me lastimé la rodilla**.

- `38bd929f` vinieron-todos · Traje, quise

  Before:

  > Two more with their own stem and no accent: **traje, trajiste, trajo** (brought) and **quise, quisiste, quiso** (wanted, tried). **Quise** is about one moment: *I wanted to* (and tried). **No quise**: *I didn't want to* (and didn't). **Quise venir, pero no pude** — *I wanted to come but couldn't* — is the porteño apology for a missed asado.

  After:

  > Two more with their own stem and no accent: **traje, trajiste, trajo** (brought) and **quise, quisiste, quiso** (wanted, tried).
  > 
  > **Quise** is about one moment: *I wanted to* (and tried). **No quise**: *I didn't want to* (and didn't).
  > 
  > **Quise venir, pero no pude** — *I wanted to come but couldn't*.

- `faac1c21` volvi-a-buenos-aires · -er and -ir, every person

  Before:

  > | | nacer | vivir | volver |
  > | yo | nac**í** | viv**í** | volv**í** |
  > | vos | nac**iste** | viv**iste** | volv**iste** |
  > | él, ella | nac**ió** | viv**ió** | volv**ió** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | nac**ieron** | viv**ieron** | volv**ieron** |
  > 
  > *Ellos* ends in **-ieron**: *comieron, salieron, vivieron*. **¿Dónde naciste?** — **Nací en Rosario.** And **no entendí** is the past too.

  After:

  > | | nacer | vivir | volver |
  > | yo | nac**í** | viv**í** | volv**í** |
  > | vos | nac**iste** | viv**iste** | volv**iste** |
  > | él, ella | nac**ió** | viv**ió** | volv**ió** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | nac**ieron** | viv**ieron** | volv**ieron** |
  > 
  > *Ellos* ends in **-ieron**: *comieron, salieron, vivieron*.
  > 
  > **¿Dónde naciste?** — **Nací en Rosario.** And **no entendí** is the past too.

- `56f2eb46` bien-cocido · ¿Y la salsa?

  Before:

  > On the table: **chimichurri** — oil, garlic, parsley, chili — and **salsa criolla** — onion, tomato, pepper. Ketchup is a crime. The meat is cooked **a la leña** at the good places.

  After:

  > On the table: **chimichurri** — oil, garlic, parsley, chili — and **salsa criolla** — onion, tomato, pepper.
  > 
  > The meat is cooked **a la leña** at the good places.

- `d9075515` bien-cocido · Bien cocido

  Before:

  > A bife comes jugoso, a punto, or **bien cocido** — *well done*; **vuelta y vuelta** is seared on each side and almost raw inside. The bill has a line for the **cubierto** — *the cover charge*: the bread and the cutlery.

  After:

  > A bife comes jugoso, a punto, or **bien cocido** — *well done*; **vuelta y vuelta** is seared on each side and almost raw inside.
  > 
  > The bill has a line for the **cubierto** — *the cover charge*: the bread and the cutlery.

- `2910b1f9` con-gas-o-sin-gas · Propina

  Before:

  > The tip is **la propina**, usually 10 percent and in cash. The bill may also have a small charge per person, the *cubierto*: it is not the tip. **¿Compartimos?** — *shall we share?* — is how two people order one enormous *bife*.

  After:

  > The tip is **la propina**, usually 10 percent and in cash. The bill may also have a small charge per person, the *cubierto*: it is not the tip.
  > 
  > **¿Compartimos?** — *shall we share?* — is how two people order one *bife*.

- `55a36cb5` confirmo-en-el-grupo · El grupo

  Before:

  > Every plan lives in **el grupo**, the WhatsApp group. Someone asks **¿confirmás?** — *are you in?* — and you answer **confirmo**. A **salida** is an outing: a night out or a day trip with friends.

  After:

  > Every plan lives in **el grupo**, the WhatsApp group. Someone asks **¿confirmás?** — *are you in?* — and you answer **confirmo**.
  > 
  > A **salida** is an outing: a night out or a day trip with friends.

- `c3ebca78` contame · How a porteño tells it

  Before:

  > **Contame** opens it; **de repente** is the twist, **por suerte** the relief, **al final** the end. **Igual** is *anyway*, *still*: *estoy cansada, pero igual voy*. **O sea** is *I mean* or *so*. **¿Qué contás?** is just a greeting: *what's new?*

  After:

  > **Contame** opens a story; **de repente** is the twist, **por suerte** the relief, **al final** the end.
  > 
  > **Igual** is *anyway*, *still*: *estoy cansada, pero igual voy*. **O sea** is *I mean* or *so*.
  > 
  > **¿Qué contás?** is a greeting: *what's new?*

- `862e703e` cuando-era-chico · Era, tenía, vivía, iba

  Before:

  > | | ser | tener | vivir | ir |
  > | yo, él, ella | era | tenía | vivía | iba |
  > | vos | eras | tenías | vivías | ibas |
  > | nosotros | éramos | teníamos | vivíamos | íbamos |
  > This past describes: how things *were*, what you *had*, where you *lived*, what you *used to* do. *Yo* and *él* share one form — the story tells you which. **De chico** is *as a kid*: **De chico vivía en Rosario.** It changes with the person: **de chica** (a woman), **de chicos** (*we*).

  After:

  > | | ser | tener | vivir | ir |
  > | yo, él, ella | era | tenía | vivía | iba |
  > | vos | eras | tenías | vivías | ibas |
  > | nosotros | éramos | teníamos | vivíamos | íbamos |
  > 
  > This past describes: how things *were*, what you *had*, where you *lived*, what you *used to* do. *Yo* and *él* share one form — the story tells you which.
  > 
  > **De chico** is *as a kid*: **De chico vivía en Rosario.** It changes with the person: **de chica** (a woman), **de chicos** (*we*).

- `da883151` cuando-era-chico · Colegio

  Before:

  > Porteños mostly say **colegio** for school, primary or secondary: **¿A qué colegio ibas?** — *Iba a un colegio de mi barrio.* *Escuela* is understood and used too, mostly for state primary schools.

  After:

  > Porteños mostly say **colegio** for school, primary or secondary: **¿A qué colegio ibas?** — *Iba a un colegio de mi barrio.*
  > 
  > *Escuela* is understood and used too, mostly for state primary schools.

- `8f4c23b2` de-que-cuadro-sos · El superclásico

  Before:

  > **Boca** against **River** is **el superclásico**; any other big rivalry game is a **clásico**. Boca fans are called *bosteros* and River fans *gallinas*: they say it about themselves with pride, but from you it can be an insult. Which side you're on, the course leaves to you.

  After:

  > **Boca** against **River** is **el superclásico**; any other big rivalry game is a **clásico**.
  > 
  > Boca fans are called *bosteros* and River fans *gallinas*. They say it about themselves with pride, but from you it can be an insult.

- `bb6b163b` de-que-cuadro-sos · ¿De qué cuadro sos?

  Before:

  > The first question in a new friendship. A team is a **cuadro**, and you inherit it: *Soy de Independiente, el cuadro de mi abuelo.* Answer with **soy de** or **soy hincha de**. **Hinchar por** is for one game or the national team: *Hincho por la selección.*

  After:

  > A team is a **cuadro**, and you inherit it: *Soy de Independiente, el cuadro de mi abuelo.*
  > 
  > To say your team, use **soy de** or **soy hincha de**.
  > 
  > **Hinchar por** is for one game or the national team: *Hincho por la selección.*

- `92fc2db4` el-partido · Dos a uno

  Before:

  > Scores are said with **a**: **ganamos dos a uno**, *empatamos cero a cero*. To score is **hacer un gol**: *Juan hizo dos goles*. And the field — and the whole stadium — is **la cancha**.

  After:

  > Scores are said with **a**: **ganamos dos a uno**, *empatamos cero a cero*.
  > 
  > To score is **hacer un gol**: *Juan hizo dos goles*.
  > 
  > The field, and the whole stadium, is **la cancha**.

- `63f495cd` en-el-restaurante · Pedir

  Before:

  > **Pido** (I order), **pedís** (you order); in the past **pedí**, **pediste**, and — with an *i* — **pidió**. At the table: **¿Me traés la carta?** and, at the end, **la cuenta**. To get in: **Una mesa para dos, por favor.** And water is **con gas** or **sin gas**: *Para tomar, un agua sin gas.*

  After:

  > **Pido** (I order), **pedís** (you order); in the past **pedí**, **pediste**, and — with an *i* — **pidió**.
  > 
  > To get in: **Una mesa para dos, por favor.** At the table: **¿Me traés la carta?** and, at the end, **la cuenta**.
  > 
  > Water is **con gas** or **sin gas**: *Para tomar, un agua sin gas.*

- `a4adbfa9` en-la-primaria · La primaria

  Before:

  > School here is **la primaria** (six to twelve) and then **la secundaria**. In a state primary school everyone wears a white **guardapolvo** over their clothes, and the best part of the day is the **recreo**. In primaria the teacher is **la maestra** or **el maestro**; from secundaria on it is **el profesor**, **la profe**. Each year of primaria is a **grado**: *primer grado*.

  After:

  > School here is **la primaria** (six to twelve) and then **la secundaria**. Each year of primaria is a **grado**: *primer grado*.
  > 
  > In a state primary school everyone wears a white **guardapolvo** over their clothes. The best part of the day is the **recreo**.
  > 
  > In primaria the teacher is **la maestra** or **el maestro**; from secundaria on it is **el profesor**, **la profe**.

- `a54ef42c` en-la-primaria · Quería, podía

  Before:

  > Wanting and being able are states, so back then they take the imperfect: **De chico quería ser maestro** — *as a kid I wanted to be a teacher*; **no podía salir solo** — *I wasn't allowed out on my own*. **En esa época** — *back then* — sets the whole scene. At a counter, **quería un cortado** is just a polite *I'd like a cortado*. With a person, **querer a** is *to love*: *quería mucho a mi maestra*.

  After:

  > Wanting and being able are states, so back then they take the imperfect. **De chico quería ser maestro** — *as a kid I wanted to be a teacher*; **no podía salir solo** — *I wasn't allowed out on my own*.
  > 
  > **En esa época** — *back then* — sets the scene.
  > 
  > At a counter, **quería un cortado** is a polite *I'd like a cortado*. With a person, **querer a** is *to love*: *quería mucho a mi maestra*.

- `32fcb37a` en-lo-de-la-abuela · En lo de

  Before:

  > **En lo de mi abuela** — *at my grandma's (place)*; **vamos a lo de Juan** — *let's go to Juan's*. Sunday lunch there meant **ravioles con tuco**, and a **sobremesa** that ran until the *mate* came out.

  After:

  > **En lo de mi abuela** — *at my grandma's (place)*; **vamos a lo de Juan** — *let's go to Juan's*.
  > 
  > Sunday lunch there meant **ravioles con tuco**, and a **sobremesa** that ran until the *mate* came out.

- `a080f44e` en-lo-de-la-abuela · What the family always did

  Before:

  > Traditions told from back then are imperfect: **nos juntábamos**, **festejábamos**, **armábamos el arbolito** on the 8th of December. **Es costumbre…** — *it's the custom to…* — brings them into the present: *Es costumbre juntarnos en Navidad.*

  After:

  > Traditions told from back then are imperfect: **nos juntábamos**, **festejábamos**, **armábamos el arbolito** on the 8th of December.
  > 
  > **Es costumbre…** — *it's the custom to…* — brings them into the present: *Es costumbre juntarnos en Navidad.*

- `636b7821` estaba-lloviendo · Había, hacía

  Before:

  > **Había** is *there was, there were*; **hacía calor** is *it was hot*. **Estaba lloviendo** — *it was raining* — puts you in the middle of it; **llovía** says the same, and can also mean *it used to rain*. **Mientras** — *while* — joins two things going on at once.

  After:

  > **Había** is *there was, there were*; **hacía calor** is *it was hot*.
  > 
  > **Estaba lloviendo** — *it was raining* — puts you in the middle of it; **llovía** says the same, and can also mean *it used to rain*.
  > 
  > **Mientras** — *while* — joins two things going on at once.

- `df3230f6` jugaban-a-la-pelota · -aba, -ía, and ver

  Before:

  > | | jugar | salir | ver |
  > | yo, él, ella | jug**aba** | sal**ía** | ve**ía** |
  > | vos | jug**abas** | sal**ías** | ve**ías** |
  > | nosotros | jug**ábamos** | sal**íamos** | ve**íamos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | jug**aban** | sal**ían** | ve**ían** |
  > *Ver* keeps its *e*: **veía**. For TV, **miraba** and **veía** both work; to see a person, only *ver*: **veía a mi abuela**. **Me encantaba andar en bici.**

  After:

  > | | jugar | salir | ver |
  > | yo, él, ella | jug**aba** | sal**ía** | ve**ía** |
  > | vos | jug**abas** | sal**ías** | ve**ías** |
  > | nosotros | jug**ábamos** | sal**íamos** | ve**íamos** |
  > | ellos, ustedes | jug**aban** | sal**ían** | ve**ían** |
  > 
  > *Ver* keeps its *e*: **veía**.
  > 
  > For TV, **miraba** and **veía** both work; to see a person, only *ver*: **veía a mi abuela**. **Me encantaba andar en bici.**

- `aefc05cf` la-final · Ellos ganaron

  Before:

  > The other team is *they*: **ganaron**, **perdieron**, **empataron**. Yours is usually *we*, from the bar or the sofa: **¡salimos campeones!** About your own team you can say either: **ganamos** (we, the fans) or **ganaron** (they, the players).

  After:

  > The other team is *they*: **ganaron**, **perdieron**, **empataron**.
  > 
  > Yours is usually *we*: **¡salimos campeones!**
  > 
  > About your own team you can say either: **ganamos** (we, the fans) or **ganaron** (they, the players).

- `9521c790` la-parrilla · El asado

  Before:

  > An asado comes in order: first **chorizo** and **morcilla**, the **achuras**, then the cuts — **vacío**, tira de asado. A **choripán** is a chorizo in bread, the food of every stadium.

  After:

  > An asado comes in order: first **chorizo** and **morcilla**, the **achuras**, then the cuts — **vacío**, tira de asado.
  > 
  > A **choripán** is a chorizo in bread.

- `95e185c4` la-parrilla · ¿Cómo la querés?

  Before:

  > Meat comes **jugosa** — *rare* — **a punto** — *medium* — or well done. The **parrilla** is both the grill and the restaurant.

  After:

  > Meat comes **jugosa** — *rare* — **a punto** — *medium* — or well done.
  > 
  > The **parrilla** is both the grill and the restaurant.

- `320ccab5` la-parrillada · La parrillada

  Before:

  > For the table there's the **parrillada** — *a mixed grill* on its own little grill: chorizo, morcilla, **mollejas** — *sweetbreads: a soft gland, not bread* — **chinchulines** and a **tira de asado**. On your own: a **bife de chorizo** or an **entraña**. The classic start is a **provoleta para compartir**; the prized cuts are **ojo de bife** and **lomo**, with *papas fritas* or *ensalada* on the side.

  After:

  > For the table there's the **parrillada** — *a mixed grill* on its own little grill. It has chorizo, morcilla, **mollejas** — *sweetbreads: a soft gland, not bread* — **chinchulines** and a **tira de asado**.
  > 
  > On your own: a **bife de chorizo** or an **entraña**. The prized cuts are **ojo de bife** and **lomo**.
  > 
  > Start with a **provoleta para compartir**. On the side: *papas fritas* or *ensalada*.

- `3b3cb9f8` las-fiestas · Los ñoquis del 29

  Before:

  > On the 29th of every month many families eat **ñoquis** with a banknote under the plate, for luck. A **feriado** is a public holiday, and there are plenty of them.

  After:

  > On the 29th of every month many families eat **ñoquis** with a banknote under the plate, for luck.
  > 
  > A **feriado** is a public holiday, and there are plenty of them.

- `a3d8add9` las-fiestas · Christmas in summer

  Before:

  > **Las fiestas** are Christmas and New Year together: *¡Felices fiestas!* **Navidad** falls in the heat and is celebrated the night before, on **Nochebuena** — the 24th: an *asado* or a cold spread, and at midnight **sidra**, **pan dulce** and a **brindis** — *¡salud!* — before the fireworks and **Papá Noel**.

  After:

  > **Las fiestas** are Christmas and New Year: *¡Felices fiestas!*
  > 
  > **Navidad** falls in the heat and is celebrated the night before, on **Nochebuena** — the 24th.
  > 
  > Dinner is an *asado* or a cold spread. At midnight: **sidra**, **pan dulce** and a **brindis** — *¡salud!* — then fireworks and **Papá Noel**.

- `3c539a2a` llegue-tarde · ¿Por qué no me avisaste?

  Before:

  > *Avisar* in the past is plain -ar: **avisé**, **avisaste**, **avisó**. The text at eight-forty: **¿Dónde estás? Te esperaba a las ocho.** And the answer, always: **ya salgo**. The other classics: **estoy llegando** (still ten minutes away), **estoy en camino** — *I'm on my way* — and **llego en diez**.

  After:

  > *Avisar* in the past is plain -ar: **avisé**, **avisaste**, **avisó**.
  > 
  > The text at eight-forty: **¿Dónde estás? Te esperaba a las ocho.** The answer: **ya salgo**.
  > 
  > Other answers: **estoy llegando** (still ten minutes away); **estoy en camino** — *I'm on my way*; **llego en diez**.

- `46a3767c` llegue-tarde · Tardar

  Before:

  > **Tardar** is *to take (time)*: **¿cuánto tardás?** — *how long will you be?*; **el bondi tardó una hora**. Porteños are rarely **puntuales**, so *tipo ocho* already has the delay built in.

  After:

  > **Tardar** is *to take (time)*: **¿cuánto tardás?** — *how long will you be?*; **el bondi tardó una hora**.
  > 
  > Porteños are rarely **puntuales**, so *tipo ocho* already has the delay built in.

- `b20e2e46` me-dio-verguenza · Me dio miedo

  Before:

  > The other way to say how you felt is **me dio** + the feeling: **me dio miedo** — *it scared me*. You will hear it with many feelings: *me dio bronca* (it made me angry), *me dio vergüenza* (I was embarrassed), *me dio lástima* (I felt sorry).

  After:

  > The other way to say how you felt is **me dio** + the feeling: **me dio miedo** — *it scared me*.
  > 
  > It works with many feelings: *me dio bronca* (it made me angry), *me dio vergüenza* (I was embarrassed), *me dio lástima* (I felt sorry).

- `9ec733ad` me-encantaba · Me gustaba, me encantaba

  Before:

  > In the past, *gustar* and *encantar* work like any -ar verb: **me gustaba**, **me encantaba** — *I liked*, *I loved*. Several things take an **-n**: **me gustaban los dibujitos**. The same **-n** makes the *they* form of any verb: **los pibes jugaban a la pelota en la vereda**.

  After:

  > In the past, *gustar* and *encantar* work like any -ar verb: **me gustaba**, **me encantaba** — *I liked*, *I loved*.
  > 
  > Several things take an **-n**: **me gustaban los dibujitos**. The same **-n** makes the *they* form of any verb: **los pibes jugaban a la pelota en la vereda**.

- `d89da798` me-encantaba · Ver keeps its e

  Before:

  > Only three verbs break the pattern: *ser* (**era**), *ir* (**iba**) and *ver*, which keeps its *e*: **veía**, **veías**. Set **antes** against **ahora** and the imperfect does the rest: *antes veía dibujitos, ahora veo series.*

  After:

  > Only three verbs break the pattern: *ser* (**era**), *ir* (**iba**) and *ver*, which keeps its *e*: **veía**, **veías**.
  > 
  > Set **antes** against **ahora** and the imperfect does the rest: *antes veía dibujitos, ahora veo series.*

- `964d8d97` me-olvide · Me olvidé de…

  Before:

  > **Me olvidé de su cumple** — *I forgot his birthday*. Like *me enojé* it keeps its pronoun, and takes **de** before what you forgot. Left something behind? No *de*: **me olvidé las llaves en casa**. For the others: **¿te olvidaste las llaves?**, **se olvidó de llamar**, **nos olvidamos de reservar**. Same shape: **me aburrí**, **me calmé**, **me enamoré** — of Buenos Aires, of course.

  After:

  > **Me olvidé de su cumple** — *I forgot his birthday*. Like *me enojé* it keeps its pronoun, and takes **de** before what you forgot.
  > 
  > Left something behind? No *de*: **me olvidé las llaves en casa**.
  > 
  > For the others: **¿te olvidaste las llaves?**, **se olvidó de llamar**, **nos olvidamos de reservar**. Same shape: **me aburrí**, **me calmé**, **me enamoré**.

- `80ff4fcc` me-senti-re-mal · Estaba or me puse?

  Before:

  > **Estaba nervioso** is how you were; **me puse nervioso** is the moment it hit you. **Me sentí re mal** — *I felt awful* — is one moment too. For *us*: **nos pusimos contentos**; and **me puse colorado** is *I blushed*. About someone else: **¿cómo se sintió tu vieja?**

  After:

  > **Estaba nervioso** is how you were; **me puse nervioso** is the moment it hit you. **Me sentí re mal** — *I felt awful* — is one moment too.
  > 
  > For *us*: **nos pusimos contentos**. About someone else: **¿cómo se sintió tu vieja?**
  > 
  > And **me puse colorado** is *I blushed*.

- `eb59a72a` no-te-puedo-creer · ¡Menos mal!

  Before:

  > When the story ends well, the listener says **¡menos mal!** — *thank goodness!* — and **¡por fin!** when it took forever. **¿En serio?** keeps you talking: *really?*

  After:

  > When the story ends well, the listener says **¡menos mal!** — *thank goodness!* — and **¡por fin!** when it took forever.
  > 
  > **¿En serio?** keeps you talking: *really?*

- `0543a4d4` por-penales · Penales

  Before:

  > A **final** that ends in a tie goes to the **alargue** — *extra time* — and then to **penales**: the **arquero atajó** one and somebody **metió** the last. **Salir campeón** is *to win the title*. **¡Aguante!** shows love for your team, win or lose: *¡Aguante Argentina!*

  After:

  > A **final** that ends in a tie goes to the **alargue** — *extra time* — and then to **penales**. The **arquero atajó** one and somebody **metió** the last.
  > 
  > **Salir campeón** is *to win the title*.
  > 
  > **¡Aguante!** shows love for your team, win or lose: *¡Aguante Argentina!*

- `592b7207` practica-en-esa-epoca · The scene, then the event

  Before:

  > **Estaba en la ducha** — the scene; **cuando sonó el timbre** — the event. If you could add *was …-ing* or *used to* in English, it's the imperfect; if it happened once and ended, it's the preterite.

  After:

  > **Estaba en la ducha** — the scene; **cuando sonó el timbre** — the event.
  > 
  > If you could add *was …-ing* or *used to* in English, it's the imperfect. If it happened once and ended, it's the preterite.

- `b82e3b85` practica-en-esa-epoca · Everyone, back then

  Before:

  > **Era, eras, éramos, eran**; **tenía, tenías, teníamos, tenían**; **jugaba, jugabas, jugábamos, jugaban**. One set of endings for *-ar*, one for *-er* and *-ir*, and *yo* and *él* always share.

  After:

  > **Era, eras, éramos, eran**; **tenía, tenías, teníamos, tenían**; **jugaba, jugabas, jugábamos, jugaban**.
  > 
  > One set of endings for *-ar*, one for *-er* and *-ir*, and *yo* and *él* always share.

- `c6bd0210` sono-el-timbre · Era la una, eran las tres

  Before:

  > The time in a story is always imperfect: **era la una**, **eran las tres**. The scene keeps going — **no había luz**, **hacía un calor bárbaro** — until something happens: **empezó a llover** and **llegué mojado**.

  After:

  > The time in a story is always imperfect: **era la una**, **eran las tres**.
  > 
  > The scene keeps going — **no había luz**, **hacía un calor bárbaro** — until something happens: **empezó a llover** and **llegué mojado**.

- `bcc20295` soy-socio-del-club · Ser socio

  Before:

  > To go to **la cancha** you are usually a **socio** — *a member* — of the **club**, and you pay a monthly fee. The **popular** is the cheap standing section, where the **hinchada** sings its **cantitos** for ninety minutes under the **banderas**.

  After:

  > To go to **la cancha** you are usually a **socio** — *a member* — of the **club**, and you pay a monthly fee.
  > 
  > The **popular** is the cheap standing section. There the **hinchada** sings its **cantitos** under the **banderas**.

- `c273802d` soy-vegetariana · Salado, picante

  Before:

  > **Salado** means *salty*, and also *savory*, the opposite of sweet: *¿algo dulce o algo salado?* Argentine food is almost never **picante**, so *spicy* here is very mild. If you don't eat meat, say **soy vegetariano** or **soy vegetariana**.

  After:

  > **Salado** means *salty*, and also *savory*, the opposite of sweet: *¿algo dulce o algo salado?*
  > 
  > Argentine food is almost never **picante**, so *spicy* here is very mild.
  > 
  > If you don't eat meat, say **soy vegetariano** or **soy vegetariana**.

- `6e6d5a70` tipo-ocho · Avisame

  Before:

  > **Avisame** — *let me know*: **dale, avisame mañana**. **Te aviso** — *I'll let you know*. To meet up: **¿nos encontramos en la esquina?** — *should we meet on the corner?* — and **te espero** — *I'll wait for you*.

  After:

  > **Avisame** — *let me know*: **dale, avisame mañana**. **Te aviso** — *I'll let you know*.
  > 
  > To meet up: **¿nos encontramos en la esquina?** — *should we meet on the corner?*; **te espero** — *I'll wait for you*.

- `e4c89765` tipo-ocho · Tipo ocho

  Before:

  > **Tipo** before a time is *around*: **tipo ocho** — *around eight*, which in Buenos Aires means eight-fifteen at the earliest. **En punto** is *on the dot*, and rarer.

  After:

  > **Tipo** before a time is *around*: **tipo ocho** — *around eight*. In Buenos Aires that means eight-fifteen at the earliest.
  > 
  > **En punto** is *on the dot*, and rarer.

- `020df5a2` una-grande-de-muzza · Pide, pidieron

  Before:

  > The *e* of *pedir* turns into *i* in **pido**, **pide**, and in the past for *él* and *ellos*: **pidió**, **pidieron**. *Nosotros* keeps its *e* — and **pedimos** is both *we order* and *we ordered*: **anoche pedimos empanadas**.

  After:

  > The *e* of *pedir* turns into *i* in **pido**, **pide**, and in the past for *él* and *ellos*: **pidió**, **pidieron**.
  > 
  > *Nosotros* keeps its *e*, and **pedimos** is both *we order* and *we ordered*: **anoche pedimos empanadas**.

- `f63133a6` una-grande-de-muzza · La pizzería

  Before:

  > Porteño pizza is its own religion: **una grande de muzza**, eaten standing up by the **porción**, or **mitad y mitad** for the undecided. At a parrilla, start with a **provoleta** and a **choripán**. **Reservé para las diez** — dinner starts late.

  After:

  > For pizza: **una grande de muzza**, eaten standing up by the **porción**, or **mitad y mitad** for the undecided.
  > 
  > At a parrilla, start with a **provoleta** and a **choripán**.
  > 
  > **Reservé para las diez** — dinner starts late.

- `1a5ebe3a` a-la-mesa · Buen provecho

  Before:

  > **¡Buen provecho!** — *enjoy!* — is said when you serve food, or when you pass people who are eating, even coworkers or strangers. The answer is **gracias**. And when the host says **sírvanse** — *help yourselves* — everybody starts right away.

  After:

  > **¡Buen provecho!** — *enjoy!* — is said when you serve food, or when you pass people who are eating, even coworkers or strangers. The answer is **gracias**.
  > 
  > When the host says **sírvanse** — *help yourselves* — everybody starts right away.

- `a5ed425f` a-la-mesa · Ustedes at the table

  Before:

  > | one person (vos) | more than one (ustedes) |
  > | probá | **prueben** |
  > | agarrá | **agarren** |
  > | tomá | **tomen** |
  > | no te olvides | **no se olviden** |
  > 
  > Also **coman**, **hagan** lugar, **vayan**, **dejen**. **Vayan** + *-ando/-iendo* is *go ahead and start*: **vayan comiendo** — *start eating*. -ar verbs **-en**, the rest **-an**. The stem is the *yo* stem: *pruebo* → **prueben**, *hago* → **hagan**, *vengo* → **vengan**. **Dejen de** + verb is *stop*: **dejen de hablar** — *stop talking*.

  After:

  > | one person (vos) | more than one (ustedes) |
  > | probá | **prueben** |
  > | agarrá | **agarren** |
  > | tomá | **tomen** |
  > | no te olvides | **no se olviden** |
  > 
  > -ar verbs **-en**, the rest **-an**. The stem is the *yo* stem: *pruebo* → **prueben**, *hago* → **hagan**, *vengo* → **vengan**. Also **coman**, **hagan** lugar, **vayan**, **dejen**.
  > 
  > **Vayan** + *-ando/-iendo* is *go ahead and start*: **vayan comiendo** — *start eating*. **Dejen de** + verb is *stop*: **dejen de hablar** — *stop talking*.

- `22e7eebe` acampamos-en-el-sur · Acampamos, recorrieron

  Before:

  > Travel stories run on the preterite: **acampamos** — *we camped* — **hicimos dedo** — *we hitchhiked* — **¿recorriste el norte?**, **mis viejos recorrieron la costa**. Distances are in **kilómetros**, and in the south there are always more than you thought.

  After:

  > Travel stories use the preterite: **acampamos** — *we camped*; **hicimos dedo** — *we hitchhiked*. Also **¿recorriste el norte?**, **mis viejos recorrieron la costa**.
  > 
  > Distances are in **kilómetros**.

- `3d839f10` cien-gramos-de-jamon · Cien gramos, un cuarto

  Before:

  > At the **fiambrería** you buy by weight: **cien gramos de jamón**, cut in **fetas** — *slices*. The classic order is **un cuarto** — 250 grams: *un cuarto de jamón cocido*. **Jamón cocido** is plain ham; **jamón crudo** is the cured one.

  After:

  > At the **fiambrería** you buy by weight: **cien gramos de jamón**, cut in **fetas** — *slices*.
  > 
  > The usual order is **un cuarto** — 250 grams: *un cuarto de jamón cocido*.
  > 
  > **Jamón cocido** is plain ham; **jamón crudo** is the cured one.

- `bbf8899a` cien-gramos-de-jamon · ¿Algo más?

  Before:

  > The vendor always asks **¿algo más?** — *anything else?* You answer **nada más, gracias**, or keep going: **y un kilo de naranjas**. To ask a price, **¿cuánto sale?** is the everyday way — same as *¿cuánto cuesta?* For things sold by weight you already know **¿a cuánto está?**

  After:

  > The vendor always asks **¿algo más?** — *anything else?* You answer **nada más, gracias**, or keep going: **y un kilo de naranjas**.
  > 
  > To ask a price, **¿cuánto sale?** is the everyday way — same as *¿cuánto cuesta?* For things sold by weight you already know **¿a cuánto está?**

- `9b2c23e3` donde-queda · Capital y conurbano

  Before:

  > The city is **la Capital** — often with no article: *vivo en Capital*. The ring of towns around it is **el conurbano**. Palermo, **San Telmo** and **La Boca** are barrios of the Capital: *Palermo está lleno de bares*, *La Boca es re turística*. The first question to any porteño: **¿en qué barrio vivís?**

  After:

  > The city is **la Capital** — often with no article: *vivo en Capital*. The ring of towns around it is **el conurbano**.
  > 
  > Palermo, **San Telmo** and **La Boca** are barrios of the Capital: *Palermo está lleno de bares*, *La Boca es re turística*.
  > 
  > Ask any porteño: **¿en qué barrio vivís?**

- `f7ca6bf1` donde-queda · Queda, me parece

  Before:

  > Remember **queda** for where a place *is*: **¿dónde queda Palermo?** New here is **me parece** — *I think, it seems to me*: *me parece re caro*. To ask for an opinion: **¿qué te parece el depto?** — *what do you think of the apartment?*

  After:

  > Remember **queda** for where a place *is*: **¿dónde queda Palermo?**
  > 
  > New here is **me parece** — *I think, it seems to me*: *me parece re caro*.
  > 
  > To ask for an opinion: **¿qué te parece el depto?** — *what do you think of the apartment?*

- `dbad321f` el-contrato-de-alquiler · Renting a place

  Before:

  > You rent through an **inmobiliaria** or straight from the **dueño**. Every month you pay the **alquiler** plus the **expensas** — *the building fees*. To sign the **contrato** you leave a **depósito** and show a **garantía**: someone who answers for you if you don't pay.

  After:

  > You rent through an **inmobiliaria** or straight from the **dueño**.
  > 
  > Every month you pay the **alquiler** plus the **expensas** — *the building fees*.
  > 
  > To sign the **contrato** you leave a **depósito** and show a **garantía**: someone who answers for you if you don't pay.

- `c7afa899` el-depto-nuevo · Before and after the move

  Before:

  > The old place is imperfect, the move is preterite: **antes alquilaba en el centro** — how it was; **me mudé a Palermo** — what happened. **Extrañaba** is missing something over a period of time: *los primeros meses extrañaba mi barrio*.

  After:

  > The old place is imperfect, the move is preterite: **antes alquilaba en el centro** — how it was; **me mudé a Palermo** — what happened.
  > 
  > **Extrañaba** is missing something over a period of time: *los primeros meses extrañaba mi barrio*.

- `69d02752` el-tramite · Vencido

  Before:

  > A document that has run out is **vencido**: *tengo el pasaporte vencido*. What comes next: **renovar** — *renew it*; **completar el formulario** — *fill in the form*; **hacer la cola** (or **la fila**) — *stand in line*; **firmar** — *sign*.

  After:

  > A document that has run out is **vencido**: *tengo el pasaporte vencido*.
  > 
  > What comes next: **renovar** — *renew it*; **completar el formulario** — *fill in the form*; **hacer la cola** (or **la fila**) — *stand in line*; **firmar** — *sign*.

- `96bfd871` el-tramite · Sacar turno

  Before:

  > For almost anything official you first **sacás turno** — you book an appointment online — and you bring the **DNI**, the national ID card, without which nothing happens. As a foreigner, you use your passport until you get your own DNI.

  After:

  > For almost anything official you first **sacás turno**: you book an appointment online.
  > 
  > You bring the **DNI**, the national ID card. As a foreigner, you use your passport until you get your own DNI.

- `632ffd1b` en-la-verduleria · ¿A cuánto está?

  Before:

  > At the verdulería the price moves, so it's **¿a cuánto está el kilo?** — *how much is it a kilo today?* You buy **un kilo**, **medio kilo** or **una docena**.

  After:

  > At the verdulería the price moves, so it's **¿a cuánto está el kilo?** — *how much is it a kilo today?*
  > 
  > You buy **un kilo**, **medio kilo** or **una docena**.

- `a7acb74c` en-la-verduleria · La papa, la cebolla

  Before:

  > For produce porteños often use the singular for the whole product: **la papa está cara** — *potatoes are expensive*; **un kilo de cebolla** — *a kilo of onions*. And fruit that **está verde** is *not ripe* yet (*es verde* is the color).

  After:

  > For produce porteños often use the singular for the whole product: **la papa está cara** — *potatoes are expensive*; **un kilo de cebolla** — *a kilo of onions*.
  > 
  > Fruit that **está verde** is *not ripe* yet (*es verde* is the color).

- `48fa40c1` esta-calentito · -ito on everything

  Before:

  > Nouns: **una cosita**, **un pedacito**, **un besito**. Adjectives: **calentito** — *nice and warm*; **cortito**; **igualito a su papá**; **solito** — *all by himself* (to an adult it sounds tender or teasing, so keep it for friends). Adverbs: **tempranito**, **rapidito**. It agrees like any adjective: **una sopa calentita**.

  After:

  > Nouns: **una cosita**, **un pedacito**, **un besito**. Adverbs: **tempranito**, **rapidito**.
  > 
  > Adjectives: **calentito** — *nice and warm*; **cortito**; **igualito a su papá**. They agree like any adjective: **una sopa calentita**.
  > 
  > Said to an adult, **solito** — *all by himself* — sounds tender or teasing, so keep it for friends.

- `fbd2133a` estan-maduras · Palta, frutilla

  Before:

  > Argentina has its own names: **palta** is avocado, **frutilla** is strawberry. Before you buy, ask **¿están maduras?** — *are they ripe?* The opposite of *madura* is **verde**: **están verdes** — *they are not ripe yet*. (*Es verde* is the color.)

  After:

  > Argentina has its own names: **palta** is avocado, **frutilla** is strawberry.
  > 
  > Before you buy, ask **¿están maduras?** — *are they ripe?* The opposite of *madura* is **verde**: **están verdes** — *they are not ripe yet*. (*Es verde* is the color.)

- `25238004` hace-fresquito · -cito

  Before:

  > Words ending in *-r*, *-n*, *-e* take **-cito**: **calor → calorcito**, **café → cafecito**. Short *sol* does too: **solcito**. Words in *-o* just swap it for **-ito**, and a *c* turns *qu*: **fresco → fresquito**; a *z* turns *c*: **pedazo → pedacito**. Porteños love diminutives for nice weather: **hace fresquito**, **qué lindo calorcito**.

  After:

  > Words ending in *-r*, *-n*, *-e* take **-cito**: **calor → calorcito**, **café → cafecito**. Short *sol* does too: **solcito**.
  > 
  > Words in *-o* just swap it for **-ito**. A *c* turns *qu*: **fresco → fresquito**; a *z* turns *c*: **pedazo → pedacito**.
  > 
  > Porteños love diminutives for nice weather: **hace fresquito**, **qué lindo calorcito**.

- `1d2ec980` hay-que · No hace falta

  Before:

  > **No hace falta** — *there's no need*. **Conviene** is the advice: **conviene ir temprano** — *it's best to go early*.

  After:

  > **No hace falta** — *there's no need*.
  > 
  > **Conviene** is the advice: **conviene ir temprano** — *it's best to go early*.

- `84966d7c` hay-que-sacar-turno · Tenés que, tiene que, tienen que

  Before:

  > **Tener que** changes with the person: **tenés que firmar** — *you have to sign*; **tiene que venir ella** — *she has to come*; **tienen que esperar** — *they have to wait*. **Hay que** stays the same for everyone: **hay que hacer la fila**.

  After:

  > **Tener que** changes with the person: **tenés que firmar** — *you have to sign*; **tiene que venir ella** — *she has to come*; **tienen que esperar** — *they have to wait*.
  > 
  > **Hay que** stays the same for everyone: **hay que hacer la fila**.

- `a1d65681` hay-que-sacar-turno · Sacar turno

  Before:

  > Nothing happens in Buenos Aires without **sacar turno** — *booking an appointment*, almost always **por internet**. Then they **piden** things: **te piden una fotocopia**, **completá esto**.

  After:

  > Nothing happens in Buenos Aires without **sacar turno** — *booking an appointment*, almost always **por internet**.
  > 
  > Then they **piden** things: **te piden una fotocopia**, **completá esto**.

- `d621bc2e` hay-que-sacar-turno · Hace falta

  Before:

  > Drop the *no* and **hace falta** says what's needed: **hace falta una fotocopia**. **¿Es obligatorio?** — *is it required?* — is a handy question at any counter.

  After:

  > Drop the *no* and **hace falta** says what's needed: **hace falta una fotocopia**.
  > 
  > **¿Es obligatorio?** — *is it required?* — is a handy question at any counter.

- `b4d85210` la-cordillera · La cordillera

  Before:

  > The **cordillera** — *the Andes range* — runs down the whole west: **lagos** and **nieve** in the south, **bodegas** — *wineries* — in Mendoza, red **valles** in Jujuy. A **mochilero** does it all with a backpack and a **carpa** — *a tent*, down to Ushuaia.

  After:

  > The **cordillera** — *the Andes range* — runs down the whole west.
  > 
  > It has **lagos** and **nieve** in the south, **bodegas** — *wineries* — in Mendoza, red **valles** in Jujuy.
  > 
  > A **mochilero** does it all with a backpack and a **carpa** — *a tent*.

- `0a7f761b` la-precaria · ¿Cuánto demora?

  Before:

  > For paperwork, **demorar** is how long something takes: **¿cuánto demora la residencia?** The honest answer is *depende*. For a person or a bus porteños say **tardar**: *Lucía tardó media hora*, *el bondi tardó un montón*. You **presentás** the papers, and when they finally **atienden** you, it's over in five minutes.

  After:

  > For paperwork, **demorar** is how long something takes: **¿cuánto demora la residencia?**
  > 
  > For a person or a bus porteños say **tardar**: *Lucía tardó media hora*, *el bondi tardó un montón*.
  > 
  > You **presentás** the papers, and when they **atienden** you, it's over in five minutes.

- `aed54cfb` la-precaria · What Migraciones asks for

  Before:

  > For the **residencia** you need your **antecedentes penales** — *criminal record certificate* — with the **apostilla**, the stamp that makes a foreign paper valid here, and a **certificado de domicilio** — *proof of address*. While you wait they give you the **precaria**, a temporary permit you can already work and travel with.

  After:

  > For the **residencia** you need your **antecedentes penales** — *criminal record certificate* — with the **apostilla**, the stamp that makes a foreign paper valid here.
  > 
  > You also need a **certificado de domicilio** — *proof of address*.
  > 
  > While you wait they give you the **precaria**, a temporary permit you can work and travel with.

- `40e8fd4a` la-semana-que-viene · Tengo ganas, pienso ir

  Before:

  > **Tengo ganas de ir a la playa** — *I feel like going to the beach*. **Pienso ir** — *I'm planning to go*. Careful: **pensar** + a verb is *to plan to*, but **pensar que** is *to think that*: *pienso que tenés razón*.

  After:

  > **Tengo ganas de ir a la playa** — *I feel like going to the beach*. **Pienso ir** — *I'm planning to go*.
  > 
  > Careful: **pensar** + a verb is *to plan to*, but **pensar que** is *to think that*: *pienso que tenés razón*.

- `a5a666cc` la-semana-que-viene · Voy a…

  Before:

  > The future is **ir a** + verb: **voy a laburar**, **vamos a comer**. For a fixed plan the plain present works too: **viajamos el viernes**. *Viajamos* is also *we traveled*: the time word tells you which. **Que viene** after a time word is *next*: **la semana que viene**, **el año que viene**, **el finde que viene**.

  After:

  > The future is **ir a** + verb: **voy a laburar**, **vamos a comer**.
  > 
  > For a fixed plan the plain present works too: **viajamos el viernes**. *Viajamos* is also *we traveled*: the time word tells you which.
  > 
  > **Que viene** after a time word is *next*: **la semana que viene**, **el año que viene**, **el finde que viene**.

- `086d52a0` las-figuritas · Every porteño childhood

  Before:

  > The **calesita** in the plaza, **golosinas** from the kiosco, a **barrilete** on a windy day, and the **álbum** of **figuritas** you filled for every **Mundial** — **lo llenábamos entre todos** — *we used to fill it together*.

  After:

  > The **calesita** in the plaza, **golosinas** from the kiosco, a **barrilete** on a windy day.
  > 
  > And the **álbum** of **figuritas** you filled for every **Mundial**: **lo llenábamos entre todos** — *we used to fill it together*.

- `d68950b1` las-figuritas · Acordarse de

  Before:

  > What you remember takes **de**: **mi abuela se acuerda de todo**; **nos acordamos de esa época**. Before **que** or **cuando**, people usually drop the **de**: *me acuerdo que íbamos a la playa*. **No me acordaba** — *I'd forgotten* — is imperfect: the not-remembering went on until someone brought it up.

  After:

  > What you remember takes **de**: **mi abuela se acuerda de todo**; **nos acordamos de esa época**.
  > 
  > Before **que** or **cuando**, people usually drop the **de**: *me acuerdo que íbamos a la playa*.
  > 
  > **No me acordaba** — *I'd forgotten* — is imperfect: the not-remembering went on until someone brought it up.

- `795eda4c` me-das-una-mano · More favors, same question

  Before:

  > Still a plain question in the present with **me** in front: **¿me acompañás?** — *will you come with me?*; **¿me llevás?** — *can you give me a ride?*; **¿me regás las plantas?** — *can you water my plants?* Asking for two of you? Swap *me* for **nos**: **¿nos llevás?** — *can you take us?*

  After:

  > Still a plain question in the present with **me** in front: **¿me acompañás?** — *will you come with me?*; **¿me llevás?** — *can you give me a ride?*; **¿me regás las plantas?** — *can you water my plants?*
  > 
  > Asking for two of you? Swap *me* for **nos**: **¿nos llevás?** — *can you take us?*

- `cc550005` me-das-una-mano · Un toque, de paso

  Before:

  > **Un toque** is *a sec, a bit*: **esperame un toque**. **De paso** is *while you're at it*: **de paso, ¿me comprás pan?**

  After:

  > **Un toque** is *a sec, a bit*: **esperame un toque**.
  > 
  > **De paso** is *while you're at it*: **de paso, ¿me comprás pan?**

- `c5ccfebf` me-haces-un-favor · Asking with the present

  Before:

  > A porteño asks with a plain question in the present: **¿me pasás la sal?**, **¿me ayudás?** — the *can you* is in the tone. **¿Podrías…?** is the extra-polite version. **¿Me pasás…?** and **¿me alcanzás…?** mean the same: *can you pass me…?*

  After:

  > A porteño asks with a plain question in the present: **¿me pasás la sal?**, **¿me ayudás?** — the *can you* is in the tone.
  > 
  > **¿Podrías…?** is the extra-polite version.
  > 
  > **¿Me pasás…?** and **¿me alcanzás…?** mean the same: *can you pass me…?*

- `72734c3b` me-mude · Me mudé

  Before:

  > **Me mudé** — *I moved (house)*; **nos mudamos** — *we moved*. It always carries *me/te/se/nos*: **te mudaste**, **se mudó**. **Mudarse con** is *to move in with*. The **mudanza** is the move itself, and always a *quilombo* (a mess).

  After:

  > **Me mudé** — *I moved (house)*; **nos mudamos** — *we moved*. It always carries *me/te/se/nos*: **te mudaste**, **se mudó**.
  > 
  > **Mudarse con** is *to move in with*.
  > 
  > The **mudanza** is the move itself.

- `c9b7d55e` me-mude · Extrañar

  Before:

  > **Extraño a mi vieja** — *I miss my mom*. A person takes **a**; a thing doesn't: **extraño el mate**. Not *echar de menos*: that is Spain's, and here nobody says it.

  After:

  > **Extraño a mi vieja** — *I miss my mom*. A person takes **a**; a thing doesn't: **extraño el mate**.
  > 
  > Not *echar de menos*: that is Spain's, and here nobody says it.

- `db277486` me-regas-las-plantas · Prendé, apagá

  Before:

  > Between friends a bare vos command is polite enough: **prendé la luz**, **apagá la tele**. With a pronoun it joins the end, like *ayudame*: **acompañame**, **llevame**.

  After:

  > Between friends a bare vos command is polite enough: **prendé la luz**, **apagá la tele**.
  > 
  > With a pronoun it joins the end, like *ayudame*: **acompañame**, **llevame**.

- `6b3cc89e` migraciones · Una consulta

  Before:

  > At any **ventanilla**, open with **una consulta** — *a quick question* — and ask for the **requisitos**, *what they need*. They'll say **el original y una copia**, and they mean both.

  After:

  > At any **ventanilla**, open with **una consulta** — *a quick question*. Then ask for the **requisitos**, *what they need*.
  > 
  > They'll say **el original y una copia**, and they mean both.

- `01a808b3` pasen-pasen · Están en su casa

  Before:

  > A porteño host says **pasen, pasen** at the door, **pónganse cómodos**, and **no se preocupen** when you apologize for being late. **Están en su casa** — *make yourselves at home*.

  After:

  > A porteño host says **pasen, pasen** at the door, **pónganse cómodos**, and **no se preocupen** when you apologize for being late.
  > 
  > **Están en su casa** — *make yourselves at home*.

- `f534bcfd` pasen-pasen · Commands for ustedes

  Before:

  > | one person (vos) | more than one (ustedes) |
  > | pasá | **pasen** |
  > | esperá | **esperen** |
  > | vení | **vengan** |
  > | traé | **traigan** |
  > | sentate | **siéntense** |
  > | no te preocupes | **no se preocupen** |
  > 
  > -ar verbs end in **-en**, the rest in **-an**, built on the *yo* form: *vengo* → **vengan**, *traigo* → **traigan**. The pronoun is **se**, stuck on the end — and then the accent appears: **siéntense, quédense**. After *no* it goes in front: **no se preocupen**.

  After:

  > | one person (vos) | more than one (ustedes) |
  > | pasá | **pasen** |
  > | esperá | **esperen** |
  > | vení | **vengan** |
  > | traé | **traigan** |
  > | sentate | **siéntense** |
  > | no te preocupes | **no se preocupen** |
  > 
  > -ar verbs end in **-en**, the rest in **-an**, built on the *yo* form: *vengo* → **vengan**, *traigo* → **traigan**.
  > 
  > The pronoun is **se**, stuck on the end — and then the accent appears: **siéntense, quédense**. After *no* it goes in front: **no se preocupen**.

- `dff327fb` practica-antes-y-ahora · Me sentí, me olvidé

  Before:

  > The moment a feeling hit, or a thing slipped your mind, is preterite with its pronoun: **me sentí re mal**, **me olvidé de avisarte** (a thing you left behind takes no *de*: **me olvidé las llaves**), **nos pusimos contentos**.

  After:

  > The moment a feeling hit, or a thing slipped your mind, is preterite with its pronoun: **me sentí re mal**, **nos pusimos contentos**, **me olvidé de avisarte**.
  > 
  > A thing you left behind takes no *de*: **me olvidé las llaves**.

- `4492bbdb` practica-la-feria · Me, nos

  Before:

  > **¿Me acompañás?** — the favor is for me; **¿nos sacás una foto?** — the favor is for us. You ask with a plain present-tense question: no *can you* needed. **¿Me podrías…?** — *could you…?* — is the more careful version.

  After:

  > **¿Me acompañás?** — the favor is for me; **¿nos sacás una foto?** — the favor is for us. You ask with a plain present-tense question: no *can you* needed.
  > 
  > **¿Me podrías…?** — *could you…?* — is the more careful version.

- `1cc4dae8` recorrer-el-pais · Recorrer

  Before:

  > **Recorrer** is *to travel around, to go all over*: **recorrimos la Patagonia**. The road is the **ruta** — *la ruta 40* runs the length of the country.

  After:

  > **Recorrer** is *to travel around, to go all over*: **recorrimos la Patagonia**.
  > 
  > The road is the **ruta** — *la ruta 40* runs the length of the country.

- `af4c1031` se-aceptan-tarjetas · On the door

  Before:

  > On the shop door: **horario de atención** — *opening hours*; **abierto / cerrado**; **solo efectivo** — *cash only*; **no funciona** — *out of order*; **liquidación** — *sale*. In a building: **no se permiten mascotas**, **se prohíbe hacer ruido**.

  After:

  > On the shop door: **horario de atención** — *opening hours*; **abierto / cerrado**; **solo efectivo** — *cash only*; **no funciona** — *out of order*; **liquidación** — *sale*.
  > 
  > In a building: **no se permiten mascotas**, **se prohíbe hacer ruido**.

- `e9b64ef7` se-aceptan-tarjetas · Se + one thing, se + several

  Before:

  > | one thing | more than one |
  > | **Se busca** perro. | **Se aceptan** tarjetas. |
  > | **Se permite** fumar en la terraza. | No **se permiten** mascotas. |
  > | **Se prohíbe** hacer ruido. | **Se hacen** envíos. |
  > 
  > The verb agrees with the thing: one → *se busca*, several → *se arreglan bicis*. Most real signs just say **Prohibido fumar**; **se prohíbe** is the formal version.

  After:

  > | one thing | more than one |
  > | **Se busca** perro. | **Se aceptan** tarjetas. |
  > | **Se permite** fumar en la terraza. | No **se permiten** mascotas. |
  > | **Se prohíbe** hacer ruido. | **Se hacen** envíos. |
  > 
  > The verb agrees with the thing: one → *se busca*, several → *se arreglan bicis*.
  > 
  > Most real signs just say **Prohibido fumar**; **se prohíbe** is the formal version.

- `a13868ae` se-alquila · Se + verb on a sign

  Before:
