  > On a sign, **se** + verb says what is sold, rented or needed, with nobody named: **se vende** auto, **se alquila** depto, **se necesita** mozo. For more than one thing, add **n**: **se venden** bicis. The same **se** asks about rules: **¿se puede fumar acá?** — *can you smoke here?*

  After:

  > On a sign, **se** + verb says what is sold, rented or needed, with nobody named: **se vende** auto, **se alquila** depto, **se necesita** mozo.
  > 
  > For more than one thing, add **n**: **se venden** bicis.
  > 
  > The same **se** asks about rules: **¿se puede fumar acá?** — *can you smoke here?*

- `4be9d9ea` se-prohibe · Out loud: ¿se puede?

  Before:

  > **Se permite** and **se prohíbe** are sign language. Out loud people say **se puede** / **no se puede**: *acá no se puede estacionar*; *¿se puede pagar con tarjeta?* And to ask a shop: **¿hacen envíos?** — *do you deliver?*

  After:

  > **Se permite** and **se prohíbe** are sign language. Out loud people say **se puede** / **no se puede**: *acá no se puede estacionar*; *¿se puede pagar con tarjeta?*
  > 
  > To ask a shop: **¿hacen envíos?** — *do you deliver?*

- `ce04f326` te-acordas · Me acordé, acordate

  Before:

  > You know **me acuerdo** — *I remember*. The moment something comes back is the past: **¡ah, ahora me acordé!**; **¿te acordaste del pan?** — *did you remember the bread?* To remind someone, use the command: **acordate de las llaves** — *remember the keys*.

  After:

  > You know **me acuerdo** — *I remember*. The moment something comes back is the past: **¡ah, ahora me acordé!**; **¿te acordaste del pan?** — *did you remember the bread?*
  > 
  > To remind someone, use the command: **acordate de las llaves** — *remember the keys*.

- `f03770e9` te-acordas · Antes era distinto

  Before:

  > **Antes era distinto** — *it used to be different*. **¡Qué épocas!** — *those were the days!* — closes it. With *ser* it is a different kind of thing: **el barrio era distinto**. With *estar* it has changed: **estás distinta** — *you look different*.

  After:

  > **Antes era distinto** — *it used to be different*. **¡Qué épocas!** — *those were the days!*
  > 
  > With *ser* it is a different kind of thing: **el barrio era distinto**. With *estar* it has changed: **estás distinta** — *you look different*.

- `492ceddb` un-ratito · -ito, -ita

  Before:

  > **-ito** makes things small, and requests smaller: **un ratito** — *a little while*; **un cafecito**; **un poquito**. It sounds kind rather than childish. It softens bad news and favors too: **tengo un problemita**, **una preguntita**.

  After:

  > **-ito** makes things small, and requests smaller: **un ratito** — *a little while*; **un cafecito**; **un poquito**. It sounds kind rather than childish.
  > 
  > It softens bad news and favors too: **tengo un problemita**, **una preguntita**.

- `9c22a7d9` un-ratito · Cerquita, despacito

  Before:

  > It works on adverbs too: **cerquita** — *close by*; **despacito** — *nice and slow*. **Chiquito** — *small, little*. Careful: **hablá más despacito** can mean *slower* or *quieter* — the situation tells you.

  After:

  > It works on adverbs too: **cerquita** — *close by*; **despacito** — *nice and slow*.
  > 
  > **Chiquito** — *small, little*.
  > 
  > Careful: **hablá más despacito** can mean *slower* or *quieter* — the situation tells you.

- `ef2f7720` zona-norte · Norte y sur

  Before:

  > **Zona norte** — the suburbs north of the General Paz, like San Isidro and Tigre — has a name for money and **árboles**; **zona sur** — Avellaneda, Lanús, Quilmes — is, in general, cheaper. Inside the Capital, Recoleta and Belgrano are the leafy north; Caballito, Almagro and Villa Crespo sit in the middle.

  After:

  > **Zona norte** is the suburbs north of the General Paz, like San Isidro and Tigre. It is known for money and **árboles**.
  > 
  > The **zona sur** — Avellaneda, Lanús, Quilmes — is generally cheaper.
  > 
  > Inside the Capital, Recoleta and Belgrano are the leafy north; Caballito, Almagro and Villa Crespo sit in the middle.

- `6f623f96` buena-onda · Pesado

  Before:

  > **Qué pesado** — *what a pain* — for someone who won't let it go. **Divertido** is *fun*, **tímido** *shy*. For a woman: **pesada**, **divertida**, **tímida**. With **estar** it is just for now: *Nico está pesado hoy* — *Nico's being annoying today*.

  After:

  > **Qué pesado** — *what a pain* — for someone who won't let it go. **Divertido** is *fun*, **tímido** *shy*.
  > 
  > For a woman: **pesada**, **divertida**, **tímida**.
  > 
  > With **estar** it is just for now: *Nico está pesado hoy* — *Nico's being annoying today*.

- `ebd8e8e5` buena-onda · Buena onda

  Before:

  > **Buena onda** is the porteño compliment for a person: easy, warm, *nice*. **Mala onda** is the opposite. **Buena onda** and **mala onda** never change: *ella es buena onda*. **Simpático** is narrower — *friendly*, pleasant to talk to — and does change: **simpática**.

  After:

  > **Buena onda** is the porteño compliment for a person: easy, warm, *nice*. **Mala onda** is the opposite.
  > 
  > **Buena onda** and **mala onda** never change: *ella es buena onda*.
  > 
  > **Simpático** is narrower — *friendly*, pleasant to talk to — and does change: **simpática**.

- `6f3c143b` capaz-a-lo-mejor · Capaz, a lo mejor

  Before:

  > **Capaz**, **a lo mejor** and **quizás** all mean *maybe*; **seguro que** is *I bet*; **supongo que** is *I guess*; **probablemente** is *probably*. All take the plain present: **capaz está en la plaza**, **seguro que es Fede**. In Buenos Aires you will hear **capaz** much more than **quizás**.

  After:

  > **Capaz**, **a lo mejor** and **quizás** all mean *maybe*. Porteños say **capaz** much more than **quizás**.
  > 
  > Also: **seguro que** is *I bet*; **supongo que** is *I guess*; **probablemente** is *probably*.
  > 
  > All take the plain present: **capaz está en la plaza**, **seguro que es Fede**.

- `7b5302fb` capaz-a-lo-mejor · Debe estar

  Before:

  > To state a guess, porteños mostly say **debe** + infinitive: **debe estar en el laburo** — *he must be at work*; **debe tener cuarenta**; **deben ser las ocho**; to vos, **debés estar cansado** — *you must be tired*. The future is more for the question: **¿dónde estará?**, **¿qué hora será?**

  After:

  > To guess, porteños mostly say **debe** + infinitive.
  > 
  > So: **debe estar en el laburo** — *he must be at work*; **debe tener cuarenta**; **deben ser las ocho**; to vos, **debés estar cansado** — *you must be tired*.
  > 
  > The future is more for the question: **¿dónde estará?**, **¿qué hora será?**

- `0f049b73` como-se-siente · Se siente

  Before:

  > For someone else it's **se siente**: **mi abuela no se siente bien**. Where it hurts is **dolor de** — **tengo dolor de cabeza** — *I've got a headache*, the same as **me duele la cabeza**.

  After:

  > For someone else it's **se siente**: **mi abuela no se siente bien**.
  > 
  > Where it hurts is **dolor de**: **tengo dolor de cabeza** — *I've got a headache*. Same as **me duele la cabeza**.

- `c2f0319f` como-se-siente · Reposo

  Before:

  > The doctor's favorite order: **tenés que hacer reposo** — *you need bed rest*. And a **jarabe** for the cough. A plain **resfrío** — *a cold* — needs no doctor, just a **remedio** from the pharmacy. Anything serious, straight to the **guardia**.

  After:

  > The doctor's order: **tenés que hacer reposo** — *you need bed rest*. And a **jarabe** for the cough.
  > 
  > A plain **resfrío** — *a cold* — needs no doctor, just a **remedio** from the pharmacy.
  > 
  > Anything serious, straight to the **guardia**.

- `edf96564` como-se-siente · La prepaga

  Before:

  > Besides the public hospital and the *obra social*, lots of porteños pay a **prepaga** — a private health plan. Once a year: a **chequeo**, an *análisis de* **sangre**, **la presión** and **la vacuna** for the **gripe**.

  After:

  > A **prepaga** is a private health plan. Many pay one, besides the public hospital and the *obra social*.
  > 
  > Once a year: a **chequeo**, an *análisis de* **sangre**, **la presión** and **la vacuna** for the **gripe**.

- `a27610da` convidame-uno · ¿Te convido?

  Before:

  > **Convidar** is *to offer, to share what's yours*: **¿te convido un mate?**, **convidame uno**. Afternoon **merienda** means mate with **bizcochitos**, or **tortas fritas** when it rains. A **matero** drinks it all day; kids get **mate cocido**, the tea-bag version. To ask for one, the question is kinder than the order: **¿me convidás un mate?** *Convidame* is for close friends.

  After:

  > **Convidar** is *to offer, to share what's yours*: **¿te convido un mate?**, **convidame uno**.
  > 
  > To ask for one, the question is kinder than the order: **¿me convidás un mate?** *Convidame* is for close friends.
  > 
  > Afternoon **merienda** means mate with **bizcochitos**, or **tortas fritas** when it rains. A **matero** drinks it all day; kids get **mate cocido**, the tea-bag version.

- `e5afe1ea` donde-estara · The future of guessing

  Before:

  > This future is for wondering: **¿dónde estará?** — *I wonder where he is*. Same with **¿qué hora será?**, **¿tendrá cargador?**, **¿habrá café?**, **¿qué hará?**, **¿vendrá?**, **¿qué le pasará?** For *they*, add **n**: **¿dónde estarán?**, **¿de quién serán?** For plans, porteños say *voy a*.

  After:

  > This future is for wondering: **¿dónde estará?** — *I wonder where he is*.
  > 
  > Same with **¿qué hora será?**, **¿tendrá cargador?**, **¿habrá café?**, **¿qué hará?**, **¿vendrá?**, **¿qué le pasará?**
  > 
  > For *they*, add **n**: **¿dónde estarán?**, **¿de quién serán?** For plans, porteños say *voy a*.

- `301acde2` el-celu · El celu, la SUBE

  Before:

  > The card for the bus and the subte is **la SUBE**: you **cargás** it at a kiosco — the same verb as charging **el celu**, the phone (short for **celular**). *Celular* is also the phone number: *pasame tu celular* — *give me your number*.

  After:

  > The card for the bus and the subte is **la SUBE**. You **cargás** it at a kiosco.
  > 
  > The same verb charges **el celu**, the phone (short for **celular**).
  > 
  > *Celular* is also the phone number: *pasame tu celular* — *give me your number*.

- `4e8246f2` el-celu · Me quedé sin batería

  Before:

  > **Me quedé sin batería** — *my battery died*, literally *I was left without battery*. A call, or the power at home, **se cortó** — *it cut out*. Without *sin*, **quedarse** is *to stay*: *¿te quedaste en casa?*

  After:

  > **Me quedé sin batería** — *my battery died*, literally *I was left without battery*.
  > 
  > A call, or the power at home, **se cortó** — *it cut out*.
  > 
  > Without *sin*, **quedarse** is *to stay*: *¿te quedaste en casa?*

- `d8cbc098` es-insoportable · No lo aguanto

  Before:

  > The other end: **no lo aguanto** — *I can't stand him*; **es insoportable**; **es re falso**. And the good end: **nos hicimos amigos** — *we became friends*. And **hay química** — *there's chemistry* — is mostly said about a couple.

  After:

  > The other end: **no lo aguanto** — *I can't stand him*; **es insoportable**; **es re falso**.
  > 
  > The good end: **nos hicimos amigos** — *we became friends*.
  > 
  > And **hay química** — *there's chemistry* — is mostly said about a couple.

- `411110dd` es-medio-vago · Medio, re

  Before:

  > Porteños grade a person with two little words: **re** turns it up — *es re generosa* — and **medio** turns it down — *es medio vago*, *he's a bit lazy*. **Medio** never changes: *medio tonta*, *medio serios*. And the verb matters: **es callado** — *he is a quiet guy* — is what he is like; **está callado** — *he is quiet today* — is how he is right now. Same with **es serio** and **estás serio**.

  After:

  > With **re** you turn it up: *es re generosa*. With **medio** you turn it down: *es medio vago*, *he's a bit lazy*.
  > 
  > **Medio** never changes: *medio tonta*, *medio serios*.
  > 
  > The verb matters: **es callado** — *he is a quiet guy* — is what he is like. But **está callado** — *he is quiet today* — is how he is right now. Same with **es serio** and **estás serio**.

- `59b9932a` estamos-de-novios · Nos conocemos, se separaron

  Before:

  > The *each other* verbs take **nos** for *we* and **se** for *they*: **nos conocemos del laburo** — *we know each other from work*; **nos escribimos** — *we write to each other*; **se separaron** — *they split up*; **siempre se pelean** — *they're always fighting*.

  After:

  > For *each other*: **nos** (*we*), **se** (*they*).
  > 
  > We: **nos conocemos del laburo** — *we know each other from work*; **nos escribimos** — *we write to each other*.
  > 
  > They: **se separaron** — *they split up*; **siempre se pelean** — *they're always fighting*.

- `97e6c524` la-ruta · Out of the city

  Before:

  > Leaving Buenos Aires you take **la autopista** (the fast road, with tolls), then **la ruta** (the ordinary road between towns). First you **cargás nafta** — *nafta*, never *gasolina* — and on the way you pay the **peaje**. **La General Paz** is the ring road between the Capital and the conurbano. Porteños *agarran* a road rather than *take* it: **agarrá la ruta**, **¿qué ruta agarramos?**

  After:

  > The fast road, with tolls, is **la autopista**. The ordinary road between towns is **la ruta**.
  > 
  > First you **cargás nafta** — *nafta*, never *gasolina* — and on the way you pay the **peaje**. **La General Paz** is the ring road between the Capital and the conurbano.
  > 
  > Porteños *agarran* a road rather than *take* it: **agarrá la ruta**, **¿qué ruta agarramos?**

- `e29e946c` la-ruta · Manejé, ¿manejaste?

  Before:

  > *Manejar* in the past is regular: **manejé**, **¿manejaste vos?**. **Chocar** swaps *c* for *qu* before *é*: **choqué**. Run a red light and it's **una multa**.

  After:

  > *Manejar* in the past is regular: **manejé**, **¿manejaste vos?**
  > 
  > **Chocar** swaps *c* for *qu* before *é*: **choqué**.
  > 
  > Run a red light and it's **una multa**.

- `3d16b064` manejar-en-baires · La avenida

  Before:

  > Buenos Aires is a grid of streets cut by wide **avenidas** — Corrientes, Santa Fe, 9 de Julio. On an avenida almost every corner has a **semáforo**. Cross when it's green: **cruzá**.

  After:

  > Buenos Aires is a grid of streets cut by wide **avenidas** — Corrientes, Santa Fe, 9 de Julio.
  > 
  > On an avenida almost every corner has a **semáforo**. Cross when it's green: **cruzá**.

- `bdb46568` manejar-en-baires · Manejar

  Before:

  > A porteño **maneja** (drives) an *auto*. *Conducir* is only for forms and signs. Downtown, the **tránsito** is often **imposible**, and so is finding a place to *estacionar* (park).

  After:

  > A porteño **maneja** (drives) an *auto*. *Conducir* is only for forms and signs.
  > 
  > Downtown, the **tránsito** is often **imposible**, and so is finding a place to *estacionar* (park).

- `9f1b4f75` me-afanaron · Me afanaron

  Before:

  > Among friends it's **me afanaron** — *they stole it* (slang). It is also what you say when you were overcharged: *me afanaron con el taxi* — *the taxi overcharged me*. The **motochorro** steals from a motorbike. The confession that comes with it: **me descuidé** — *I wasn't careful*.

  After:

  > Among friends it's **me afanaron** — *they stole it* (slang).
  > 
  > It is also for when you were overcharged: *me afanaron con el taxi* — *the taxi overcharged me*.
  > 
  > The **motochorro** steals from a motorbike. The confession: **me descuidé** — *I wasn't careful*.

- `f82e6975` me-cae-bien · Me cae bien

  Before:

  > **Me cae bien** is *I like him* — as a person, not romantically. It works like *gustar*: **me cae bien Juan**, **me caen bien tus amigos**. The first impression: **me cayó mal**.

  After:

  > **Me cae bien** is *I like him* — as a person, not romantically.
  > 
  > It works like *gustar*: **me cae bien Juan**, **me caen bien tus amigos**.
  > 
  > The first impression: **me cayó mal**.

- `3cc9772d` me-contrataron · El currículum

  Before:

  > You send **el currículum**; they ask about your **experiencia** and tell you about the **puesto** — *the position*. **Pagan bien** — *they pay well* — is what everyone wants to hear.

  After:

  > You send **el currículum**. They ask about your **experiencia** and tell you about the **puesto** — *the position*.
  > 
  > **Pagan bien** — *they pay well*.

- `47214591` me-contrataron · El aguinaldo

  Before:

  > Twice a year, in June and December, the **aguinaldo** arrives — half a month's extra pay. Every job *en blanco* pays it by law. A **licencia** is *paid leave* for a reason (sick, a new baby, exams). Your yearly holiday is **vacaciones**.

  After:

  > Twice a year, in June and December, the **aguinaldo** arrives — half a month's extra pay. Every job *en blanco* pays it by law.
  > 
  > A **licencia** is *paid leave* for a reason (sick, a new baby, exams).
  > 
  > Your yearly holiday is **vacaciones**.

- `7b3f9683` me-contrataron · Consiguió, renunció

  Before:

  > The rest of the story is in the third person: **consiguió laburo** — *she got a job*; **renunció** — *he quit*. Nobody says *fue despedido*: it's **lo echaron** — *they fired him* — and **me contrataron** — *they hired me*.

  After:

  > The rest of the story is third person. So: **consiguió laburo** — *she got a job*; **renunció** — *he quit*.
  > 
  > Nobody says *fue despedido*. It's **lo echaron** — *they fired him*. And **me contrataron** — *they hired me*.

- `68bbdfb3` me-estafaron · El cuento del tío

  Before:

  > **Me estafaron** — *I got scammed* : nobody grabs anything, they trick you. A fake call from the bank, a **billete trucho**, or the **cuento del tío**: a caller pretends to be a relative or an official and asks for money. First thing: **bloqueá la tarjeta**. Then the denuncia; if you're lucky, the street **cámaras** recorded it.

  After:

  > **Me estafaron** — *I got scammed*. Nobody grabs anything, they trick you.
  > 
  > It can be a fake call from the bank, a **billete trucho**, or the **cuento del tío**: a caller pretends to be a relative or an official and asks for money.
  > 
  > First: **bloqueá la tarjeta**. Then the denuncia. With luck, the street **cámaras** recorded it.

- `3aedcd83` me-robaron · Me robaron

  Before:

  > **Me robaron el celu** — *my phone got stolen*, literally *they stole the phone from me*. The thief is a **chorro** in lunfardo, a **ladrón** in the news. On the subte it is usually a **punga** — *a pickpocket* — and you notice later. **Me asaltaron** is worse: they robbed you face to face.

  After:

  > **Me robaron el celu** — *my phone got stolen*, literally *they stole the phone from me*.
  > 
  > The thief is a **chorro** in lunfardo, a **ladrón** in the news. On the subte it is usually a **punga** — *a pickpocket*.
  > 
  > **Me asaltaron** is worse: they robbed you face to face.

- `65cb8068` me-robaron · La denuncia

  Before:

  > To report it you **hacés la denuncia** at the **comisaría**, or online. The **policía** rarely finds the phone; the denuncia is for the insurance. To block the line, call your phone company. In an emergency, call **911**.

  After:

  > To report it you **hacés la denuncia** at the **comisaría**, or online.
  > 
  > The **policía** rarely finds the phone; the denuncia is for the insurance.
  > 
  > To block the line, call your phone company. In an emergency, call **911**.

- `1a8b45ec` me-siento-mal · Me siento

  Before:

  > **Me siento mal** — *I feel sick*; **¿cómo te sentís?** — *how do you feel?* The vowel changes with *yo*, not with *vos*: **me siento**, but **te sentís**. *Dormir* does the same: **duermo**, but **dormís**; **dormí** is the past, *I slept*. On its own, **lo siento** is *I'm sorry*.

  After:

  > **Me siento mal** — *I feel sick*; **¿cómo te sentís?** — *how do you feel?*
  > 
  > The vowel changes with *yo*, not with *vos*: **me siento**, but **te sentís**. *Dormir* does the same: **duermo**, but **dormís**; **dormí** is the past, *I slept*.
  > 
  > On its own, **lo siento** is *I'm sorry*.

- `f8060d63` me-siento-mal · La obra social

  Before:

  > Your health insurance is your **obra social**; the doctor writes a **receta** and you take the **pastillas** **cada ocho horas**.

  After:

  > Your health insurance is your **obra social**.
  > 
  > The doctor writes a **receta** and you take the **pastillas** **cada ocho horas**.

- `3cf567c2` medio-agrandado · The good and the bad

  Before:

  > **Macanudo** is the old-school *great guy* — you will hear it from older people; under fifty it is **copado** or **piola**, and **es una genia** for a woman who is great at what she does; **trabajador** is *hard-working*. **Agrandado** is *full of himself*, **egoísta** *selfish* — and *egoísta* is the same for him and for her, like *inteligente*.

  After:

  > **Macanudo** is the old-school *great guy*; you will hear it from older people. Under fifty it is **copado** or **piola**.
  > 
  > A woman who is great at what she does: **es una genia**. And **trabajador** is *hard-working*.
  > 
  > **Agrandado** is *full of himself*, **egoísta** *selfish*. The word *egoísta* is the same for him and for her, like *inteligente*.

- `501983de` medio-agrandado · Plural

  Before:

  > Describe a group with **son** and a plural ending (**-s**, or **-es** after a consonant): **son re trabajadores**, **son medio distraídos**. A mixed group takes the masculine form.

  After:

  > A group takes **son** and a plural ending: **son re trabajadores**, **son medio distraídos**. Add **-s**, or **-es** after a consonant.
  > 
  > A mixed group takes the masculine form.

- `f7382eb9` no-lo-aguanto · Le cae, nos cae

  Before:

  > The whole *gustar* pattern: **le cae bien a mi vieja**, **nos cae re bien**, **¿te caen bien mis amigos?** The person being liked is the subject: *mis amigos **te caen** bien*.

  After:

  > The whole *gustar* pattern: **le cae bien a mi vieja**, **nos cae re bien**, **¿te caen bien mis amigos?**
  > 
  > The person being liked is the subject: *mis amigos **te caen** bien*.

- `524511f8` no-tengo-senal · Sin señal, sin datos

  Before:

  > **No tengo señal** — *no signal*; **me quedé sin datos** — *I ran out of data*. **¿Tenés wifi?** is the first question at any bar.

  After:

  > **No tengo señal** — *no signal*; **me quedé sin datos** — *I ran out of data*.
  > 
  > **¿Tenés wifi?** is the first question at any bar.

- `6d7c070b` no-tengo-senal · Descargá, borrá

  Before:

  > Tech orders are plain vos commands: **descargá la app**, **borrá las fotos**. What you already did is preterite: **la descargué**, **las borré**. Day to day people say **bajá** or **bajate** for *download*: **bajate la app, es gratis**. *Descargar* is what the screen says.

  After:

  > Tech orders are plain vos commands: **descargá la app**, **borrá las fotos**.
  > 
  > What you already did is preterite: **la descargué**, **las borré**.
  > 
  > Day to day people say **bajá** or **bajate** for *download*: **bajate la app, es gratis**. *Descargar* is what the screen says.

- `ecc14597` no-tengo-senal · No anda, se apagó

  Before:

  > When anything stops working, porteños say it **no anda**: *no anda el wifi*, *no me anda el celu*. A machine that fails on its own takes **se**: **se apagó** — *it switched off*; **se colgó** — *it froze*. The universal fix: **apagalo y prendelo**.

  After:

  > When anything stops working, porteños say it **no anda**: *no anda el wifi*, *no me anda el celu*.
  > 
  > A machine that fails on its own takes **se**: **se apagó** — *it switched off*; **se colgó** — *it froze*.
  > 
  > The fix: **apagalo y prendelo**.

- `dc575731` practica-a-lo-mejor · Wonder, then guess

  Before:

  > **¿Qué le pasará?** wonders; **a lo mejor tiene gripe** guesses; **seguro que está cansado** bets. The future can guess on its own too: **Sofi estará en el subte** — *Sofi's probably on the subway*.

  After:

  > **¿Qué le pasará?** wonders; **a lo mejor tiene gripe** guesses; **seguro que está cansado** bets.
  > 
  > The future can guess on its own too: **Sofi estará en el subte** — *Sofi's probably on the subway*.

- `04747eea` quien-ceba · Cebar

  Before:

  > One person **ceba** — pours the water from the **termo** — and passes the mate around the **ronda**. You drink it all, through the **bombilla**, and hand it back to the **cebador**, the person who pours.

  After:

  > One person **ceba**: pours the water from the **termo** and passes the mate around the **ronda**.
  > 
  > You drink it all, through the **bombilla**, and hand it back to the **cebador**, the person who pours.

- `dbb9fb4d` salir-con-alguien · Salir con, cortar

  Before:

  > **Estoy saliendo con alguien** — *I'm seeing someone*. When it ends, porteños **cortan**: **cortamos** — *we broke up*. With a name they say **con Santi cortamos** — *Santi and I broke up* — and the same for a fight: **con Sofi nos peleamos**. **Nos peleamos** is *we fight* and also *we had a fight*.

  After:

  > **Estoy saliendo con alguien** — *I'm seeing someone*. When it ends, porteños **cortan**: **cortamos** — *we broke up*.
  > 
  > With a name they say **con Santi cortamos** — *Santi and I broke up*. The same for a fight: **con Sofi nos peleamos**.
  > 
  > **Nos peleamos** is *we fight* and also *we had a fight*.

- `e73a1f00` salir-con-alguien · Alguien, nadie

  Before:

  > **Alguien** is *someone*, **nadie** is *nobody* — and Spanish keeps the *no* as well: **no vino nadie**. If *nadie* comes first, drop the *no*: **nadie me dijo nada**.

  After:

  > **Alguien** is *someone*, **nadie** is *nobody*.
  > 
  > Spanish keeps the *no* as well: **no vino nadie**. If *nadie* comes first, drop the *no*: **nadie me dijo nada**.

- `28e641b6` se-caso · Se casó, nació

  Before:

  > | Mi prima **se casó** el sábado. | got married |
  > | **Nos casamos** en marzo. | we got married |
  > | El bebé **nació** ayer. | was born |
  > | ¿Dónde **naciste**? | were you born |
  > 
  > **Casarse** takes its pronoun; **nacer** doesn't. **Nos casamos** is also the present: *nos casamos en julio* — *we're getting married in July*. The wedding is a **casamiento**; the legal one is **por civil**, or just **el civil**. Before it, **se comprometieron** — *they got engaged*; after it, the **luna de miel**. **Está embarazada** is *she's pregnant*.

  After:

  > | Mi prima **se casó** el sábado. | got married |
  > | **Nos casamos** en marzo. | we got married |
  > | El bebé **nació** ayer. | was born |
  > | ¿Dónde **naciste**? | were you born |
  > 
  > **Casarse** takes its pronoun; **nacer** doesn't. **Nos casamos** is also the present: *nos casamos en julio* — *we're getting married in July*.
  > 
  > The wedding is a **casamiento**; the legal one is **por civil**, or just **el civil**. Before it, **se comprometieron** — *they got engaged*; after it, the **luna de miel**.
  > 
  > **Está embarazada** is *she's pregnant*.

- `164a4c51` se-recibio · Se recibió, se jubiló

  Before:

  > Life's big steps take the pronoun, like **se casó**: **me recibí** — *I graduated*; mi hermano **se recibió** de abogado; mi tía **se jubiló** — *retired*. Ask **¿de qué te recibiste?** — *what did you graduate in?* **Se murió** — *died* — is for talking among friends; to the family you say **falleció** — *passed away*.

  After:

  > Life's big steps take the pronoun, like **se casó**. So: **me recibí** — *I graduated*; mi hermano **se recibió** de abogado; mi tía **se jubiló** — *retired*.
  > 
  > Ask **¿de qué te recibiste?** — *what did you graduate in?*
  > 
  > **Se murió** — *died* — is for friends; to the family you say **falleció** — *passed away*.

- `5da4918a` se-separaron · Estamos de novios

  Before:

  > Going steady is **estar de novios**. The one before is **mi ex**, and a porteño couple calls each other **amor**. When it ends badly: **me dejó** — *she left me* — or **me mintió** — *he lied to me*.

  After:

  > Going steady is **estar de novios**. The one before is **mi ex**, and a porteño couple calls each other **amor**.
  > 
  > When it ends badly: **me dejó** — *she left me* — or **me mintió** — *he lied to me*.

- `88b903ef` sos-un-genio · Me salvaste

  Before:

  > Porteño thanks praise the person: **sos un genio**, **me salvaste** — *you saved me*. To a woman it is always **sos una genia**. For a present: **¡qué detalle!** — *how thoughtful!* — or **¡te pasaste!** — *this is too much, you did an amazing job!* Said in an angry voice, the same words mean *you went too far*.

  After:

  > Porteño thanks praise the person: **sos un genio**, **me salvaste** — *you saved me*. To a woman it is always **sos una genia**.
  > 
  > For a present: **¡qué detalle!** — *how thoughtful!* — or **¡te pasaste!** — *this is too much, you did an amazing job!*
  > 
  > Said in an angry voice, the same words mean *you went too far*.

- `c38f50d3` sos-un-genio · Ni hablar

  Before:

  > The answers: *de nada*, **ni hablar** — *don't mention it* — or **lo hice de onda** — *I did it to be nice*. **Te agradezco** is warmer and a touch more formal: **te agradezco de verdad**.

  After:

  > The answers: *de nada*, **ni hablar** — *don't mention it* — or **lo hice de onda** — *I did it to be nice*.
  > 
  > **Te agradezco** is warmer and a touch more formal: **te agradezco de verdad**.

- `088111ce` te-convido-un-mate · Calentá el agua, no la hiervas

  Before:

  > The rules for the person who pours: **calentá el agua** in the pava — *heat the water* — but **no la hiervas**: water that **hierve** burns the yerba. **Yo cebo**, you drink. When there's no taste left, **el mate está lavado** — *washed out* — and it's time to change the yerba.

  After:

  > For the person who pours: **calentá el agua** in the pava — *heat the water* — but **no la hiervas**. Water that **hierve** burns the yerba.
  > 
  > **Yo cebo**, you drink.
  > 
  > When there's no taste left, **el mate está lavado** — *washed out* — and it's time to change the yerba.

- `85eeb729` te-debo-una · Mil gracias

  Before:

  > **Gracias por todo** — *thanks for everything*; **mil gracias** — *thanks a million*; **gracias otra vez** — *thanks again*. The thanks gets bigger, the grammar doesn't change. You already know **un montón**: *gracias un montón*.

  After:

  > The thanks gets bigger, the grammar doesn't change.
  > 
  > **Gracias por todo** — *thanks for everything*; **mil gracias** — *thanks a million*; **gracias otra vez** — *thanks again*.
  > 
  > You already know **un montón**: *gracias un montón*.

- `e8975dc4` te-debo-una · Te debo una

  Before:

  > **Te debo una** — *I owe you one*. The answer is **de nada**. **No pasa nada** — *it's fine, no worries* — is the answer to *perdón*.

  After:

  > **Te debo una** — *I owe you one*. The answer is **de nada**.
  > 
  > **No pasa nada** — *it's fine, no worries* — is the answer to *perdón*.

- `018deba2` te-lo-devuelvo · Devolvémelo

  Before:

  > On a vos command both pronouns join the end, person first, and the stress gets a written accent: **devolvémelo** — *give it back to me*; **pasámelo** — *send it to me*; **decímelo** — *tell me*. To him or her, *le* turns into *se* again: **mandáselo**.

  After:

  > On a vos command both pronouns join the end, person first.
  > 
  > The stress gets a written accent: **devolvémelo** — *give it back to me*; **pasámelo** — *send it to me*; **decímelo** — *tell me*.
  > 
  > To him or her, *le* turns into *se* again: **mandáselo**.

- `e310bbae` te-lo-devuelvo · Te lo, se lo

  Before:

  > | Te devuelvo **el libro**. | Te **lo** devuelvo. |
  > | ¿Me dejás **las llaves**? | ¿Me **las** dejás? |
  > | Le regalé **un libro**. | **Se lo** regalé. |
  > 
  > The person goes first, the thing second. **Lo, la, los, las** match the thing: *el libro* → **lo**, *las llaves* → **las**. *Le* or *les* before them becomes **se**; add **a Sofi** to say who: **se la regalé a Sofi**.

  After:

  > | Te devuelvo **el libro**. | Te **lo** devuelvo. |
  > | ¿Me dejás **las llaves**? | ¿Me **las** dejás? |
  > | Le regalé **un libro**. | **Se lo** regalé. |
  > 
  > The person goes first, the thing second. **Lo, la, los, las** match the thing: *el libro* → **lo**, *las llaves* → **las**.
  > 
  > *Le* or *les* before them becomes **se**. Add **a Sofi** to say who: **se la regalé a Sofi**.

- `eac9e910` te-lo-presto · Te lo paso

  Before:

  > **Te lo paso** is *I'll send it to you* — a number, a photo, a file. And a loan comes back **sin falta** — *for sure* (word for word, *without fail*).

  After:

  > **Te lo paso** is *I'll send it to you* — a number, a photo, a file.
  > 
  > A loan comes back **sin falta** — *for sure* (word for word, *without fail*).

- `9cabde8c` tenes-registro · Papers and parking

  Before:

  > Your driver's license is the **registro** — *¿tenés registro?*. Your own or rented parking space is a **cochera**; a public paid parking lot is an *estacionamiento*.

  After:

  > Your driver's license is the **registro**: *¿tenés registro?*
  > 
  > Your own or rented parking space is a **cochera**. A public paid parking lot is an *estacionamiento*.

- `b8dca299` cuando-vuelvas · Cuando quieras

  Before:

  > **Cuando quieras** — *whenever you like* — leaves an invitation open. A question with **¿cuándo?** keeps the normal verb: **¿cuándo nos vemos?** A plan with **cuando** doesn't: **cuando nos veamos, te cuento**.

  After:

  > **Cuando quieras** — *whenever you like* — leaves an invitation open.
  > 
  > A question with **¿cuándo?** keeps the normal verb: **¿cuándo nos vemos?** A plan with **cuando** doesn't: **cuando nos veamos, te cuento**.

- `cc1eb54f` cuando-vuelvas · Hasta que, para que

  Before:

  > **Hasta que** — *until* — works like *cuando*: **hasta que te mudes** looks ahead, but **hasta que conseguí laburo** is past, so the normal verb. **Para que** — *so that* — and **antes de que** — *before* — always take the subjunctive: **para que sepas**, **vení antes de que empiece**. And **que yo sepa** — *as far as I know*.

  After:

  > **Hasta que** — *until* — works like *cuando*. Ahead: **hasta que te mudes**. But **hasta que conseguí laburo** is past, so the normal verb.
  > 
  > **Para que** — *so that* — and **antes de que** — *before* — always take the subjunctive: **para que sepas**, **vení antes de que empiece**.
  > 
  > And **que yo sepa** — *as far as I know*.

- `c5e7d696` cuidate · The long goodbye

  Before:

  > A porteño goodbye is several: **bueno, nos hablamos**, **cuidate**, **un beso**, **que descanses** — and then another *chau* at the door. **Un abrazo** — or just **abrazo** — is the usual sign-off, especially between men: **dale, abrazo**; **chau, un abrazo**.

  After:

  > A goodbye is several. First **bueno, nos hablamos**, **cuidate**, **un beso**, **que descanses**, then another *chau* at the door.
  > 
  > **Un abrazo** — or just **abrazo** — is the usual sign-off, especially between men: **dale, abrazo**; **chau, un abrazo**.

- `26ece732` es-un-afano · Garpar, un afano

  Before:

  > Money talk goes lunfardo fast: **¿quién garpa?** — *who's paying?*; **¡es un afano!** — *it's a rip-off!*; **berreta** — *cheap, shoddy*. And food is **el morfi**.

  After:

  > Money talk goes lunfardo fast: **¿quién garpa?** — *who's paying?*; **¡es un afano!** — *it's a rip-off!*; **berreta** — *cheap, shoddy*.
  > 
  > Food is **el morfi**.

- `71152a01` es-un-afano · Chanta, cheto, piola

  Before:

  > People get lunfardo labels: a **chanta** talks big and doesn't deliver, a **cheto** is snobby or upper-class, and someone **piola** is cool and easy-going. **No me chamuyes** — *don't give me that* — is the subjunctive again.

  After:

  > People get lunfardo labels. A **chanta** talks big and doesn't deliver. A **cheto** is snobby or upper-class. Someone **piola** is cool and easy-going.
  > 
  > **No me chamuyes** — *don't give me that* — is the subjunctive again.

- `931d857d` es-un-afano · Qué embole

  Before:

  > **¡Qué embole!** — *how boring!* — for a rainy Sunday with nothing to do. Like all of these: friends, not job interviews.

  After:

  > **¡Qué embole!** — *how boring!* — for a rainy Sunday with nothing to do.
  > 
  > Like all of these: friends, not job interviews.

- `3fe61f9b` lunfardo · Lunfardo

  Before:

  > Lunfardo is Buenos Aires slang, born in the port and the tango: **morfar** (to eat), **guita** (money), **fiaca** (laziness), **pilcha** (clothes). Friends, not job interviews. **Guita** and **fiaca** are fine in any relaxed talk; **morfar** is rougher: with people you just met, say *comer*.

  After:

  > Lunfardo is Buenos Aires slang, born in the port and the tango. Like **morfar** (to eat), **guita** (money), **fiaca** (laziness), **pilcha** (clothes).
  > 
  > Friends, not job interviews. **Guita** and **fiaca** are fine in relaxed talk; **morfar** is rougher: with new people, say *comer*.

- `e1ff50e4` lunfardo · Bancar

  Before:

  > **Te banco** — *I'm here for you*; **no lo banco** — *I can't stand him*. One verb, opposite meanings. It also means *to wait for*: **¿me bancás acá?** — *can you wait for me here?* For things you often hear **bancarse**: *no me banco el frío* — *I can't stand the cold*.

  After:

  > **Te banco** — *I'm here for you*; **no lo banco** — *I can't stand him*. One verb, opposite meanings.
  > 
  > It also means *to wait for*: **¿me bancás acá?** — *can you wait for me here?*
  > 
  > For things you often hear **bancarse**: *no me banco el frío* — *I can't stand the cold*.

- `74fa9b53` me-preocupa · Qué suerte que

  Before:

  > **¡Qué suerte que vivas cerca!**, **¡qué bueno que estemos todos juntos!** Any *qué* + reaction + *que* brings the subjunctive. **Estemos** is *we*: **-mos** on the end. About something already over, porteños just use the past: **¡qué suerte que no llovió!**

  After:

  > Any *qué* + reaction + *que* brings the subjunctive.
  > 
  > **¡Qué suerte que vivas cerca!**, **¡qué bueno que estemos todos juntos!** **Estemos** is *we*: **-mos** on the end.
  > 
  > About something already over, porteños just use the past: **¡qué suerte que no llovió!**

- `28a65e19` necesito-que-me-ayudes · -ar takes e, the rest take a

  Before:

  > **Ayudar → ayudes**, **esperar → esperes**, **mandar → mandes**, **avisar → avises**. *Poner* and *traer* keep the *g* of their *yo*: **pongo → pongas**, **traigo → traiga**. The same form, with **no** in front, tells someone not to: *esperame* — **no me esperes**; *llamame* — **no me llames**. The pronoun jumps to the front.

  After:

  > **Ayudar → ayudes**, **esperar → esperes**, **mandar → mandes**, **avisar → avises**.
  > 
  > *Poner* and *traer* keep the *g* of their *yo*: **pongo → pongas**, **traigo → traiga**.
  > 
  > The same form, with **no** in front, tells someone not to: *esperame* — **no me esperes**; *llamame* — **no me llames**. The pronoun jumps to the front.

- `97dca14e` necesito-que-me-ayudes · Necesito que, ¿querés que…?

  Before:

  > | | vos | yo, él, ella |
  > | ayudar | **ayudes** | **ayude** |
  > | esperar | **esperes** | **espere** |
  > | poner | **pongas** | **ponga** |
  > | traer | **traigas** | **traiga** |
  > | llevar | **lleves** | **lleve** |
  > | dar | **des** | **dé** |
  > **Necesito que**, **te pido que**, **prefiero que** and the offer **¿querés que…?** all take the subjunctive: *necesito que me ayudes*, *te pido que me esperes*, *¿querés que te lleve?* For *we*: **¿qué querés que hagamos?**

  After:

  > | | vos | yo, él, ella |
  > | ayudar | **ayudes** | **ayude** |
  > | esperar | **esperes** | **espere** |
  > | poner | **pongas** | **ponga** |
  > | traer | **traigas** | **traiga** |
  > | llevar | **lleves** | **lleve** |
  > | dar | **des** | **dé** |
  > 
  > **Necesito que**, **te pido que**, **prefiero que** and the offer **¿querés que…?** all take the subjunctive: *necesito que me ayudes*, *¿querés que te lleve?*
  > 
  > For *we*: **¿qué querés que hagamos?**

- `c877808b` necesito-que-me-ayudes · ¿Querés que te lleve?

  Before:

  > The offer is the same pattern turned around: **¿querés que te lleve?** — *do you want me to give you a ride?*; **¿querés que traiga algo?** — the guest's question before every *asado*. The *yo* form matches *él*: **lleve**, **traiga**, **ponga**.

  After:

  > The offer is the same pattern turned around: **¿querés que te lleve?** — *do you want me to give you a ride?*
  > 
  > Before an *asado*, the guest asks **¿querés que traiga algo?**
  > 
  > The *yo* form matches *él*: **lleve**, **traiga**, **ponga**.

- `3720e015` no-creo · Para mí

  Before:

  > Day to day an opinion starts with **para mí**: *para mí, está cerrado* — *if you ask me, it's closed*. **Para mí que** + a normal verb is a guess: *para mí que hoy llueve*. And **no me parece que** takes the subjunctive, like *no creo que*: *no me parece que haya mucha gente*.

  After:

  > Day to day an opinion starts with **para mí**: *para mí, está cerrado* — *if you ask me, it's closed*.
  > 
  > **Para mí que** + a normal verb is a guess: *para mí que hoy llueve*.
  > 
  > And **no me parece que** takes the subjunctive, like *no creo que*: *no me parece que haya mucha gente*.

- `709de3f5` no-seas-asi · More don'ts

  Before:

  > | No **dejes** el celu en la mesa. | Don't leave your phone on the table. |
  > | No **saques** la billetera. | Don't take your wallet out. |
  > | No le **cuentes** a nadie. | Don't tell anyone. |
  > | No **te vayas**. | Don't go. |
  > | No **te pongas** nervioso. | Don't get nervous. |
  > | No **seas** así. | Don't be like that. |
  > Every *don't* grows from the *yo* of the present: *dejo* → **dejes**, *cuento* → **cuentes**, *saco* → **saques** (the *c* turns *qu* to keep its sound). *Ser* and *ir* break the rule: **seas**, like *sea*, and **vayas** (*no te vayas*).

  After:

  > | No **dejes** el celu en la mesa. | Don't leave your phone on the table. |
  > | No **saques** la billetera. | Don't take your wallet out. |
  > | No le **cuentes** a nadie. | Don't tell anyone. |
  > | No **te vayas**. | Don't go. |
  > | No **te pongas** nervioso. | Don't get nervous. |
  > | No **seas** así. | Don't be like that. |
  > 
  > Every *don't* grows from the *yo* of the present: *dejo* → **dejes**, *cuento* → **cuentes**, *saco* → **saques** (the *c* turns *qu* to keep its sound).
  > 
  > *Ser* and *ir* break the rule: **seas**, like *sea*, and **vayas** (*no te vayas*).

- `7fd63f41` no-seas-asi · No te vayas, no te pongas así

  Before:

  > With *-se* verbs the pronoun goes in front: *me voy* → **no te vayas**, *me puse nervioso* → **no te pongas nervioso**, *me enojé* → **no te enojes**. And the one every friend says: **no seas así**.

  After:

  > With *-se* verbs the pronoun goes in front: *me voy* → **no te vayas**.
  > 
  > Also *me puse nervioso* → **no te pongas nervioso**, *me enojé* → **no te enojes**.
  > 
  > Friends also say **no seas así**.

- `6f8ab658` no-te-preocupes · Do, and don't

  Before:

  > | do | don't |
  > | Vení. | No **vengas**. |
  > | Andá. | No **vayas**. |
  > | Decime. | No **me digas**. |
  > | Tocá. | No **toques**. |
  > | Olvidate. | No **te olvides**. |
  > 
  > *No* + the subjunctive (*tocar* → **toques**: the *c* turns into *qu* to keep its sound), and the pronoun moves in front: *preocupate* → **no te preocupes**. **¡No me digas!** on its own shows surprise: *no way!*

  After:

  > | do | don't |
  > | Vení. | No **vengas**. |
  > | Andá. | No **vayas**. |
  > | Decime. | No **me digas**. |
  > | Tocá. | No **toques**. |
  > | Olvidate. | No **te olvides**. |
  > 
  > *No* + the subjunctive; the pronoun moves in front: *preocupate* → **no te preocupes**.
  > 
  > In *tocar* → **toques** the *c* turns into *qu* to keep its sound.
  > 
  > **¡No me digas!** on its own shows surprise: *no way!*

- `8fe89548` no-te-preocupes · No te hagas problema

  Before:

  > **No te hagas problema** — *don't worry about it* — is what a porteño says when you apologize. **No te olvides** — *don't forget*: *de* + a verb (*no te olvides de llamar*); with a thing, *de* is often dropped (*no te olvides la SUBE*).

  After:

  > **No te hagas problema** — *don't worry about it* — answers an apology.
  > 
  > **No te olvides** — *don't forget*. Before a verb add *de* (*no te olvides de llamar*). With a thing, *de* is often dropped (*no te olvides la SUBE*).

- `a2b395ff` practica-cuando-vuelvas · Wishes and not-yet

  Before:

  > Wishes and the future share the form: **que te diviertas**, **ojalá que ganemos**, **cuando vuelvas**, **hasta que sepa algo**. An open choice takes it too: **lo que quieras** — *whatever you want*.

  After:

  > Wishes and the future share the form. So: **que te diviertas**, **ojalá que ganemos**, **cuando vuelvas**, **hasta que sepa algo**.
  > 
  > So does an open choice: **lo que quieras** — *whatever you want*.

- `cc5c7a5d` practica-cuando-vuelvas · No hace falta que

  Before:

  > **No hace falta que** + subjunctive is *you do not need to*: **no hace falta que vengas hoy**. With a thing, **hace falta** is *we need*: **hace falta pan**.

  After:

  > **No hace falta que** + subjunctive is *you do not need to*: **no hace falta que vengas hoy**.
  > 
  > With a thing, **hace falta** is *we need*: **hace falta pan**.

- `156c9226` puede-ser-que · Puede ser que, for them

  Before:

  > | | él, ella | ellos |
  > | ser | sea | **sean** |
  > | estar | esté | **estén** |
  > | tener | tenga | **tengan** |
  > | poder | pueda | **puedan** |
  > | llegar | llegue | **lleguen** |
  > For *they*, add **-n**. **Puede ser que** and **es posible que** take the subjunctive like *no creo que*: *puede ser que estén cerrados*. The same form is *you* for a group: *que tengan buen finde, chicos*.

  After:

  > | | él, ella | ellos |
  > | ser | sea | **sean** |
  > | estar | esté | **estén** |
  > | tener | tenga | **tengan** |
  > | poder | pueda | **puedan** |
  > | llegar | llegue | **lleguen** |
  > 
  > For *they*, add **-n**. The same form is *you* for a group: *que tengan buen finde, chicos*.
  > 
  > **Puede ser que** and **es posible que** take the subjunctive like *no creo que*: *puede ser que estén cerrados*.

- `3f9a94db` puede-ser-que · Puede ser que

  Before:

  > **Puede ser** — *could be* — is a common *maybe*, like **capaz**. Add *que* and the subjunctive follows: **puede ser que llueva**, **puede ser que estén cerrados**. **Es posible que** says the same, a little more carefully.

  After:

  > **Puede ser** — *could be* — is a common *maybe*, like **capaz**.
  > 
  > Add *que* and the subjunctive follows: **puede ser que llueva**, **puede ser que estén cerrados**.
  > 
  > **Es posible que** says the same, a little more carefully.

- `ec947d91` puede-ser-que · Seguro, or not

  Before:

  > Sure of it? The normal verb: **seguro que abren**, **creo que viene**. Doubt it, and the subjunctive: **no creo que abran hoy**. Even the forecast gets the treatment: **el pronóstico es de lluvia, pero no creo que llueva**.

  After:

  > When you are sure, use the normal verb: **seguro que abren**, **creo que viene**.
  > 
  > When you doubt, the subjunctive: **no creo que abran hoy**. Even with the forecast: **el pronóstico es de lluvia, pero no creo que llueva**.

- `295c40a8` que-bueno · ¿Hay novedades?

  Before:

  > **Novedades** is news about people and plans: **¿hay novedades de Juan?**, **todavía no hay novedades**. **Noticias** works too, and is also the news on TV; **una noticia** is one piece of news. Good news gets **¡buenísimo!**; bad news **¡qué mal!**

  After:

  > **Novedades** is news about people and plans: **¿hay novedades de Juan?**, **todavía no hay novedades**.
  > 
  > **Noticias** works too, and is also the news on TV; **una noticia** is one piece of news.
  > 
  > Good news gets **¡buenísimo!**; bad news **¡qué mal!**

- `9fa1891b` que-bueno · Reacting

  Before:

  > | ¡Qué bueno que **estés** acá! | So good you're here! |
  > | Qué lástima que no **vengas**. | Shame you're not coming. |
  > | Me molesta que **llegue** tarde. | It bothers me that he's late. |
  > 
  > A reaction to something now or later + **que** → the subjunctive. For something that already happened, the normal past: **¡qué bueno que viniste!** **Me alegro** on its own: *I'm glad*. With more: **me alegro que** or **me alegro de que** — both are used.

  After:

  > | ¡Qué bueno que **estés** acá! | So good you're here! |
  > | Qué lástima que no **vengas**. | Shame you're not coming. |
  > | Me molesta que **llegue** tarde. | It bothers me that he's late. |
  > 
  > A reaction to something now or later + **que** → the subjunctive. For something that already happened, the normal past: **¡qué bueno que viniste!**
  > 
  > **Me alegro** on its own: *I'm glad*. With more: **me alegro que** or **me alegro de que** — both are used.

- `6275c34f` que-significa · No sabía, me di cuenta

  Before:

  > **No sabía** — *I didn't know* — is the imperfect: a state. **Me di cuenta** — *I realized* — is the moment you finally understood. **¿Sabías que…?** opens half the gossip in Buenos Aires.

  After:

  > **No sabía** — *I didn't know* — is the imperfect: a state. **Me di cuenta** — *I realized* — is the moment you finally understood.
  > 
  > Gossip often opens with **¿Sabías que…?**

- `d16cd641` que-significa · No hace falta que entiendas todo

  Before:

  > After *quiero que*, *necesito que*, *no hace falta que* and *para que* (so that), the verb takes the subjunctive: **quiero que me expliques** — *I want you to explain it to me*; **no hace falta que entiendas todo** — *you don't need to understand everything*; **mirá el video para que entiendas** — *watch the video so you understand*.

  After:

  > The subjunctive follows *quiero que*, *necesito que*, *no hace falta que* and *para que* (so that).
  > 
  > So: **quiero que me expliques** — *I want you to explain it to me*; **no hace falta que entiendas todo** — *you don't need to understand everything*.
  > 
  > And: **mirá el video para que entiendas** — *watch the video so you understand*.

- `7d1e7009` que-te-mejores · ¡Éxitos!

  Before:

  > Before an interview or an exam porteños say **¡éxitos!** or **¡mucha suerte!** — and before a **parcial**, the university exam everyone dreads, both.

  After:

  > Before an interview or an exam porteños say **¡éxitos!** or **¡mucha suerte!**
  > 
  > Before a **parcial**, a university exam, they say both.

- `9c1177f1` que-te-mejores · Wishes for the big days

  Before:

  > | Que te mejores. | Get well soon. |
  > | Que te diviertas. | Have fun. |
  > | Que lo disfrutes. | Enjoy it. |
  > | Que cumplas muchos más. | I hope you have many more birthdays. |
  > | Ojalá que te guste. | I hope you like it. |
  > | Ojalá que ganemos. | I hope we win. |
  > 
  > The pronoun comes along into the wish: *te mejores*, *te diviertas*. For *we*, **-emos** on *-ar* verbs: *ganar* → **ganemos**. **Espero que** — *I hope* — works like *ojalá*: *espero que te guste*.

  After:

  > | Que te mejores. | Get well soon. |
  > | Que te diviertas. | Have fun. |
  > | Que lo disfrutes. | Enjoy it. |
  > | Que cumplas muchos más. | I hope you have many more birthdays. |
  > | Ojalá que te guste. | I hope you like it. |
  > | Ojalá que ganemos. | I hope we win. |
  > 
  > The pronoun comes along into the wish: *te mejores*, *te diviertas*. For *we*, **-emos** on *-ar* verbs: *ganar* → **ganemos**.
  > 
  > **Espero que** — *I hope* — works like *ojalá*: *espero que te guste*.

- `5477ef64` que-te-vaya-bien · Que…: a wish

  Before:

  > A wish is *que* + the subjunctive: **que te vaya bien** — *good luck, hope it goes well* — is also a friendly goodbye. The answer to any wish is **gracias, igualmente** — *thanks, you too*. Afterwards you ask **¿cómo te fue?** — *how did it go?*

  After:

  > A wish is *que* + the subjunctive: **que te vaya bien** — *good luck, hope it goes well*. Also a friendly goodbye.
  > 
  > The answer to any wish is **gracias, igualmente** — *thanks, you too*.
  > 
  > Afterwards you ask **¿cómo te fue?** — *how did it go?*

- `c0f803fa` que-te-vaya-bien · Wishes

  Before:

  > | Que te vaya bien. | Good luck. |
  > | Que la pases lindo. | Have a great time. |
  > | Que tengas buen finde. | Have a good weekend. |
  > | Ojalá que no llueva. | I hope it doesn't rain. |
  > | Ojalá salga todo bien. | I hope it all works out. |
  > 
  > A wish is **que** or **ojalá** + the subjunctive. After **ojalá**, *que* is optional: *ojalá que llueva* = *ojalá llueva*. *Tener → tengas, salir → salga*: the *g* of *tengo, salgo* comes along.

  After:

  > | Que te vaya bien. | Good luck. |
  > | Que la pases lindo. | Have a great time. |
  > | Que tengas buen finde. | Have a good weekend. |
  > | Ojalá que no llueva. | I hope it doesn't rain. |
  > | Ojalá salga todo bien. | I hope it all works out. |
  > 
  > A wish is **que** or **ojalá** + the subjunctive. After **ojalá**, *que* is optional: *ojalá que llueva* = *ojalá llueva*.
  > 
  > *Tener → tengas, salir → salga*: the *g* of *tengo, salgo* comes along.

- `2d43e784` quiero-que-vengas · One word for yo and él

  Before:

  > **Venga**, **vaya**, **haga** are both *I* and *he/she*: *quiere que venga* — *he wants me/him to come*. Context, or an added **yo** or **él**, tells you who: *quiere que vaya yo*. On its own, **que** + this form is *let him* or *tell him to*: *dale, que venga*.

  After:

  > **Venga**, **vaya**, **haga** are both *I* and *he/she*: *quiere que venga* — *he wants me/him to come*.
  > 
  > Context, or an added **yo** or **él**, tells you who: *quiere que vaya yo*.
  > 
  > On its own, **que** + this form is *let him* or *tell him to*: *dale, que venga*.

- `35a38f0f` quiero-que-vengas · Quiero que + a new verb form

  Before:

  > When you want *someone else* to do something, the second verb changes: **quiero que vengas** — *I want you to come*. This form is the **subjunctive**. **No hace falta que** — *there's no need to* — takes it too: *no hace falta que traigas nada*.

  After:

  > When you want *someone else* to do something, the second verb changes.
  > 
  > So: **quiero que vengas** — *I want you to come*. This is the **subjunctive**.
  > 
  > **No hace falta que** — *there's no need to* — takes it too: *no hace falta que traigas nada*.

- `17486898` saludos-a-tu-vieja · Me voy yendo

  Before:

  > The porteño exit starts long before the door: **bueno, me voy yendo** — *well, I'd better get going* — and then twenty minutes of talk. On the way out, love for whoever isn't there: **saludos a tu vieja**, **mandale saludos a Juan**. *Tu vieja* / *tu viejo* is only for friends your age; to anyone else, *saludos a tu mamá*.

  After:

  > You announce the exit long before the door: **bueno, me voy yendo** — *well, I'd better get going*.
  > 
  > On the way out, send love to whoever isn't there: **saludos a tu vieja**, **mandale saludos a Juan**.
  > 
  > *Tu vieja* / *tu viejo* is only for friends your age; to anyone else, *saludos a tu mamá*.

- `99d583b4` saludos-a-tu-vieja · One more wish

  Before:

  > A goodbye is a string of wishes, all *que* + the subjunctive: **que duermas bien**, **que sigas bien**, **que llegues bien**. On the phone it ends with **besos** and **escribime**.

  After:

  > Goodbye wishes are all *que* + the subjunctive: **que duermas bien**, **que sigas bien**, **que llegues bien**.
  > 
  > On the phone it ends with **besos** and **escribime**.

- `c25e8bf6` te-aconsejo · ¡Cuidado!

  Before:

  > **¡Cuidado!** — *careful!* — or the full **tené cuidado**: **tené cuidado con la billetera en el subte**. The tips that come with it: **no uses el celu en la calle**, **no tomes taxi para todo**, **es mejor que pagues en efectivo**, **te conviene reservar**.

  After:

  > **¡Cuidado!** — *careful!* — or the full **tené cuidado**: **tené cuidado con la billetera en el subte**.
  > 
  > The tips that come with it: **no uses el celu en la calle**, **no tomes taxi para todo**, **es mejor que pagues en efectivo**, **te conviene reservar**.

- `ccbc17ab` te-aconsejo · Te aconsejo que, conviene que

  Before:

  > | Te aconsejo que **lleves** efectivo. | My advice: bring cash. |
  > | Conviene que **tomes** el subte. | Best to take the subway. |
  > | Es mejor que **reserves**. | Better book ahead. |
  > | Es importante que **uses** la SUBE. | It's important to use your SUBE card. |
  > | Es mejor que **salgamos** temprano. | We'd better leave early. |
  > 
  > Advice with **que** takes the subjunctive. In everyday speech the short way wins — no *que*, just the infinitive: *te conviene reservar hoy*, *te conviene tomar el subte*, *te recomiendo ir en subte*. *-ar* verbs take **e** (*llevar* → **lleves**), the rest **a** (*salgo* → **salgamos**).

  After:

  > | Te aconsejo que **lleves** efectivo. | My advice: bring cash. |
  > | Conviene que **tomes** el subte. | Best to take the subway. |
  > | Es mejor que **reserves**. | Better book ahead. |
  > | Es importante que **uses** la SUBE. | It's important to use your SUBE card. |
  > | Es mejor que **salgamos** temprano. | We'd better leave early. |
  > 
  > Advice with **que** takes the subjunctive. *-ar* verbs take **e** (*llevar* → **lleves**), the rest **a** (*salgo* → **salgamos**).
  > 
  > In everyday speech the short way wins — no *que*, just the infinitive: *te conviene reservar hoy*, *te recomiendo ir en subte*.

- `236b1e04` a-medias · Te debo, me debés

  Before:

  > **Te debo diez mil pesos** — *I owe you*; **me debés la mitad** — *you owe me half*. Friends split the bill **a medias**. A **deuda** is what you owe; if you are short, say **te pago a fin de mes** — when you get paid.

  After:

  > **Te debo diez mil pesos** — *I owe you*; **me debés la mitad** — *you owe me half*.
  > 
  > Friends split the bill **a medias**.
  > 
  > A **deuda** is what you owe; if you are short, say **te pago a fin de mes** — when you get paid.

- `719de067` a-medias · ¿Débito o crédito?

  Before:

  > The question at every register. **Crédito** usually means **cuotas**, and the good ones are **sin interés**. A **caja de ahorro** (*savings account* by name) is really the everyday bank account: your pay arrives there and your debit card uses it.

  After:

  > The question at every register. **Crédito** usually means **cuotas**, and the good ones are **sin interés**.
  > 
  > A **caja de ahorro** (*savings account* by name) is really the everyday bank account: your pay arrives there and your debit card uses it.

- `9a9f029d` a-medias · ¿Te alcanza?

  Before:

  > *No me alcanza* for you is **¿te alcanza?** — *do you have enough?*. **Cobré** — *I got paid*; **¿gastás mucho?**, **¿ahorrás algo?** — vos adds **-ás**. For everyone at work: **cobramos el lunes** — *we get paid on Monday*.

  After:

  > *No me alcanza* for you is **¿te alcanza?** — *do you have enough?*
  > 
  > **Cobré** — *I got paid*; **¿gastás mucho?**, **¿ahorrás algo?** — vos adds **-ás**.
  > 
  > For everyone at work: **cobramos el lunes** — *we get paid on Monday*.

- `29090f79` cada-vez-mas · Ya no, sigue

  Before:

  > **Ya no** is *not anymore*: **ya no vivo ahí**. Porteños say it even more with **no … más**: *no vivo más ahí*. The opposite is **sigue** — *still*: *sigue roto*, *sigue lloviendo*. **Cada vez más** — *more and more*: *cada vez más caro*; **cada vez menos** — *less and less*.

  After:

  > **Ya no** is *not anymore*: **ya no vivo ahí**. Porteños say it even more with **no … más**: *no vivo más ahí*.
  > 
  > The opposite is **sigue** — *still*: *sigue roto*, *sigue lloviendo*.
  > 
  > **Cada vez más** — *more and more*: *cada vez más caro*; **cada vez menos** — *less and less*.

- `3b867a4a` cada-vez-mas · Creció

  Before:

  > **Crecer** is *to grow* — a city, a kid, a tree: **el barrio creció**, **crecí en Rosario** — *I grew up in Rosario*. Porteños also say **me crié**: *me crié en Flores* — *I grew up in Flores*. 

  After:

  > **Crecer** is *to grow* — a city, a kid, a tree: **el barrio creció**, **crecí en Rosario** — *I grew up in Rosario*.
  > 
  > Porteños also say **me crié**: *me crié en Flores* — *I grew up in Flores*.

- `529fca7e` cada-vez-mas · Antes… ahora

  Before:

  > To say how things changed, set **antes** + the *-aba / -ía* past against **ahora** + the present: *antes era tranquilo, ahora hay mucho ruido*; *antes costaba poco, ahora es carísimo*.

  After:

  > To say what changed, set **antes** + the *-aba / -ía* past against **ahora** + the present.
  > 
  > So: *antes era tranquilo, ahora hay mucho ruido*; *antes costaba poco, ahora es carísimo*.

- `0da84ce9` como-no-dona-rosa · Don, doña

  Before:

  > The old neighbor is **don José** or **doña Rosa** — first name, with respect. A **persona mayor**, a **jubilado**, still gets **vos**, plus a **cómo no** (*of course*) and a **permiso** when you need to get past — *con permiso* exists, but porteños mostly say just **permiso**, and you **pedís permiso** before going in: *entró sin pedir permiso*.

  After:

  > The old neighbor is **don José** or **doña Rosa** — first name, with respect.
  > 
  > A **persona mayor**, a **jubilado**, still gets **vos**, plus a **cómo no** (*of course*).
  > 
  > Say **permiso** when you need to get past; *con permiso* exists, but porteños mostly say just **permiso**. You also **pedís permiso** before going in: *entró sin pedir permiso*.

- `60f3cec7` dicen-que · Dicen que, parece que

  Before:

  > **Parece que** — *it seems*, *apparently* — passes on news you are not sure of: **parece que va a llover**. *Dicen que* does the same for what people say: *dicen que va a llover todo el finde*. If it turns out to be false, **es mentira**.

  After:

  > **Parece que** — *it seems*, *apparently* — passes on news you are not sure of: **parece que va a llover**.
  > 
  > *Dicen que* does the same for what people say: *dicen que va a llover todo el finde*.
  > 
  > If it turns out to be false, **es mentira**.

- `b876ba73` en-cuotas · No me alcanza

  Before:

  > **No me alcanza** — *I don't have enough* (usually money). Word for word: *it doesn't reach for me*. **Ahorrar** is to save, **gastar** to spend: **gasté todo el sueldo**.

  After:

  > **No me alcanza** — *I don't have enough* (usually money). Word for word: *it doesn't reach for me*.
  > 
  > **Ahorrar** is to save, **gastar** to spend: **gasté todo el sueldo**.

- `6db7132b` estas-cambiado · Cambiaste, mejoró, empeoró

  Before:

  > Change, person by person: **cambiaste un montón**, **cambiaron los precios**, **el barrio mejoró**, **el servicio empeoró**. **Estás cambiado** — *you look different* — agrees like an adjective: *estás cambiada*.

  After:

  > Change, person by person: **cambiaste un montón**, **cambiaron los precios**, **el barrio mejoró**, **el servicio empeoró**.
  > 
  > **Estás cambiado** — *you look different* — agrees like an adjective: *estás cambiada*.

- `375791dd` me-contaron · ¿Escuchaste? Me enteré

  Before:

  > News starts with **¿escuchaste…?** or **¿te enteraste?** — *did you hear?*: *¿escuchaste lo de Pablo?* The answer is **me enteré** — *I found out*: *me enteré ayer*. A useful piece of news is **un dato**: *gracias por el dato*.

  After:

  > News starts with **¿escuchaste…?** or **¿te enteraste?** — *did you hear?*: *¿escuchaste lo de Pablo?*
  > 
  > The answer is **me enteré** — *I found out*: *me enteré ayer*.
  > 
  > A useful piece of news is **un dato**: *gracias por el dato*.

- `51b423d8` me-contaron · Me contaron

  Before:

  > When several people told you, or you'd rather not say who, use **me contaron que…** or **me comentaron que…**: *me contaron que te mudaste*. For one person: **Mati me comentó que renunció**. Porteño news travels by word of mouth and by *audio*, a WhatsApp voice message.

  After:

  > Use **me contaron que…** or **me comentaron que…** when several people told you, or you won't say who. So: *me contaron que te mudaste*. For one person: **Mati me comentó que renunció**.
  > 
  > News also travels by *audio*, a WhatsApp voice message.

- `f448808c` me-contaron · ¿Es cierto?

  Before:

  > Before you pass it on, ask **¿es cierto?** — *is it true?* **Cierto** changes with the noun: *la noticia es cierta*. If the answer is **es mentira** — *it's not true* — stop there. **Lo leyó en las redes** — *she read it on social media* — is not a source.

  After:

  > Before you pass it on, ask **¿es cierto?** — *is it true?* **Cierto** changes with the noun: *la noticia es cierta*.
  > 
  > If the answer is **es mentira** — *it's not true* — stop there.
  > 
  > **Lo leyó en las redes** — *she read it on social media* — is not a source.

- `8470eb4b` me-dijo-que · Te juro

  Before:

  > **Te juro** — *I swear* — is how a porteño vouches for the story: **te juro que me dijo eso**. A long talk with a friend is **una charla**: **charlé con Sofi**.

  After:

  > **Te juro** — *I swear* — is how a porteño vouches for the story: **te juro que me dijo eso**.
  > 
  > A long talk with a friend is **una charla**: **charlé con Sofi**.

- `ac8034cf` me-dijo-que · ¿Qué le preguntaste?

  Before:

  > Now the vos side: **¿qué le preguntaste?**, **¿qué le contestaste?** — the same *-aste* as *llamaste*. And for him or her: **me explicó que…**, **me juró que…**, **me gritó**.

  After:

  > Now the vos side: **¿qué le preguntaste?**, **¿qué le contestaste?** — the same *-aste* as *llamaste*.
  > 
  > And for him or her: **me explicó que…**, **me juró que…**, **me gritó**.

- `7fef5512` me-gustaria · Me gustaría

  Before:

  > **Me gustaría** — *I'd like* — is *gustar* in the conditional, with the same *me*: **me gustaría ir**, **¿te gustaría venir?**. **Me encantaría** is the enthusiastic yes. For more than one thing, **-ían**: **nos encantarían unas empanadas**. To order or offer something day to day, porteños say **quería** or **¿querés?**: *¿querés tomar unos mates?*

  After:

  > **Me gustaría** — *I'd like* — is *gustar* in the conditional, with the same *me*. Examples: **me gustaría ir**, **¿te gustaría venir?**
  > 
  > **Me encantaría** is the enthusiastic yes. For more than one thing, **-ían**: **nos encantarían unas empanadas**.
  > 
  > To order or offer, porteños say **quería** or **¿querés?**: *¿querés tomar unos mates?*

- `52d12bb1` me-pregunto · Telling what someone said

  Before:

  > | what she said | what you tell |
  > | «¿Venís?» | Me preguntó **si venía**. |
  > | «Sí.» | Le contesté **que sí**. |
  > The question comes back with **si** — *she asked me if I was coming*; the answer with **que** — *I told her yes*. What was going on slides into the imperfect: *venís* → **venía**. If it is still true now, the present can stay: *le pregunté si viene*.

  After:

  > | what she said | what you tell |
  > | «¿Venís?» | Me preguntó **si venía**. |
  > | «Sí.» | Le contesté **que sí**. |
  > 
  > The question comes back with **si** — *she asked me if I was coming*; the answer with **que** — *I told her yes*.
  > 
  > What was going on slides into the imperfect: *venís* → **venía**. If it is still true now, the present can stay: *le pregunté si viene*.

- `b1cb665e` no-llego-a-fin-de-mes · Llegar a fin de mes

  Before:

  > **Llegar a fin de mes** — *to make it to payday* — is the monthly sport: **no llego a fin de mes**. When the money runs short: **no me alcanza** — *it's not enough*. You cut the **gastos**, compare **marcas** at the **chino** (the corner supermarket), and notice when they **remarcaron** everything overnight.

  After:

  > **Llegar a fin de mes** is *to make it to payday*: **no llego a fin de mes**.
  > 
  > When the money runs short: **no me alcanza** — *it's not enough*.
  > 
  > You cut the **gastos**, compare **marcas** at the **chino** (the corner supermarket), and notice when they **remarcaron** everything overnight.

- `da398a9c` no-me-alcanza · Aumentaron, bajaron

  Before:

  > Bills come in plural, and so do the verbs: **aumentaron la luz y el gas**, **aumentan las tarifas**. The plural can also be a nameless *they*: **me aumentaron el alquiler** — *they raised my rent*. Prices that **bajaron**? Nobody remembers. Porteños keep count: *el **boleto** aumentó tres veces este año*.

  After:

  > Bills come in plural, and so do the verbs: **aumentaron la luz y el gas**, **aumentan las tarifas**.
  > 
  > The plural can also be a nameless *they*: **me aumentaron el alquiler** — *they raised my rent*.
  > 
  > Prices that **bajaron**? Nobody remembers. Porteños keep count: *el **boleto** aumentó tres veces este año*.

- `6adce778` ponele · Fillers

  Before:

  > A porteño sentence breathes through its fillers: **ponele** — *let's say*; **digamos** — *sort of*; **qué sé yo** — *I don't know*; **bah** — *well, I mean*: *son las ocho, bah, ponele* — or a shrug, *oh well*. The ones you will hear most: **o sea** — *I mean*; **tipo** — *like, around*: *es tipo a las nueve*; and **viste** at the end, for *you know*: *estaba cansado, viste*.

  After:

  > A porteño sentence is full of fillers. First: **ponele** — *let's say*; **digamos** — *sort of*; **qué sé yo** — *I don't know*.
  > 
  > Also **bah** — *well, I mean*: *son las ocho, bah, ponele* — or a shrug, *oh well*.
  > 
  > Most common: **o sea** — *I mean*; **tipo** — *like, around*: *es tipo a las nueve*; and **viste** at the end, for *you know*: *estaba cansado, viste*.

- `c2f1eb75` ponele · The words that glue a conversation

  Before:

  > **Por las dudas** — *just in case*; **de última** — *if all else fails*; **si querés** — *if you like*. Say them and you sound like you're from here.

  After:

  > **Por las dudas** — *just in case*; **de última** — *if all else fails*; **si querés** — *if you like*.

- `c6b40c9b` ponele · Yes, no, and no way

  Before:

  > **¡Más vale!** — *of course!*; **sí o sí** — *no matter what*; **ni loco** — *no way* — and a woman says **ni loca**: *¿volver con mi ex? Ni loca.* Before a verb **más vale** is *you'd better*: **más vale llevar paraguas**, **más vale que llegues temprano** (subjunctive after *que*). And when there's too much going on: **estoy a full** — *I'm really busy*.

  After:

  > **¡Más vale!** — *of course!*; **sí o sí** — *no matter what*; **ni loco** — *no way*. A woman says **ni loca**: *¿volver con mi ex? Ni loca.*
  > 
  > Before a verb **más vale** is *you'd better*: **más vale llevar paraguas**, **más vale que llegues temprano** (subjunctive after *que*).
  > 
  > When there's too much going on: **estoy a full** — *I'm really busy*.

- `9346219b` practica-a-medias · Money between friends

  Before:

  > **Me debés la mitad**, **pagamos a medias**, **¿te alcanza?**. And news between friends: **me contaron que…**, **¿es cierto?**

  After:

  > **Me debés la mitad**, **pagamos a medias**, **¿te alcanza?**
  > 
  > And news between friends: **me contaron que…**, **¿es cierto?**

- `8c3c3de9` practica-me-dijo · What people are like, and where places are

  Before:

  > People: **es medio vago**, **son re trabajadores**. Places: **quedan en zona norte**, **¿te queda cerca?** **Quedar** is also how clothes fit: **te queda bien**, **te queda chico**. **Te queda de paso** — *it's on your way*.

  After:

  > People: **es medio vago**, **son re trabajadores**. Places: **quedan en zona norte**, **¿te queda cerca?**
  > 
  > **Quedar** is also how clothes fit: **te queda bien**, **te queda chico**.
  > 
  > **Te queda de paso** — *it's on your way*.

- `cc6f4c93` practica-si-ganara · If we had

  Before:

  > **Si ganara**, **si viviera**, **si tuviéramos**: the *if* half takes *-ra*, the other half *-ía*. Politely, the *-ía* alone is enough: **¿te molestaría esperar?**

  After:

  > **Si ganara**, **si viviera**, **si tuviéramos**: the *if* half takes *-ra*, the other half *-ía*.
  > 
  > Politely, the *-ía* alone is enough: **¿te molestaría esperar?**

- `3119b315` preferiria · The polite would

  Before:

  > The conditional takes the edge off a request: *¿me das una mano?* becomes **¿me darías una mano?**, *¿me ayudás?* becomes **¿me ayudarías?**. **¿Te molestaría…?** — *would you mind…?* — works like *me gustaría*. With friends the plain *¿me ayudás?* is already polite; the **-ía** form is for bigger favors or people you don't know.

  After:

  > The conditional softens a request. So *¿me das una mano?* becomes **¿me darías una mano?**, and *¿me ayudás?* becomes **¿me ayudarías?**
  > 
  > **¿Te molestaría…?** — *would you mind…?* — works like *me gustaría*.
  > 
  > With friends the plain *¿me ayudás?* is already polite; the **-ía** form is for bigger favors or people you don't know.

- `a1ed21d8` preferiria · Suggest, and say no nicely

  Before:

  > **Podríamos ir al cine** — *we could go to the movies*. **Nos convendría salir temprano** — *we'd better leave early*. Turning it down: **preferiría ir otro día**. Saying yes: between friends it is **dale** or **obvio** — **obvio, te ayudo**. **Con gusto** is the formal one, what you hear in a shop.

  After:

  > **Podríamos ir al cine** — *we could go to the movies*. **Nos convendría salir temprano** — *we'd better leave early*.
  > 
  > Turning it down: **preferiría ir otro día**.
  > 
  > Saying yes: between friends it is **dale** or **obvio** — **obvio, te ayudo**. **Con gusto** is the formal one, what you hear in a shop.

- `8ea5b476` si-ganara · We and you

  Before:

  > Dreaming together: **si tuviéramos plata, iríamos a Bariloche** — the *we* form carries an accent, **tuviéramos**. Asking a friend: **si pudieras, ¿dónde vivirías?** — *si pudieras* is the vos form. **Ganar** is *to win* and also *to earn*: *si ganara más* — *if I earned more*.

  After:

  > Dreaming together: **si tuviéramos plata, iríamos a Bariloche**. The *we* form carries an accent, **tuviéramos**.
  > 
  > Asking a friend: **si pudieras, ¿dónde vivirías?** — *si pudieras* is the vos form.
  > 
  > **Ganar** is *to win* and also *to earn*: *si ganara más* — *if I earned more*.

- `04f23526` si-tuviera · Only after si

  Before:

  > You meet the **-ra** form (*tuviera*, *pudiera*) after *si* (if) and after *ojalá* (I wish), and almost nowhere else at this level. Mind the difference: **ojalá llueva** — *I hope it rains* (it can happen); **ojalá pudiera ir** — *I wish I could go* (it is not real).

  After:

  > The **-ra** form (*tuviera*, *pudiera*) comes after *si* (if) and after *ojalá* (I wish). At this level, almost nowhere else.
  > 
  > Mind the difference: **ojalá llueva** — *I hope it rains* (it can happen); **ojalá pudiera ir** — *I wish I could go* (it is not real).

- `787e171d` te-doy-una-mano · Dejá, faltaba más

  Before:

  > Offer help with **te doy una mano**. If they try to carry the bags themselves: **dejá, dejá**. When they thank you: **no es nada** or **faltaba más** (*don't mention it*). And they may call you **querido** or **querida**.

  After:

  > Offer help with **te doy una mano**. If they try to carry the bags themselves: **dejá, dejá**.
  > 
  > When they thank you: **no es nada** or **faltaba más** (*don't mention it*).
  > 
  > They may call you **querido** or **querida**.

- `8af67e04` todo-aumenta · Subir, bajar, cobrar

  Before:

  > Prices **suben** — or **aumentan**, same thing — and, now and then, **bajan**. **Cobrar** has two meanings. *To charge*: **¿cuánto te cobraron?** — *how much did they charge you?* And *to get paid*: **¿ya cobraste?** — *did you get paid yet?*

  After:

  > Prices **suben** — or **aumentan**, same thing — and, now and then, **bajan**.
  > 
  > **Cobrar** has two meanings. *To charge*: **¿cuánto te cobraron?** — *how much did they charge you?* And *to get paid*: **¿ya cobraste?** — *did you get paid yet?*

- `da454431` todo-aumenta · Todo aumenta

  Before:

  > In Buenos Aires prices **aumentan** so often that it is small talk: *¿viste cómo aumentó el café?* Your **aumento** — *raise* — never keeps up with the **inflación**. When something costs too much: **está por las nubes** — *sky-high*; **sale un ojo de la cara** — *it costs a fortune*.

  After:

  > In Buenos Aires prices **aumentan** so often that it is small talk: *¿viste cómo aumentó el café?*
  > 
  > Your **aumento** — *raise* — never keeps up with the **inflación**.
  > 
  > When something costs too much: **está por las nubes** — *sky-high*; **sale un ojo de la cara** — *it costs a fortune*.

- `1d87549c` usted · Quisiera, ¿no tendrás…?

  Before:

  > Day to day porteños soften a request with **quería**: **quería un turno para mañana**, **te quería pedir algo**. **Quisiera** — *I would like* — is the formal one, for the phone or an office: **quisiera un turno**; nobody orders food with it. To ask a stranger for something, add **no** and the future: **¿no tendrás hora?** — *you wouldn't happen to have the time?*

  After:

  > Porteños soften a request with **quería**: **quería un turno para mañana**, **te quería pedir algo**.
  > 
  > **Quisiera** — *I would like* — is the formal one, for the phone or an office: **quisiera un turno**. Nobody orders food with it.
  > 
  > To ask a stranger for something, add **no** and the future: **¿no tendrás hora?** — *you wouldn't happen to have the time?*

- `dda7b964` usted · Polite, still vos

  Before:

  > Porteños say **vos** to almost everyone: the shop owner, a doctor, an old man on the bus. You may still hear **usted** sometimes, but **vos** is always fine for you. The politeness lives in the words around it: **disculpá que te moleste**, **perdón por la molestia**, **muy amable**.

  After:

  > Porteños say **vos** to almost everyone: the shop owner, a doctor, an old man on the bus.
  > 
  > You may still hear **usted** sometimes, but **vos** is always fine for you.
  > 
  > The politeness lives in the words around it: **disculpá que te moleste**, **perdón por la molestia**, **muy amable**.

- `e41cacc8` usted · Softening a request

  Before:

  > | straight | softer |
  > | ¿Tenés cambio? | ¿**No tendrás** cambio? |
  > | ¿Sabés dónde queda? | ¿**No sabrás** dónde queda? |
  > | Quiero un turno. | **Quisiera** un turno. |
  > | Perdón. | **Disculpá que te moleste**. |
  > 
  > **No** plus the future turns a question into a gentle one: *you wouldn't happen to…?* After **disculpá que**, the verb takes the subjunctive: *te moleste*.

  After:

  > | straight | softer |
  > | ¿Tenés cambio? | ¿**No tendrás** cambio? |
  > | ¿Sabés dónde queda? | ¿**No sabrás** dónde queda? |
  > | Quiero un turno. | **Quisiera** un turno. |
  > | Perdón. | **Disculpá que te moleste**. |
  > 
  > **No** plus the future turns a question into a gentle one: *you wouldn't happen to…?*
  > 
  > After **disculpá que**, the verb takes the subjunctive: *te moleste*.

- `540d8ca7` ya-no-es-lo-mismo · Cada vez peor, de moda

  Before:

  > **Cada vez peor** and **cada vez mejor** — *worse and worse, better and better*. What's in is **de moda**; what's gone **desapareció**: **desaparecieron los almacenes** — *the corner shops disappeared*, and where the old house stood there's a **torre** — *a high-rise*. Ya no es **lo mismo** — *it's not the same anymore*.

  After:

  > **Cada vez peor** and **cada vez mejor** — *worse and worse, better and better*.
  > 
  > What's in is **de moda**; what's gone **desapareció**: **desaparecieron los almacenes** — *the corner shops disappeared*. Where the old house stood there's a **torre** — *a high-rise*.
  > 
  > Ya no es **lo mismo** — *it's not the same anymore*.

- `bacc1caa` yo-en-tu-lugar · Hablaría, saldría, dirías

  Before:

  > | | regular | shortened |
  > | yo, él, ella | hablar**ía**, dejar**ía**, pedir**ía**, volver**ía** | **saldría**, **tendría**, **diría** |
  > | vos | ir**ías** | **tendrías**, **dirías** |
  > | with *me* | **me quedaría** | |
  >
