  > **Yo en tu lugar, hablaría con él.** Vos adds **-s**: *¿vos qué le dirías?* **Tendrías que** is advice too: *tendrías que llamarlo*.

  After:

  > | | regular | shortened |
  > | yo, él, ella | hablar**ía**, dejar**ía**, pedir**ía**, volver**ía** | **saldría**, **tendría**, **diría** |
  > | vos | ir**ías** | **tendrías**, **dirías** |
  > | with *me* | **me quedaría** | |
  > 
  > **Yo en tu lugar, hablaría con él.** Vos adds **-s**: *¿vos qué le dirías?*
  > 
  > **Tendrías que** is advice too: *tendrías que llamarlo*.

- `f09f8f3b` yo-en-tu-lugar · Yo en tu lugar

  Before:

  > **Yo en tu lugar** — *in your place, if I were you* — means the same as *yo que vos*, a little more serious. The advice after it is still *would*: **yo en tu lugar, hablaría con él**, **yo me quedaría**.

  After:

  > **Yo en tu lugar** — *in your place, if I were you* — is like *yo que vos*, a little more serious.
  > 
  > The advice after it is still *would*: **yo en tu lugar, hablaría con él**, **yo me quedaría**.

- `0d67de33` yo-que-vos · Would: -ía

  Before:

  > | | regular | shortened |
  > | yo, él, ella | comprar**ía**, ir**ía** | **tendría**, **podría**, **haría**, **diría** |
  > | vos | ir**ías** | |
  > The ending goes on the whole infinitive: *comprar* → **compraría**. **Yo que vos** + would is advice: *yo que vos, esperaría*. **Tendría que** is *should* — *tendría que ir* — and **podría** is *could*.

  After:

  > | | regular | shortened |
  > | yo, él, ella | comprar**ía**, ir**ía** | **tendría**, **podría**, **haría**, **diría** |
  > | vos | ir**ías** | |
  > 
  > The ending goes on the whole infinitive: *comprar* → **compraría**.
  > 
  > **Yo que vos** + would is advice: *yo que vos, esperaría*. **Tendría que** is *should* — *tendría que ir* — and **podría** is *could*.

- `19d601d6` acabo-de · Just, usually, again

  Before:

  > | **Acabo de** llegar. | I just got here. |
  > | **Suelo** almorzar tarde. | I usually have lunch late. |
  > | **Volví a** perder las llaves. | I lost my keys again. |
  > 
  > All three take an infinitive. **Soler** has a past too, **solía** — *solía almorzar tarde* — but *antes almorzaba tarde* says the same.

  After:

  > | **Acabo de** llegar. | I just got here. |
  > | **Suelo** almorzar tarde. | I usually have lunch late. |
  > | **Volví a** perder las llaves. | I lost my keys again. |
  > 
  > All three take an infinitive.
  > 
  > **Soler** has a past too, **solía** — *solía almorzar tarde* — but *antes almorzaba tarde* says the same.

- `043bf160` acabo-de · Acabo de

  Before:

  > **Acabo de** + infinitive is *I just did it*: **acabo de comer**, **acaba de salir**. Porteños mostly say **recién** + the past instead: **recién llegué**, **recién salió** — it means the same. **Volví a** is *I did it again*: **volví a llamar**. Or just **de nuevo** or **otra vez** — *again*.

  After:

  > **Acabo de** + infinitive is *I just did it*: **acabo de comer**, **acaba de salir**. Porteños mostly say **recién** + the past instead: **recién llegué**, **recién salió**.
  > 
  > **Volví a** is *I did it again*: **volví a llamar**. Or just **de nuevo** or **otra vez** — *again*.

- `d578e8dc` acabo-de · Suelo

  Before:

  > **Soler** is *to usually*: **suelo almorzar tarde** — *I usually have lunch late*; **¿solés venir acá?** It only ever comes with an infinitive. In speech porteños more often say **generalmente** or **casi siempre**: **generalmente almuerzo tarde**.

  After:

  > **Soler** is *to usually*: **suelo almorzar tarde** — *I usually have lunch late*; **¿solés venir acá?** It only comes with an infinitive.
  > 
  > In speech porteños more often say **generalmente** or **casi siempre**: **generalmente almuerzo tarde**.

- `c7048a9e` costumbres · Late, mate and the tip

  Before:

  > Nobody arrives on time: **si te dicen a las nueve, llegás a las nueve y media**. In the mate round **gracias** means *no more for me*: **si decís gracias, no tomás más**. Men greet each other with a kiss too. And **la propina** is about ten per cent, in cash.

  After:

  > Nobody arrives on time: **si te dicen a las nueve, llegás a las nueve y media**.
  > 
  > In the mate round **gracias** means *no more for me*: **si decís gracias, no tomás más**.
  > 
  > Men greet each other with a kiss too. And **la propina** is about ten per cent, in cash.

- `d2d06eea` costumbres · La previa, la sobremesa

  Before:

  > **La previa** is the drinks before going out; **la sobremesa** is the long chat at the table after eating, which nobody cuts short.

  After:

  > **La previa** is the drinks before going out.
  > 
  > After eating comes **la sobremesa**: the long chat at the table.

- `92568dab` de-acuerdo · De acuerdo

  Before:

  > **Estoy de acuerdo** — *I agree*. Disagreeing: **no estoy de acuerdo**, or a flat **para nada** — *not at all*. In between: **depende** — *it depends*; **en parte** — *partly*.

  After:

  > **Estoy de acuerdo** — *I agree*. Disagreeing: **no estoy de acuerdo**, or a flat **para nada** — *not at all*.
  > 
  > In between: **depende** — *it depends*; **en parte** — *partly*.

- `b617a7a0` de-acuerdo · Softening it

  Before:

  > A porteño argues hard and softens it: **puede ser, pero…** — *could be, but…* **Puede ser que** + the subjunctive is *maybe*: **puede ser que tengas razón** — *you might be right*; **puede ser que llueva**.

  After:

  > Porteños soften an argument with **puede ser, pero…** — *could be, but…*
  > 
  > **Puede ser que** + the subjunctive is *maybe*: **puede ser que tengas razón** — *you might be right*; **puede ser que llueva**.

- `1ff21937` deje-de-fumar · Dejé de, volví a

  Before:

  > **Dejar de** + infinitive is *to stop, to quit*: **dejé de fumar**, **¿dejaste de ir al gimnasio?**, **¡dejá de quejarte!** Its opposite is **volver a**: **volvió a fumar** — *he started smoking again*. **Ponerse a** is *to start doing*: **me puse a estudiar** — *I started studying*.

  After:

  > **Dejar de** + infinitive is *to stop, to quit*: **dejé de fumar**, **¿dejaste de ir al gimnasio?**, **¡dejá de quejarte!**
  > 
  > Its opposite is **volver a**: **volvió a fumar** — *he started smoking again*.
  > 
  > **Ponerse a** is *to start doing*: **me puse a estudiar** — *I started studying*.

- `559eee63` deje-de-fumar · How often?

  Before:

  > **De vez en cuando** is *every now and then*: **fumo de vez en cuando**. **Casi nunca** is *hardly ever*: **casi nunca manejo**. Both can be a whole answer: *¿Salís mucho? — De vez en cuando.* Your **rutina** is what you do every day.

  After:

  > **De vez en cuando** is *every now and then*: **fumo de vez en cuando**. **Casi nunca** is *hardly ever*: **casi nunca manejo**.
  > 
  > Both can be a whole answer: *¿Salís mucho? — De vez en cuando.*
  > 
  > Your **rutina** is what you do every day.

- `4b021c65` el-cajero · Se me bloqueó

  Before:

  > The bank has its own accidents: **se me bloqueó la tarjeta**, **se me venció la tarjeta**. Same *se me* as a dropped glass. How it happens: **puse mal la clave tres veces** — *I entered the wrong PIN three times*.

  After:

  > The bank has its own accidents: **se me bloqueó la tarjeta**, **se me venció la tarjeta**. Same *se me* as a dropped glass.
  > 
  > How it happens: **puse mal la clave tres veces** — *I entered the wrong PIN three times*.

- `71a97b7b` el-cajero · Transferir

  Before:

  > **Te transfiero** — *I'll transfer it to you*; with vos, **¿me transferís?** The past: **te transferí**, **¿me transferiste?** — but él changes to **transfirió**. Asking for it: **transferime**.

  After:

  > **Te transfiero** — *I'll transfer it to you*; with vos, **¿me transferís?** Asking for it: **transferime**.
  > 
  > The past: **te transferí**, **¿me transferiste?** — but él changes to **transfirió**.

- `bf96bee5` el-cajero · El cajero y el alias

  Before:

  > The ATM is **el cajero**: **voy al cajero a sacar plata**. Paying a friend goes by **transferencia** to their **alias** — a few words instead of an account number: **¿a qué alias transferiste?** After it: **¿me mandás el comprobante?** The **sucursal** is the bank branch; a **comisión** is the fee a cajero charges; **las cuentas** are your bills, or your bank accounts.

  After:

  > The ATM is **el cajero**: **voy al cajero a sacar plata**.
  > 
  > Paying a friend goes by **transferencia** to their **alias** — a few words instead of an account number: **¿a qué alias transferiste?** Then: **¿me mandás el comprobante?**
  > 
  > The **sucursal** is the bank branch; a **comisión** is the fee a cajero charges; **las cuentas** are your bills, or your bank accounts.

- `91a5e921` el-consorcio · Las expensas

  Before:

  > Living in a building means paying **expensas** — *the monthly building fees* — to the **consorcio** — *the owners' association*. The **encargado** is the building caretaker, and knows everything.

  After:

  > Living in a building means paying **expensas** — *the monthly building fees* — to the **consorcio** — *the owners' association*.
  > 
  > The **encargado** is the building caretaker.

- `d6e4060c` el-consorcio · El calefón

  Before:

  > The **calefón** heats the water, and breaks in July. When there's a **pérdida** — *a leak* — you call the **plomero** to **arreglar** it.

  After:

  > The **calefón** heats the water.
  > 
  > When there's a **pérdida** — *a leak* — you call the **plomero** to **arreglar** it.

- `5efffc1b` el-tecnico · Anda, andan, anduvo

  Before:

  > One machine **anda**, two **andan**: **no andan las pilas**. The past is irregular: **ayer anduvo bien** — *it worked fine yesterday*. **Funciona**, **funcionan** mean the same and are just as common. For *how* something works, only *funcionar*: **¿cómo funciona este control?**

  After:

  > One machine **anda**, two **andan**: **no andan las pilas**. The past is irregular: **ayer anduvo bien** — *it worked fine yesterday*.
  > 
  > **Funciona**, **funcionan** mean the same and are just as common. For *how* something works, only *funcionar*: **¿cómo funciona este control?**

- `026bd560` le-metio-los-cuernos · Los cuernos

  Before:

  > People say **la engañó** — *he cheated on her* — and in gossip even more often **le metió los cuernos**, literally *put the horns on*. **Engañar** is also plain *to trick, to fool*.

  After:

  > People say **la engañó** — *he cheated on her*. In gossip, more often **le metió los cuernos**, literally *put the horns on*.
  > 
  > **Engañar** is also plain *to trick, to fool*.

- `c9bc18db` me-dan-asco · ¿Te dio miedo?

  Before:

  > In the story it's the past: **¿te dio miedo?** — **no, me dio risa**. **Me da vergüenza** — *I'm embarrassed* — is built the same way.

  After:

  > In the story it's the past: **¿te dio miedo?** — **no, me dio risa**.
  > 
  > **Me da vergüenza** — *I'm embarrassed* — is built the same way.

- `c3650361` me-lo-podes-arreglar · Arreglar

  Before:

  > **¿Me lo podés arreglar?** — *can you fix it for me?* After the **técnico** comes: **lo arregló en cinco minutos** — *he fixed it in five minutes*. If he never comes, **hacés un reclamo** — *you file a complaint* — and **nos quejamos** all summer. The questions for the técnico: **¿tiene arreglo?** — *can it be fixed?*; **¿cuánto me sale el arreglo?**; **¿cuándo podés venir?**

  After:

  > **¿Me lo podés arreglar?** — *can you fix it for me?* After the **técnico** comes: **lo arregló en cinco minutos** — *he fixed it in five minutes*.
  > 
  > If he never comes, **hacés un reclamo** — *you file a complaint* — and **nos quejamos** all summer.
  > 
  > Questions for him: **¿tiene arreglo?** — *can it be fixed?*; **¿cuánto me sale el arreglo?**; **¿cuándo podés venir?**

- `f4296b31` me-pico-un-mosquito · The summer visitor

  Before:

  > A hot night, a **cucaracha** in the kitchen, and it **vuela**: **grité**, **pegué un salto** — more natural than a bare *salté* — and in the end **la maté** with an **ojota**. Every porteño has this story. The other summer visitor is the **mosquito**: **me picó un mosquito**, so nobody goes out without **repelente**.

  After:

  > A hot night, a **cucaracha** in the kitchen, and it **vuela**: **grité**, **pegué un salto** — more natural than a bare *salté* — and in the end **la maté** with an **ojota**.
  > 
  > The other summer visitor is the **mosquito**: **me picó un mosquito**, so nobody goes out without **repelente**.

- `abc22894` nada-que-ver · Picking a side

  Before:

  > All the way with you: **exacto**, **totalmente**, **obvio**, **sin duda** — and the stiffer **coincido**. All the way against: **al contrario**, **no creo**, **estás equivocado**. And for the friend who goes too far: **¡no exageres!**

  After:

  > All the way with you: **exacto**, **totalmente**, **obvio**, **sin duda** — and the stiffer **coincido**.
  > 
  > All the way against: **al contrario**, **no creo**, **estás equivocado**.
  > 
  > For the friend who goes too far: **¡no exageres!**

- `17c1c9e2` no-anda · No anda

  Before:

  > For machines, porteños say **anda** — *it works*: **no anda el wifi**, **¿anda el ascensor?**. *Funciona* means the same and is just as common. The other ways things fail: **se rompió**, **está roto**, **no prende** — *it won't turn on*; **se colgó** — *it froze*; **pierde agua** — *it's leaking*.

  After:

  > For machines, porteños say **anda** — *it works*: **no anda el wifi**, **¿anda el ascensor?**. *Funciona* means the same and is just as common.
  > 
  > Other ways things fail: **se rompió**, **está roto**, **no prende** — *it won't turn on*; **se colgó** — *it froze*; **pierde agua** — *it's leaking*.

- `c011bb73` no-anda · Quejarse

  Before:

  > **Me quejé** — *I complained*; **¿de qué te quejás?** — *what are you complaining about?* **Reclamar** is to file a complaint, and something you do a lot with a phone company.

  After:

  > **Me quejé** — *I complained*; **¿de qué te quejás?** — *what are you complaining about?*
  > 
  > **Reclamar** is to file a complaint, as with a phone company.

- `cba630fb` no-sabes-lo-que-me-contaron · The reactions

  Before:

  > **¡No me digas!** and **¡callate!** both mean *no way!* — between friends *callate* isn't rude. **¡Mirá vos!** is milder: *wow, really?* By the afternoon **todo el mundo** knows, thanks to the **chusma**: the person who loves gossip.

  After:

  > **¡No me digas!** and **¡callate!** both mean *no way!* — between friends *callate* isn't rude. **¡Mirá vos!** is milder: *wow, really?*
  > 
  > By the afternoon **todo el mundo** knows, thanks to the **chusma**: the person who loves gossip.

- `457aa31e` no-sabes-lo-que-me-contaron · Lo que

  Before:

  > **Lo que** is *what* when it means *the thing that*: **¿viste lo que pasó?**, **contame lo que pasó**. It is how every chisme starts: **no sabés lo que me contaron** — *you won't believe what they told me*. A direct question keeps **qué**: *¿qué pasó?*

  After:

  > **Lo que** is *what* when it means *the thing that*: **¿viste lo que pasó?**, **contame lo que pasó**. Every chisme starts with it: **no sabés lo que me contaron** — *you won't believe what they told me*.
  > 
  > A direct question keeps **qué**: *¿qué pasó?*

- `78c655d8` nunca-habia · Nobody told me

  Before:

  > **No me habían avisado** — *nobody had told me*; **ya lo había llamado** — *I'd already called him*. One more irregular participle: *escribir* → **escrito**.

  After:

  > **No me habían avisado** — *nobody had told me*; **ya lo había llamado** — *I'd already called him*.
  > 
  > One more irregular participle: *escribir* → **escrito**.

- `196e6740` nunca-habia · Nunca había + -ado, -ido

  Before:

  > | yo, él, ella | **había** | probado, estado, empezado |
  > | vos | **habías** | terminado, escuchado |
  > | nosotros | **habíamos** | llamado, avisado |
  > | ellos | **habían** | **escrito**, hecho, dicho, visto |
  > 
  > Any participle goes with any person: **habíamos probado**, **habías escrito**. **Nunca había probado algo así** — never, up to a moment in the past. **Ya habíamos terminado cuando llegaste** — done before the other thing happened.

  After:

  > | yo, él, ella | **había** | probado, estado, empezado |
  > | vos | **habías** | terminado, escuchado |
  > | nosotros | **habíamos** | llamado, avisado |
  > | ellos | **habían** | **escrito**, hecho, dicho, visto |
  > 
  > Any participle goes with any person: **habíamos probado**, **habías escrito**.
  > 
  > **Nunca había probado algo así** — never, up to a moment in the past. **Ya habíamos terminado cuando llegaste** — done before the other thing happened.

- `480771b2` nunca-habia · Nunca había…

  Before:

  > A first time is told with **nunca había** + a participle: **nunca había probado el dulce de leche** — *I'd never tried dulce de leche* (until that day). **Por primera vez** is the other half: **lo probé por primera vez en Buenos Aires**.

  After:

  > A first time is told with **nunca había** + a participle: **nunca había probado el dulce de leche** — *I'd never tried dulce de leche*.
  > 
  > **Por primera vez** goes with it: **lo probé por primera vez en Buenos Aires**.

- `6448cf46` para-que · Antes de que

  Before:

  > **Antes de que llegue** — *before he gets here* — always takes the subjunctive: it hasn't happened yet. Same person on both sides: **antes de** + infinitive — *antes de salir*.

  After:

  > **Antes de que llegue** — *before he gets here* — always takes the subjunctive: it hasn't happened yet.
  > 
  > Same person on both sides: **antes de** + infinitive — *antes de salir*.

- `4288460e` para-que-entres · The stems you know

  Before:

  > The subjunctive keeps the *yo* stem: *cierro* → **cierres**, *encuentro* → **encuentres**, *riego* → **riegues** (the **u** is only spelling: it keeps the *g* hard). The regular ones just swap the vowel: **entres**, **abras**.

  After:

  > The subjunctive keeps the *yo* stem: *cierro* → **cierres**, *encuentro* → **encuentres**, *riego* → **riegues**. The **u** is only spelling: it keeps the *g* hard.
  > 
  > The regular ones just swap the vowel: **entres**, **abras**.

- `a46e4610` para-que-entres · While I'm away

  Before:

  > Going on vacation? Every instruction is a **para que** + what the *other* person does: **te dejo la llave para que entres**, **para que riegues las plantas**, **para que el gato coma**. Two people, so the second verb is subjunctive.

  After:

  > Every instruction is a **para que** + what the *other* person does: **te dejo la llave para que entres**, **para que riegues las plantas**, **para que el gato coma**.
  > 
  > Two people, so the second verb is subjunctive.

- `cc050468` practica-nunca-habia · Para que, nunca había

  Before:

  > **Te dejo la llave para que entres** — someone else's action takes the subjunctive. **Nunca había probado algo así** — one past before another.

  After:

  > **Te dejo la llave para que entres** — someone else's action takes the subjunctive.
  > 
  > **Nunca había probado algo así** — one past before another.

- `ce78c3e8` practica-nunca-habia · Se me, se nos

  Before:

  > The verb agrees with the thing: **se me cayó la taza**, **se me cayeron las llaves**. The middle word is who it happened to: **se nos acabó el café**.

  After:

  > The verb agrees with the thing: **se me cayó la taza**, **se me cayeron las llaves**.
  > 
  > The middle word is who it happened to: **se nos acabó el café**.

- `7bc7d559` practica-nunca-habia · Me olvidé or se me pasó?

  Before:

  > A thing left behind is **me olvidé**: **me olvidé las llaves**. Something you did not remember to do is **se me pasó**: **se me pasó llamar a mi vieja** — or **me olvidé de llamar**.

  After:

  > A thing left behind is **me olvidé**: **me olvidé las llaves**.
  > 
  > Something you did not remember to do is **se me pasó**: **se me pasó llamar a mi vieja** — or **me olvidé de llamar**.

- `d236343e` practica-tenes-razon · Ganaron, anduvo

  Before:

  > *They* in the past: **ganaron**, **perdieron**, **empataron**. The machine that let you down: **ayer anduvo**, **hoy no anda**.

  After:

  > *They* in the past: **ganaron**, **perdieron**, **empataron**.
  > 
  > The machine that let you down: **ayer anduvo**, **hoy no anda**.

- `d74aeb37` practica-tenes-razon · Para mí

  Before:

  > **Para mí**, **me parece que**, **coincido**, **al contrario** — the whole argument. *Opino que* is for TV panels: at the table it is **para mí, el árbitro estuvo mal**. And the line that ends it: **¡no exageres!** For advice, **tendrías que** — *you should*: **tendrías que ir al médico**.

  After:

  > **Para mí**, **me parece que**, **coincido**, **al contrario** — the whole argument. *Opino que* is for TV panels: at the table it is **para mí, el árbitro estuvo mal**.
  > 
  > The line that ends it: **¡no exageres!**
  > 
  > For advice, **tendrías que** — *you should*: **tendrías que ir al médico**.

- `605b9c23` practica-un-aplauso · Me dan

  Before:

  > **Me da asco la cucaracha**, **me dan asco las cucarachas** — the verb follows the thing. In the past: **¿te dio miedo?** — **no, me dio risa**.

  After:

  > **Me da asco la cucaracha**, **me dan asco las cucarachas** — the verb follows the thing.
  > 
  > In the past: **¿te dio miedo?** — **no, me dio risa**.

- `94d127e4` que-susto · Me dio miedo

  Before:

  > **Me da miedo** — *it scares me* — works like *gustar*; in a story it's **me dio miedo**. The reaction afterwards: **¡qué susto!**

  After:

  > **Me da miedo** — *it scares me* — works like *gustar*; in a story it's **me dio miedo**.
  > 
  > The reaction afterwards: **¡qué susto!**

- `c3471351` que-susto · Telling it

  Before:

  > A scare story has its set pieces: **de repente** — *suddenly*; **me pegué un susto** — *I got such a fright*; **salí corriendo** — *I ran off* (more natural than a bare *corrí*); **casi me muero**; **pensé que era un ladrón**; and the relief: **al final no era nada**.

  After:

  > A scare story has its set pieces: **de repente** — *suddenly*; **me pegué un susto** — *I got such a fright*; **salí corriendo** — *I ran off* (more natural than a bare *corrí*).
  > 
  > Then: **casi me muero**; **pensé que era un ladrón**.
  > 
  > The relief: **al final no era nada**.

- `79c02546` se-aplaude-al-asador · Se: what everyone does at an asado

  Before:

  > | **Se trae** algo. | You bring something. |
  > | **Se pone** la mesa. | The table gets set. |
  > | **Se brinda** con todos. | Everyone toasts together. |
  > | **Se aplaude** al asador. | Everyone applauds the grill master. |
  > 
  > **Se** + the *él* form: no subject, because it's everybody. *Acá se come tarde* works the same way. As a guest you say **llevar**: **¿qué llevo?** — *what should I bring?* The host, already there, says **traer**: **¿quién trae el vino?**

  After:

  > | **Se trae** algo. | You bring something. |
  > | **Se pone** la mesa. | The table gets set. |
  > | **Se brinda** con todos. | Everyone toasts together. |
  > | **Se aplaude** al asador. | Everyone applauds the grill master. |
  > 
  > **Se** + the *él* form: no subject, because it's everybody. *Acá se come tarde* works the same way.
  > 
  > As a guest you say **llevar**: **¿qué llevo?** — *what should I bring?* The host, already there, says **traer**: **¿quién trae el vino?**

- `742f77df` se-aplaude-al-asador · Un aplauso para el asador

  Before:

  > When the meat reaches the table, **se aplaude al asador**: *¡un aplauso para el asador!* **Se brinda** with everyone at the table. Leaving without saying goodbye to each person? Very **maleducado**. And **los ñoquis del 29**: on the 29th of each month people eat gnocchi, with money under the plate for luck.

  After:

  > When the meat reaches the table, **se aplaude al asador**: *¡un aplauso para el asador!* **Se brinda** with everyone at the table.
  > 
  > Leaving without saying goodbye to each person is **maleducado**.
  > 
  > Also **los ñoquis del 29**: on the 29th of each month people eat gnocchi, with money under the plate for luck.

- `6f7cea1b` se-me-cayo · It fell on me

  Before:

  > Spanish tells accidents as if they happened *to* you: **se me cayó** — *I dropped it* (it fell, and it happened to me); **se me rompió** — *it broke on me*; **se me pasó** — *I forgot* (it slipped my mind). Nobody is to blame, which is the point.

  After:

  > Spanish tells accidents as if they happened *to* you: **se me cayó** — *I dropped it*. Nobody is to blame.
  > 
  > More: **se me rompió** — *it broke on me*; **se me pasó** — *I forgot*.

- `aa612ce1` se-me-quemo · Se me quemó, se me quemaron

  Before:

  > | | one thing | more than one |
  > | to me | **se me** quemó la comida | **se me** quemaron las tostadas |
  > | to you | **se te** quemó | **se te** quemaron |
  > | to us | **se nos** quemó | **se nos** quemaron |
  > 
  > The verb follows the thing: one thing **-ó**, more than one **-aron / -eron**. The middle word says who it happened to: **se me**, **se te**, **se le**, **se nos**.

  After:

  > | | one thing | more than one |
  > | to me | **se me** quemó la comida | **se me** quemaron las tostadas |
  > | to you | **se te** quemó | **se te** quemaron |
  > | to us | **se nos** quemó | **se nos** quemaron |
  > 
  > The verb follows the thing: one thing **-ó**, more than one **-aron / -eron**.
  > 
  > The middle word says who it happened to: **se me**, **se te**, **se le**, **se nos**.

- `22f82d78` se-me-quemo · Nobody's fault

  Before:

  > **Se me** + verb says it happened *to* you, by accident — you didn't do it on purpose: **no fue mi culpa, se me cayó** — *it wasn't my fault, I dropped it*. And the excuse every porteño has ready when they're late: **se me hizo tarde** — *it got late*.

  After:

  > **Se me** + verb says it happened *to* you, by accident: **no fue mi culpa, se me cayó** — *it wasn't my fault, I dropped it*.
  > 
  > The excuse for being late: **se me hizo tarde** — *it got late*.

- `5c59e9dc` se-tapo-la-pileta · Se tapó, se trabó

  Before:

  > Things at home break on their own, with **se**: **se tapó el inodoro** — *the toilet's clogged*; **se trabó la puerta** — *the door got stuck* (an elevator stuck between floors **se quedó**: *se quedó el ascensor*); **se cortó el agua**. Nobody did it — it just **se rompió**. **La pileta** is the swimming pool and also the kitchen sink — **la pileta de la cocina**, or **la bacha**.

  After:

  > Things at home break on their own, with **se**. Nobody did it: it **se rompió**. Like **se tapó el inodoro** — *the toilet's clogged*; **se trabó la puerta** — *the door got stuck*; **se cortó el agua**.
  > 
  > An elevator stuck between floors **se quedó**: *se quedó el ascensor*.
  > 
  > **La pileta** is the swimming pool and also the kitchen sink — **la pileta de la cocina**, or **la bacha**.

- `e7c5b8b2` se-tapo-la-pileta · ¿A quién llamo?

  Before:

  > Each problem has its person: the **plomero** for a **canilla** that **gotea** — *a dripping faucet* — the **gasista** for the calefón, the **encargado** for the **portero eléctrico**. A **filtración** from the apartment upstairs goes to the **administración** — *the management office* — and takes months. A toilet or a faucet that leaks **pierde agua**: **el inodoro pierde agua**.

  After:

  > Each problem has its person. The **plomero** fixes a **canilla** that **gotea** — *a dripping faucet*; the **gasista**, the calefón; the **encargado**, the **portero eléctrico**.
  > 
  > A **filtración** from the apartment upstairs goes to the **administración** — *the management office*.
  > 
  > A toilet or a faucet that leaks **pierde agua**: **el inodoro pierde agua**.

- `4bffbc84` sin-que-se-escape · Para que, sin que

  Before:

  > | yo form | para que… (vos) |
  > | entro | para que **entres** |
  > | abro | para que **abras** |
  > | cierro | para que **cierres** |
  > | encuentro | para que **encuentres** |
  > | riego | para que **riegues** |
  > 
  > For *él, ella*: **para que el gato coma**. **Sin que** and **antes de que** take the same forms: *sin que se escape*, *antes de que vuelva*.

  After:

  > | yo form | para que… (vos) |
  > | entro | para que **entres** |
  > | abro | para que **abras** |
  > | cierro | para que **cierres** |
  > | encuentro | para que **encuentres** |
  > | riego | para que **riegues** |
  > 
  > For *él, ella*: **para que el gato coma**.
  > 
  > **Sin que** and **antes de que** take the same forms: *sin que se escape*, *antes de que vuelva*.

- `02a86818` tenes-razon · ¿Vos qué opinás?

  Before:

  > **Opinar** is having a view: **¿vos qué opinás?** — *what do you think?*; **opino lo mismo**. But **yo opino que…** sounds formal, like a TV panel: in daily speech porteños start with **para mí** or **me parece que**: **para mí, depende del tema**; **me parece que tenés razón**.

  After:

  > **Opinar** is having a view: **¿vos qué opinás?** — *what do you think?*; **opino lo mismo**.
  > 
  > But **yo opino que…** sounds formal. In daily speech porteños start with **para mí** or **me parece que**: **para mí, depende del tema**; **me parece que tenés razón**.

- `1ce38771` ya-habia · Había + -ado, -ido

  Before:

  > One past behind another: **había** + a participle. **Cuando llegué, ya habían comido** — *when I got there, they'd already eaten*. Yo, él and ella take **había**; vos, **habías**; ellos, **habían**. Pronouns go in front: **ya se había ido**, **no me habían dicho nada**. *Hecho, dicho, visto* are the irregular ones.

  After:

  > One past behind another: **había** + a participle. **Cuando llegué, ya habían comido** — *when I got there, they'd already eaten*.
  > 
  > Yo, él and ella take **había**; vos, **habías**; ellos, **habían**.
  > 
  > Pronouns go in front: **ya se había ido**, **no me habían dicho nada**.

- `867bb841` aunque-llueva · Igual, a pesar de

  Before:

  > Porteños close the thought with **igual** — *anyway*: *aunque esté cansada, voy igual*; *gracias igual* — *thanks anyway*. **De todos modos** says the same but sounds written: *salí de todos modos*. **A pesar de** takes a noun or an infinitive: *a pesar del frío*.

  After:

  > Porteños close the thought with **igual** — *anyway*: *aunque esté cansada, voy igual*; *gracias igual* — *thanks anyway*.
  > 
  > **De todos modos** says the same but sounds written: *salí de todos modos*.
  > 
  > **A pesar de** takes a noun or an infinitive: *a pesar del frío*.

- `84182bc5` aunque-llueva · ¿Te sirve?

  Before:

  > **Servir** is *to be of use*, and it works like *gustar*: **¿te sirven?** — *can you use them?*; **sí, me sirve** — *that works for me*. Things that are no good **no sirven**: **estas zapatillas no sirven**.

  After:

  > **Servir** is *to be of use*, and it works like *gustar*: **¿te sirven?** — *can you use them?*; **sí, me sirve** — *that works for me*.
  > 
  > Things that are no good **no sirven**: **estas zapatillas no sirven**.

- `63fad49e` aunque-llueva · Aunque + fact, aunque + maybe

  Before:

  > | a fact — normal verb | a maybe — subjunctive |
  > | Aunque **llueve**, vamos. | Aunque **llueva**, vamos. |
  > | Aunque **cuesta**, sigo. | Aunque **cueste**, sigo. |
  > | Aunque **estoy** cansado, voy. | Aunque **esté** cansado, voy. |
  > 
  > Left: it *is* raining. Right: whether or not it rains. **Igual** at the end — *anyway* — often closes it.

  After:

  > | a fact — normal verb | a maybe — subjunctive |
  > | Aunque **llueve**, vamos. | Aunque **llueva**, vamos. |
  > | Aunque **cuesta**, sigo. | Aunque **cueste**, sigo. |
  > | Aunque **estoy** cansado, voy. | Aunque **esté** cansado, voy. |
  > 
  > Left: it *is* raining. Right: whether or not it rains.
  > 
  > **Igual** at the end — *anyway* — often closes it.

- `2df58fe2` aunque-no-tenga-ganas · Por más que

  Before:

  > **Por más que** — *no matter how much* — is a stronger *aunque*: **por más que me cueste, sigo** — *however hard it is, I keep going*. A maybe takes the subjunctive, a fact doesn't: *por más que **entreno**, engordo* — I do train; it just doesn't show.

  After:

  > **Por más que** — *no matter how much* — is a stronger *aunque*: **por más que me cueste, sigo** — *however hard it is, I keep going*.
  > 
  > A maybe takes the subjunctive, a fact doesn't: *por más que **entreno**, engordo* — I do train; it just doesn't show.

- `cf806c31` como-si-nada · Se hace el vivo

  Before:

  > **Hacerse el** + a describing word is *to act, to play*: **se hace el vivo** — *he acts all clever*; **se hizo el distraído** — *he pretended not to notice*. For a woman it's **la**: **se hace la tonta**, **se hizo la distraída**.

  After:

  > **Hacerse el** + a describing word is *to act, to play*: **se hace el vivo** — *he acts all clever*; **se hizo el distraído** — *he pretended not to notice*.
  > 
  > For a woman it's **la**: **se hace la tonta**, **se hizo la distraída**.

- `d953b996` como-si-nada · Como si + past subjunctive

  Before:

  > | Habla **como si supiera** todo. | He talks as if he knew everything. |
  > | **Como si fuera** fácil. | As if it were easy. |
  > | Me saludó **como si nada**. | He said hi as if nothing had happened. |
  > | ¡**Ni que fuera** tan difícil! | It's not like it's that hard! |
  > 
  > **Ni que…** is a complaint: you are saying the thing is NOT true. After **como si** and **ni que**, always the *-ra* form, whatever the time.

  After:

  > | Habla **como si supiera** todo. | He talks as if he knew everything. |
  > | **Como si fuera** fácil. | As if it were easy. |
  > | Me saludó **como si nada**. | He said hi as if nothing had happened. |
  > | ¡**Ni que fuera** tan difícil! | It's not like it's that hard! |
  > 
  > **Ni que…** is a complaint: you are saying the thing is NOT true.
  > 
  > After **como si** and **ni que**, always the *-ra* form, whatever the time.

- `6fb95bc7` conoces-a-alguien-que · De confianza

  Before:

  > In Buenos Aires you don't google an electrician, you ask around: **¿conocés a alguien de confianza?** Same for a flatmate: *busco a alguien **ordenado**, que no **fume** y que **comparta** los gastos*.

  After:

  > In Buenos Aires you don't google an electrician, you ask around: **¿conocés a alguien de confianza?**
  > 
  > Same for a flatmate: *busco a alguien **ordenado**, que no **fume** y que **comparta** los gastos*.

- `81b69635` conoces-a-alguien-que · No hay nadie que…

  Before:

  > Nobody, nothing, no one at all — always the subjunctive, because it doesn't exist: **no conozco a nadie que hable inglés**, **no hay ningún electricista que venga el domingo**. **Algún, ningún** drop the *-o* before a masculine noun (*algún bar*, *ningún electricista*); **alguna, ninguna** for feminine ones.

  After:

  > Nobody, nothing, no one at all — always the subjunctive, because it doesn't exist: **no conozco a nadie que hable inglés**, **no hay ningún electricista que venga el domingo**.
  > 
  > **Algún, ningún** drop the *-o* before a masculine noun (*algún bar*, *ningún electricista*); **alguna, ninguna** for feminine ones.

- `d4f3c5be` el-de-la-vidriera · El de Lucía, el de rojo

  Before:

  > **El de** is also for owners and people. With a name it is *someone's*: **el de Lucía** — *Lucía's*; **las de Santi** — *Santi's*. With people it is *the guy in, the people from*: **el de rojo** — *the guy in red*; **los de arriba** — *the people upstairs*.

  After:

  > **El de** is also for owners and people. With a name it is *someone's*: **el de Lucía** — *Lucía's*; **las de Santi** — *Santi's*.
  > 
  > With people it is *the guy in, the people from*: **el de rojo** — *the guy in red*; **los de arriba** — *the people upstairs*.

- `320367c4` el-de-la-vidriera · El de, el que

  Before:

  > | the one + a place or a detail | **el de** la vidriera, **la de** la izquierda |
  > | the one + a verb | **el que** quieras, **la que** te probaste |
  > | more than one | **los de** allá, **las que** prefieras |
  > 
  > Not chosen yet, so the subjunctive: **el que elijas**; to a group, **elijan los que quieran**. Don't mind? **Como quieras.**

  After:

  > | the one + a place or a detail | **el de** la vidriera, **la de** la izquierda |
  > | the one + a verb | **el que** quieras, **la que** te probaste |
  > | more than one | **los de** allá, **las que** prefieras |
  > 
  > Not chosen yet, so the subjunctive: **el que elijas**; to a group, **elijan los que quieran**.
  > 
  > Don't mind? **Como quieras.**

- `5ca0fdb9` la-ventaja-es-que · Además, sobre todo

  Before:

  > To stack up reasons: **además** — *besides* — adds one more; **sobre todo** — *above all* — picks the one that counts: *me gusta, **sobre todo** por la gente*. Porteños also say **más que nada** — *mainly*.

  After:

  > To stack up reasons: **además** — *besides* — adds one more; **sobre todo** — *above all* — picks the one that counts: *me gusta, **sobre todo** por la gente*.
  > 
  > Porteños also say **más que nada** — *mainly*.

- `65ee37e5` la-ventaja-es-que · Por lo menos, la verdad

  Before:

  > **Por lo menos** — *at least* — finds the good side: *pagan poco, pero **por lo menos** queda cerca*. **La verdad** — *honestly* — says what you really think: ***la verdad**, no me conviene*. **Conviene** works like *gustar*: **me conviene** — *it's better for me*; **te conviene llevar paraguas** — *it's a good idea to take an umbrella*.

  After:

  > **Por lo menos** — *at least* — finds the good side: *pagan poco, pero **por lo menos** queda cerca*.
  > 
  > **La verdad** — *honestly* — says what you really think: ***la verdad**, no me conviene*.
  > 
  > **Conviene** works like *gustar*: **me conviene** — *it's better for me*; **te conviene llevar paraguas** — *it's a good idea to take an umbrella*.

- `75167017` llevo-dos-anos · Sigo, seguís: still

  Before:

  > **Seguir** + *-ando / -iendo* is *still doing* or *keep doing*: **sigo laburando ahí**, **¿seguís viviendo en San Telmo?** With a noun or adjective it is just *still*: **sigo cansado**, **¿seguís con fiebre?** **Seguir sin** + infinitive is *still not*: **¿seguís sin fumar?**

  After:

  > **Seguir** + *-ando / -iendo* is *still doing* or *keep doing*: **sigo laburando ahí**, **¿seguís viviendo en San Telmo?**
  > 
  > With a noun or adjective it is just *still*: **sigo cansado**, **¿seguís con fiebre?**
  > 
  > **Seguir sin** + infinitive is *still not*: **¿seguís sin fumar?**

- `28e10253` llevo-dos-anos · How long: llevar, hace… que

  Before:

  > **Hace un año que vivo acá** — *I've been living here for a year*: Spanish stays in the present. **Llevar** + time + *-ando / -iendo* says the same: **llevo un año viviendo acá**. Or just a place: **lleva un año acá**. With **sin**: **llevás tres días sin dormir**. To ask: **¿hace cuánto que vivís acá?** Day to day porteños say **hace… que**.

  After:

  > **Hace un año que vivo acá** — *I've been living here for a year*: Spanish stays in the present. To ask: **¿hace cuánto que vivís acá?**
  > 
  > **Llevar** + time + *-ando / -iendo* says the same: **llevo un año viviendo acá**. Or just a place: **lleva un año acá**. With **sin**: **llevás tres días sin dormir**.
  > 
  > Day to day porteños say **hace… que**.

- `f4e91a43` mis-viejos-querian · Pedir permiso

  Before:

  > **Estricto, estricta** is *strict*. *Para salir tenía que pedir **permiso*** — *to go out I had to ask permission*. *Mi vieja **quería que volviera** temprano* — *my mom wanted me back early*.

  After:

  > **Estricto, estricta** is *strict*.
  > 
  > *Para salir tenía que pedir **permiso*** — *to go out I had to ask permission*. *Mi vieja **quería que volviera** temprano* — *my mom wanted me back early*.

- `dc66155e` mis-viejos-querian · Querían que estudiara

  Before:

  > | ellos (preterite) | yo, él, ella | vos |
  > | salieron | **saliera** | **salieras** |
  > | dijeron | **dijera** | **dijeras** |
  > | fueron | **fuera** | **fueras** |
  > | vinieron | **viniera** | **vinieras** · ellos **vinieran** |
  > 
  > Swap **-ron** for **-ra** and it works for every verb: *estudiar* → **estudiara, estudiaras**. **Fuera** is both *ser* and *ir*: *quería que fueras feliz* (be), *quería que fueras al asado* (go). **Mis viejos querían que estudiara una carrera** — the wish is in the past, so the second verb is too.

  After:

  > | ellos (preterite) | yo, él, ella | vos |
  > | salieron | **saliera** | **salieras** |
  > | dijeron | **dijera** | **dijeras** |
  > | fueron | **fuera** | **fueras** |
  > | vinieron | **viniera** | **vinieras** · ellos **vinieran** |
  > 
  > **Mis viejos querían que estudiara una carrera** — the wish is in the past, so the second verb is too. Swap **-ron** for **-ra** and it works for every verb: *estudiar* → **estudiara, estudiaras**.
  > 
  > **Fuera** is both *ser* and *ir*: *quería que fueras feliz* (be), *quería que fueras al asado* (go).

- `1257cd1c` no-seas-cabeza-dura · ¡Nada que ver!

  Before:

  > An argument in Buenos Aires is a sport, not a fight: **discutimos** a lot and stay friends. To reject a point, say **¡nada que ver!** — *not at all!* (it can also mean *nothing alike* or *nothing to do with it*). If the other person won't listen: **no seas cabeza dura** — *don't be so stubborn*.

  After:

  > An argument in Buenos Aires is not a fight: **discutimos** a lot and stay friends.
  > 
  > To reject a point, say **¡nada que ver!** — *not at all!* It can also mean *nothing alike* or *nothing to do with it*.
  > 
  > If the other person won't listen: **no seas cabeza dura** — *don't be so stubborn*.

- `8e0594ac` no-te-hagas-el-gil · ¡Qué caradura!

  Before:

  > A **caradura** has no shame: never pays, asks for too much, lies and doesn't feel bad about it. **¡Qué caradura!** — *you've got some nerve!* Between friends it's often a joke.

  After:

  > A **caradura** has no shame: never pays, asks for too much, lies and doesn't feel bad about it.
  > 
  > **¡Qué caradura!** — *you've got some nerve!* Between friends it's often a joke.

- `c22a6a2e` no-te-hagas-el-gil · Como si no pasara nada

  Before:

  > After **como si** and **ni que**, always the *-ra* form: **como si no pasara nada** — *as if nothing were happening*; **como si no me viera** — *as if he didn't see me*; **¡ni que fueras mi viejo!** — *it's not like you're my dad!* But **hace como que** takes a normal verb: *hace como que no le importa* — *he acts like he doesn't care*.

  After:

  > After **como si** and **ni que**, always the *-ra* form: **como si no pasara nada** — *as if nothing were happening*; **como si no me viera** — *as if he didn't see me*; **¡ni que fueras mi viejo!** — *it's not like you're my dad!*
  > 
  > But **hace como que** takes a normal verb: *hace como que no le importa* — *he acts like he doesn't care*.

- `3e245af1` no-te-hagas-el-gil · Hacerse el

  Before:

  > **Hacerse el** + a describing word is *to pretend, to act*: **me hice** el distraído — *I pretended not to notice*; **te hacés** el sordo — *you act like you can't hear*; **se hace** el canchero — *he shows off, he acts all cool*. **No te hagas el gil** — *don't play dumb*. A woman says **la**: *me hice **la** simpática*.

  After:

  > **Hacerse el** + a describing word is *to pretend, to act*: **me hice** el distraído — *I pretended not to notice*; **te hacés** el sordo — *you act like you can't hear*; **se hace** el canchero — *he shows off, he acts all cool*.
  > 
  > **No te hagas el gil** — *don't play dumb*.
  > 
  > A woman says **la**: *me hice **la** simpática*.

- `4cb250cf` por-un-lado · Por un lado, por otro lado

  Before:

  > **Por un lado… por otro lado** weighs two sides. **En cambio** — *but, on the other hand* — sets two people or things against each other: *yo laburo, en cambio él no*.

  After:

  > **Por un lado… por otro lado** weighs two sides.
  > 
  > **En cambio** — *but, on the other hand* — sets two people or things against each other: *yo laburo, en cambio él no*.

- `b2d04c3b` por-un-lado · De hecho, en realidad

  Before:

  > **De hecho** — *in fact* — backs up what you just said: *no es caro; de hecho, es barato*. **En realidad** — *actually* — corrects it: *en realidad no es tan caro*. **Por eso** — *that's why*: *está cerca, por eso me gusta*.

  After:

  > **De hecho** — *in fact* — backs up what you just said: *no es caro; de hecho, es barato*.
  > 
  > **En realidad** — *actually* — corrects it: *en realidad no es tan caro*.
  > 
  > **Por eso** — *that's why*: *está cerca, por eso me gusta*.

- `d0bec188` por-un-lado · Para mí

  Before:

  > To give your view, porteños say **para mí** or **me parece que**: *para mí conviene esperar*; *me parece que es caro*. **¿Qué opinás?** is the natural question; **opino que…** as an answer sounds stiff. **No me convence** — *I'm not sure about it* — works like *gustar*.

  After:

  > To give your view, porteños say **para mí** or **me parece que**: *para mí conviene esperar*; *me parece que es caro*.
  > 
  > **¿Qué opinás?** is the natural question; **opino que…** as an answer sounds stiff.
  > 
  > **No me convence** — *I'm not sure about it* — works like *gustar*.

- `3f1a2506` practica-a-la-mesa · Como si, el de, hacerse el

  Before:

  > **Como si no me viera** — *as if he didn't see me*. **El de la vidriera** — *the one in the window*; **el que quieras** — *whichever you want*. And pretending: **te hacés el dormido** — *you're pretending to be asleep*.

  After:

  > **Como si no me viera** — *as if he didn't see me*.
  > 
  > **El de la vidriera** — *the one in the window*; **el que quieras** — *whichever you want*.
  > 
  > And pretending: **te hacés el dormido** — *you're pretending to be asleep*.

- `d5688bae` practica-a-la-mesa · Coman, no se olviden

  Before:

  > For a group: **coman**, **prueben**, **sírvanse**, **no se olviden**. The *no* doesn't change the verb, only where the pronoun goes: **siéntense** → **no se sienten**; **sírvanse** → **no se sirvan**.

  After:

  > For a group: **coman**, **prueben**, **sírvanse**, **no se olviden**.
  > 
  > The *no* doesn't change the verb, only where the pronoun goes: **siéntense** → **no se sienten**; **sírvanse** → **no se sirvan**.

- `85402fa0` practica-aunque-sea · ¿Hace cuánto?

  Before:

  > **Hace un año que estudio** says the same as **llevo un año estudiando**. **Seguimos buscando** — *we're still looking*; **volví a empezar** — *I started again*.

  After:

  > **Hace un año que estudio** says the same as **llevo un año estudiando**.
  > 
  > **Seguimos buscando** — *we're still looking*; **volví a empezar** — *I started again*.

- `df9106c3` practica-aunque-sea · Querían que, por más que

  Before:

  > **Mis viejos querían que estudiara** — the wish is in the past, so the second verb is too. **Por más que me cueste** — however hard it is. Something not sure or not real yet takes the subjunctive: *por más que llueva, vamos igual*. A fact takes the normal verb: *por más que ahorro, no me alcanza*.

  After:

  > **Mis viejos querían que estudiara** — the wish is in the past, so the second verb is too.
  > 
  > **Por más que me cueste** — however hard it is. Something not sure or not real yet takes the subjunctive: *por más que llueva, vamos igual*. A fact takes the normal verb: *por más que ahorro, no me alcanza*.

- `74f663ec` queria-que-vinieras · Quería que vinieras

  Before:

  > The second verb follows the first into the past:
  > 
  > | now | then |
  > | Quiero que **vengas**. | Quería que **vinieras**. |
  > | Te pido que **llames**. | Te pedí que **llamaras**. |
  > | Me dice que **espere**. | Me dijo que **esperara**. |
  > 
  > The past subjunctive is the *ellos* past with **-ra**: *vinieron* → **viniera**, *hicieron* → **hiciera**. With vos: **vinieras, hicieras, llamaras**. The *yo* and *él/ella* forms are the same: **quería que viniera** — *wanted me / him / her to come*.

  After:

  > The second verb follows the first into the past:
  > 
  > | now | then |
  > | Quiero que **vengas**. | Quería que **vinieras**. |
  > | Te pido que **llames**. | Te pedí que **llamaras**. |
  > | Me dice que **espere**. | Me dijo que **esperara**. |
  > 
  > The past subjunctive is the *ellos* past with **-ra**: *vinieron* → **viniera**, *hicieron* → **hiciera**. With vos: **vinieras, hicieras, llamaras**.
  > 
  > The *yo* and *él/ella* forms are the same: **quería que viniera** — *wanted me / him / her to come*.

- `058b9be3` si-hubiera-sabido · Si hubiera + participle

  Before:

  > | yo, él, ella | **hubiera** | sabido, venido, ido |
  > | vos | **hubieras** | llamado, avisado, pensado |
  > 
  > For what didn't happen, both halves: **Si hubiera sabido, te hubiera avisado.** *If I'd known, I'd have told you.* You will also hear **habría** in the second half: *te habría avisado*. Same meaning. Compare *si supiera* — if I knew (now).

  After:

  > | yo, él, ella | **hubiera** | sabido, venido, ido |
  > | vos | **hubieras** | llamado, avisado, pensado |
  > 
  > For what didn't happen, both halves: **Si hubiera sabido, te hubiera avisado.** *If I'd known, I'd have told you.* Compare *si supiera* — if I knew (now).
  > 
  > You will also hear **habría** in the second half: *te habría avisado*. Same meaning.

- `ff92a735` si-hubiera-sabido · The same participles

  Before:

  > **Hubiera** takes the participles *había* does: **sabido, venido, ido, llamado, dicho, hecho**. **Si hubieras venido** — *if you had come*. On its own it's *would have* — *yo hubiera dicho que sí*, *I would have said yes* — or regret, *should have*: **ojalá hubiera estudiado** — *I wish I'd studied*; **¡me hubieras llamado!** — *you should have called me!*

  After:

  > **Hubiera** takes the participles *había* does: **sabido, venido, ido, llamado, dicho, hecho**. **Si hubieras venido** — *if you had come*.
  > 
  > On its own it's *would have*: *yo hubiera dicho que sí*, *I would have said yes*.
  > 
  > Or regret, *should have*: **ojalá hubiera estudiado** — *I wish I'd studied*; **¡me hubieras llamado!** — *you should have called me!*

- `70cedfaf` si-hubieramos-salido · More participles

  Before:

  > -ar verbs take **-ado**, the rest **-ido**: **tomado, reservado, quedado, perdido, conseguido**. *Traer* gives **traído**, with an accent. *Si hubiera tomado un taxi…*, *si me hubiera quedado en casa…*

  After:

  > -ar verbs take **-ado**, the rest **-ido**: **tomado, reservado, quedado, perdido, conseguido**. *Traer* gives **traído**, with an accent.
  > 
  > *Si hubiera tomado un taxi…*, *si me hubiera quedado en casa…*

- `d338d3db` si-hubieramos-salido · Qué mala suerte

  Before:

  > When nobody's to blame: **¡qué mala suerte!** And at Ezeiza, the international airport, the board says what every porteño expects: the flight is **demorado** — *delayed*.

  After:

  > When nobody's to blame: **¡qué mala suerte!**
  > 
  > At Ezeiza, the international airport, the board says the flight is **demorado** — *delayed*.

- `3a35b239` te-doy-la-razon · A ver, pensándolo bien

  Before:

  > **A ver** — *let's see* — slows an argument down. **Pensándolo bien** — *on second thought* — is how you change sides: *pensándolo bien, me convenciste* — *on second thought, you convinced me*. To heat it up again: **sos un exagerado** — *you're being dramatic*.

  After:

  > **A ver** — *let's see* — slows an argument down.
  > 
  > **Pensándolo bien** — *on second thought* — is how you change sides: *pensándolo bien, me convenciste* — *on second thought, you convinced me*.
  > 
  > To heat it up again: **sos un exagerado** — *you're being dramatic*.

- `b93ba3b3` te-doy-la-razon · Me equivoqué

  Before:

  > The hardest words in any argument: **me equivoqué** — *I was wrong*. To a friend: **te equivocás** — *you're wrong*. To admit it: **reconozco que** tenés razón — *I admit you're right* — or just **te doy la razón** — *you're right*. **En eso** te doy la razón — *you're right about that* — admits only one point.

  After:

  > The hardest words in an argument: **me equivoqué** — *I was wrong*. To a friend: **te equivocás** — *you're wrong*.
  > 
  > To admit it: **reconozco que** tenés razón — *I admit you're right* — or just **te doy la razón** — *you're right*.
  > 
  > **En eso** te doy la razón — *you're right about that* — admits only one point.

- `b52a18e2` un-depto-que-tenga · La garantía

  Before:

  > To rent in Buenos Aires you need a **garantía** — *a guarantor*, usually a relative who owns property: *mi papá me sale de garantía* — *my dad is my guarantor*. No garantía? Foreigners often pay a **seguro de caución** — *rent guarantee insurance* — or take an **alquiler temporario amoblado** — *a furnished short-term rental*.

  After:

  > To rent in Buenos Aires you need a **garantía** — *a guarantor*, usually a relative who owns property: *mi papá me sale de garantía* — *my dad is my guarantor*.
  > 
  > No garantía? Foreigners often pay a **seguro de caución** — *rent guarantee insurance* — or take an **alquiler temporario amoblado** — *a furnished short-term rental*.

- `68fde42c` a-menos-que · Por si

  Before:

  > Unlike *a menos que*, **por si** — *just in case* — keeps the normal verb. It's what people say for everyday precautions: **llevá paraguas por si llueve**; *te dejo las llaves por si llegás antes*. Short version: **por las dudas** — *llevá paraguas, por las dudas*.

  After:

  > Unlike *a menos que*, **por si** — *just in case* — keeps the normal verb: **llevá paraguas por si llueve**; *te dejo las llaves por si llegás antes*.
  > 
  > Short version: **por las dudas** — *llevá paraguas, por las dudas*.

- `bbdfc70e` ando-buscando · Voy conociendo, se va llenando

  Before:

  > With **ir**, it grows bit by bit: **voy conociendo gente**, **el bondi se va llenando**, **me voy acostumbrando al ritmo**. It's the verb of settling in. Careful: in **andá llenando la pava**, *andá* is the command of *ir* (go): *start filling the kettle*.

  After:

  > With **ir**, it grows bit by bit: **voy conociendo gente**, **el bondi se va llenando**, **me voy acostumbrando al ritmo**.
  > 
  > Careful: in **andá llenando la pava**, *andá* is the command of *ir* (go): *start filling the kettle*.

- `ba67d83b` animate · Estaría bueno que…

  Before:

  > When you say who should do it, use **que** + subjunctive: **estaría bueno que te tomes unos días** — *it'd be good if you took a few days off*. For people in general, the infinitive: *estaría bueno **descansar***. And the push: **¡animate!** — *go for it!*

  After:

  > When you say who should do it, use **que** + subjunctive: **estaría bueno que te tomes unos días** — *it'd be good if you took a few days off*. For people in general, the infinitive: *estaría bueno **descansar***.
  > 
  > And the push: **¡animate!** — *go for it!*

- `6044efe3` caiste · ¡Caíste!

  Before:

  > When someone believes your joke: **¡caíste!** — *got you!* — and **qué inocente** — *you're so easy to trick*. If they complain too much: **estás exagerando** — *you're overdoing it*. **Te hacés el vivo** — *you're trying to be clever* — is for someone who tries to take advantage of others.

  After:

  > When someone believes your joke: **¡caíste!** — *got you!* — and **qué inocente** — *you're so easy to trick*.
  > 
  > If they complain too much: **estás exagerando** — *you're overdoing it*.
  > 
  > **Te hacés el vivo** — *you're trying to be clever* — is for someone who tries to take advantage of others.

- `a3dfd806` caiste · Se calentó, apodos

  Before:

  > If someone **se calentó** — *got mad* — or **se ofendió** — *took offense* — the joke went too far. **Se picó**: a game or an argument got heated. An **apodo** (*nickname*) like **el Gordo** or **el Pelado** is friendly among friends; never use one with someone you just met.

  After:

  > If someone **se calentó** — *got mad* — or **se ofendió** — *took offense* — the joke went too far. **Se picó**: a game or an argument got heated.
  > 
  > An **apodo** (*nickname*) like **el Gordo** or **el Pelado** is friendly among friends; never use one with someone you just met.

- `7d0442f0` con-tal-de-que · Que no lo choques

  Before:

  > When someone lends you something, the conditions come fast: *te lo presto, con tal de que lo **cuides**, no lo **choques** y me lo **devuelvas entero***. The warning at the end is **si no** — *otherwise*: *si no, no te presto nada más*.

  After:

  > When someone lends you something, the conditions come fast: *te lo presto, con tal de que lo **cuides**, no lo **choques** y me lo **devuelvas entero***.
  > 
  > The warning at the end is **si no** — *otherwise*: *si no, no te presto nada más*.

- `a59cf449` con-tal-de-que · Con tal de que, salvo que

  Before:

  > | Te lo presto, **con tal de que** me lo **devuelvas**. | on condition that |
  > | Te lo presto, **salvo que** lo **necesite**. | unless |
  > | Llevá el auto, **siempre y cuando** no lo **choques**. | as long as |
  > | Llevá plata **por si** la **necesitás**. | just in case |
  > 
  > Every condition takes the subjunctive, except **por si**. For *vos*: **devuelvas, rompas, cuides, manejes, choques**.

  After:

  > | Te lo presto, **con tal de que** me lo **devuelvas**. | on condition that |
  > | Te lo presto, **salvo que** lo **necesite**. | unless |
  > | Llevá el auto, **siempre y cuando** no lo **choques**. | as long as |
  > | Llevá plata **por si** la **necesitás**. | just in case |
  > 
  > Every condition takes the subjunctive, except **por si**.
  > 
  > For *vos*: **devuelvas, rompas, cuides, manejes, choques**.

- `4f42bca0` deberias · Soft advice

  Before:

  > | **Deberías** descansar. | You should rest. |
  > | **Debería** llamarla. | I should call her. |
  > | **Estaría bueno** juntarnos. | It'd be nice to get together. |
  > | **Habría que** avisarle. | We ought to let him know. |
  > 
  > All four can take an infinitive. **Estaría bueno** — the softest porteño suggestion — also takes **que** + subjunctive: *estaría bueno que vengas*. *Tenés que* is stronger — *you have to*; *deberías* is advice.

  After:

  > | **Deberías** descansar. | You should rest. |
  > | **Debería** llamarla. | I should call her. |
  > | **Estaría bueno** juntarnos. | It'd be nice to get together. |
  > | **Habría que** avisarle. | We ought to let him know. |
  > 
  > All four can take an infinitive. **Estaría bueno** — the softest porteño suggestion — also takes **que** + subjunctive: *estaría bueno que vengas*.
  > 
  > *Tenés que* is stronger — *you have to*; *deberías* is advice.

- `a03e7504` deberias · Tendrías que, yo que vos

  Before:

  > Day to day porteños give advice with **tendrías que** more than with *deberías*: **tendrías que descansar**. Also **yo que vos** — *if I were you*: *yo que vos, iría al médico*; or a question: **¿por qué no vas al médico?**

  After:

  > Day to day porteños give advice with **tendrías que** more than with *deberías*: **tendrías que descansar**.
  > 
  > Also **yo que vos** — *if I were you*: *yo que vos, iría al médico*.
  > 
  > Or a question: **¿por qué no vas al médico?**

- `5aaa801b` dijo-que-pasaria · Me aseguró que…

  Before:

  > To stress a promise, porteños say **me aseguró que…** — *he assured me* — or **me juró que…** — *he swore*. Then **me puso una excusa** — *he gave me an excuse* — and you learn not to **confiar en** (*trust*) the **mentiroso** (*liar*).

  After:

  > To stress a promise, porteños say **me aseguró que…** — *he assured me* — or **me juró que…** — *he swore*.
  > 
  > Then **me puso una excusa** — *he gave me an excuse* — and you learn not to **confiar en** (*trust*) the **mentiroso** (*liar*).

- `3960dc2b` dijo-que-vendria · Me prometiste

  Before:

  > **Me prometiste que ibas a venir** — *you promised me you'd come* (more careful: *que vendrías*). And the classic ending: **al final no vino** — *in the end he didn't come*. If he did, he **cumplió** — *kept his word*: *prometió llamar y cumplió*.

  After:

  > **Me prometiste que ibas a venir** — *you promised me you'd come* (more careful: *que vendrías*).
  > 
  > The ending: **al final no vino** — *in the end he didn't come*.
  > 
  > If he did, he **cumplió** — *kept his word*: *prometió llamar y cumplió*.

- `89ceed83` dijo-que-vendria · Dijo que iba a venir

  Before:

  > Reporting a promise: «voy» → **dijo que iba a venir**; «te llamo» → **dijo que me iba a llamar**. That is what porteños say day to day. In writing and in careful speech the same thing goes into *would*: **dijo que vendría**, **dijo que me llamaría** — learn to recognize it. Even shorter in fast speech: **dijo que me llamaba** — *he said he'd call me*.

  After:

  > Reporting a promise: «voy» → **dijo que iba a venir**; «te llamo» → **dijo que me iba a llamar**. That is what porteños say day to day.
  > 
  > In writing and in careful speech it goes into *would*: **dijo que vendría**, **dijo que me llamaría** — learn to recognize it.
  > 
  > Even shorter in fast speech: **dijo que me llamaba** — *he said he'd call me*.

- `361c790d` es-re-rata · Rata, careta, grasa

  Before:

  > People get labels: a **rata** never pays; a **careta** is fake or uptight (or someone who doesn't drink or party); **grasa** is tacky (a snobbish word: fine as a joke about a shirt, rude about a person). **Estar manija** is to be very excited about something: **estoy manija con el recital**. The **cana** is the police. A friend who backs you **te hace la gamba**; one who talks nonsense **manda fruta**.

  After:

  > People get labels: a **rata** never pays; a **careta** is fake or uptight (or someone who doesn't drink or party); **grasa** is tacky — fine as a joke about a shirt, rude about a person.
  > 
  > **Estar manija** is to be very excited about something: **estoy manija con el recital**. The **cana** is the police.
  > 
  > A friend who backs you **te hace la gamba**; one who talks nonsense **manda fruta**.

- `18957af7` ese-chabon · Trucho, zarpado

  Before:

  > **Trucho** — *fake, dodgy*: *un billete trucho*. **Zarpado** — *over the top*, for good or bad. **Bardo** — *a mess, trouble*: *se armó un bardo*.

  After:

  > **Trucho** — *fake, dodgy*: *un billete trucho*.
  > 
  > **Zarpado** — *over the top*, for good or bad.
  > 
  > **Bardo** — *a mess, trouble*: *se armó un bardo*.

- `c3c9a208` ese-chabon · Chabón, mina

  Before:

  > A **chabón** is a guy, a **mina** a woman — among friends; both sound rude to strangers. Someone great at what they do is a **groso**.

  After:

  > A **chabón** is a guy, a **mina** a woman — among friends; both sound rude to strangers.
  > 
  > Someone great at what they do is a **groso**.

- `1db93231` estoy-al-horno · Al horno, al pedo

  Before:

  > **Estoy al horno** — *I'm in big trouble*. **Qué bajón** — *what a shame*. **Al pedo** — *for nothing, doing nothing*: **fui al pedo**, **estoy al pedo**. And the flat *no way*: **ni en pedo**. The other *pedo* phrases: **estar en pedo** — *to be drunk*; **de pedo** — *by pure luck*: *llegué de pedo*. Among friends only.

  After:

  > **Estoy al horno** — *I'm in big trouble*. **Qué bajón** — *what a shame*.
  > 
  > **Al pedo** — *for nothing, doing nothing*: **fui al pedo**, **estoy al pedo**.
  > 
  > Other *pedo* phrases, among friends only: **ni en pedo**, the flat *no way*; **estar en pedo** — *to be drunk*; **de pedo** — *by pure luck*: *llegué de pedo*.

- `94b56b2f` me-da-bronca · Me da + a feeling

  Before:

  > | Me da **bronca**. | It makes me mad. |
  > | Me da **vergüenza**. | I'm embarrassed. |
  > | Me da **lástima**. | I feel sorry (for him). |
  > | Me da **igual**. | I don't care. |
  > 
  > The cause follows **que** in the subjunctive: **me da bronca que no avise**. In the past, **me dio**: *me dio vergüenza*.

  After:

  > | Me da **bronca**. | It makes me mad. |
  > | Me da **vergüenza**. | I'm embarrassed. |
  > | Me da **lástima**. | I feel sorry (for him). |
  > | Me da **igual**. | I don't care. |
  > 
  > The cause follows **que** in the subjunctive: **me da bronca que no avise**.
  > 
  > In the past, **me dio**: *me dio vergüenza*.

- `598bffd2` me-estas-cargando · ¿Me estás cargando?

  Before:

  > **Cargar** someone is *to tease, to joke with someone*: **¿me estás cargando?** — *are you joking with me?* Porteños **cargan** the people they like; being left alone is the bad sign.

  After:

  > **Cargar** someone is *to tease, to joke with someone*: **¿me estás cargando?** — *are you joking with me?*
  > 
  > Porteños **cargan** the people they like.

- `e7590664` me-estas-cargando · En joda

  Before:

  > **Lo dije en joda** — *I said it as a joke*. **Tomar el pelo** is the same game as cargar: **¿me estás tomando el pelo?** — *are you joking with me?* **¿Me estás jodiendo?** is very casual: friends only. A **broma** is a prank, or something not said seriously.

  After:

  > **Lo dije en joda** — *I said it as a joke*. A **broma** is a prank, or something not said seriously.
  > 
  > **Tomar el pelo** is the same game as cargar: **¿me estás tomando el pelo?** — *are you joking with me?*
  > 
  > **¿Me estás jodiendo?** is very casual: friends only.

- `71b73b1e` me-pone-nervioso-que · Me pone, me enoja, me alegra

  Before:

  > | Me **pone nervioso** que llegue tarde. | It annoys me when he's late. |
  > | Me **enoja** que me **mienta**. | It makes me angry that he lies to me. |
  > | Me **alegra** que vengas. | I'm glad you're coming. |
  > | Me da bronca que **cancele**. | It makes me mad that he cancels. |
  > 
  > Feeling first, then **que** + subjunctive. **Nervioso** here is often *annoyed* or *tense*, not only *nervous*. If it's you doing it, no *que*: *me enoja **llegar** tarde*.

  After:

  > | Me **pone nervioso** que llegue tarde. | It annoys me when he's late. |
  > | Me **enoja** que me **mienta**. | It makes me angry that he lies to me. |
  > | Me **alegra** que vengas. | I'm glad you're coming. |
  > | Me da bronca que **cancele**. | It makes me mad that he cancels. |
  > 
  > Feeling first, then **que** + subjunctive. If it's you doing it, no *que*: *me enoja **llegar** tarde*.
  > 
  > **Nervioso** here is often *annoyed* or *tense*, not only *nervous*.

- `b1156beb` me-pone-nervioso-que · Me clavó el visto

  Before:

  > The porteño complaint of the WhatsApp age: **me clavó el visto** — *he read my message and didn't answer*. And the friend who forgets everything is **re colgado**.

  After:

  > A WhatsApp complaint: **me clavó el visto** — *he read my message and didn't answer*.
  > 
  > The friend who forgets everything is **re colgado**.

- `8b11e00f` ni-ahi · Ni ahí

  Before:

  > **Ni ahí** — *no way, not even close* — is a strong porteño no, also to a question like **¿estás cansado?**. **Tal cual** — *exactly* — is how they agree.

  After:

  > **Ni ahí** — *no way, not even close* — is a strong porteño no, also to a question like **¿estás cansado?**.
  > 
  > **Tal cual** — *exactly* — is how they agree.

- `6f7a19a0` ni-ahi · Es cualquiera

  Before:

  > **Es cualquiera** — *that's nonsense*; **dijo cualquiera** — *he talked nonsense*; **hizo cualquiera** — *he did something stupid*. **Flasheaste** — *you're imagining things*. And a **¡qué lindo!** can be sincere, or ironic when the rain starts.

  After:

  > **Es cualquiera** — *that's nonsense*; **dijo cualquiera** — *he talked nonsense*; **hizo cualquiera** — *he did something stupid*.
  > 
  > **Flasheaste** — *you're imagining things*.
  > 
  > A **¡qué lindo!** can be sincere, or ironic when the rain starts.

- `da8de45b` no-rompas · Swearing at people

  Before:

  > These hurt. **Pelotudo** is stronger than *boludo*; only close friends say it as a joke. A **forro** is a nasty person. An **hinchapelotas** won't stop bothering you: *¡no rompas las bolas!* — often just *¡no rompas!* **Andá a cagar** is *go to hell*: a joke between friends, a fight with a stranger. **Hijo de puta** is one of the strongest insults — but shouted at a great goal it is admiration.

  After:

  > These hurt. **Pelotudo** is stronger than *boludo*; only close friends say it as a joke. A **forro** is a nasty person.
  > 
  > An **hinchapelotas** won't stop bothering you: *¡no rompas las bolas!* — often just *¡no rompas!* **Andá a cagar** is *go to hell*: a joke between friends, a fight with a stranger.
  > 
  > **Hijo de puta** is one of the strongest insults — but shouted at a great goal it is admiration.

- `33981f7b` practica-alguien-que-sepa · Alguien que, nadie que

  Before:

  > **¿Conocés a alguien que arregle bicis?** — don't know if they exist, subjunctive. **No hay nadie que cocine así** — they don't, subjunctive again. **Me pone nervioso que cancele** — a feeling about it, subjunctive too.

  After:

  > **¿Conocés a alguien que arregle bicis?** — don't know if they exist, subjunctive.
  > 
  > **No hay nadie que cocine así** — they don't, subjunctive again.
  > 
  > **Me pone nervioso que cancele** — a feeling about it, subjunctive too.

- `bfd29eb6` practica-alguien-que-sepa · Deberíamos, estaría bueno que

  Before:

  > **Deberíamos juntarnos**, **deberían pagarte más**, **estaría bueno que te tomes unos días**. And when they're scared: **¡animate!**

  After:

  > **Deberíamos juntarnos**, **deberían pagarte más**, **estaría bueno que te tomes unos días**.
  > 
  > And when they're scared: **¡animate!**

- `09c42605` practica-no-lo-aguanto · Ando buscando, le cae bien

  Before:

  > **Ando buscando laburo** — *I've been looking for work*: **ando** + *-ando* is these days. **Estamos por arrancar** — *we're about to start*. **Le cae bien a todo el mundo** — *everybody likes him*; **a ese no lo aguanto** — *I can't stand that guy*.

  After:

  > **Ando buscando laburo** — *I've been looking for work*: **ando** + *-ando* is these days.
  > 
  > **Estamos por arrancar** — *we're about to start*.
  > 
  > **Le cae bien a todo el mundo** — *everybody likes him*; **a ese no lo aguanto** — *I can't stand that guy*.

- `97c5fbb3` practica-no-lo-aguanto · Aumentaron la luz

  Before:

  > The small talk of bills: **aumentaron la luz y el gas** — *electricity and gas went up*; **el boleto subió** — *the fare went up*; **no llego a fin de mes** — *I can't make ends meet*: the money runs out before the month does.

  After:

  > The small talk of bills: **aumentaron la luz y el gas** — *electricity and gas went up*; **el boleto subió** — *the fare went up*.
  > 
  > Also **no llego a fin de mes** — *I can't make ends meet*.

- `2d79110f` practica-se-aceptan-tarjetas · Con tal de que, se aceptan

  Before:

  > **Con tal de que me lo devuelvas** — *as long as you give it back* — and **salvo que lo necesite** — *unless I need it* — take the subjunctive. On the door: **se aceptan tarjetas**, **se hacen envíos**.

  After:

  > **Con tal de que me lo devuelvas** — *as long as you give it back* — and **salvo que lo necesite** — *unless I need it* — take the subjunctive.
  > 
  > On the door: **se aceptan tarjetas**, **se hacen envíos**.

- `bf2dc98a` practica-se-aceptan-tarjetas · Pasaría, devolvería

  Before:

  > What he swore he'd do, told later — the everyday way: **dijo que iba a pasar**, **me aseguró que me lo iba a devolver**. More careful, and in writing: **dijo que pasaría**, **me lo devolvería**. Two have a changed stem: *poner* → **pondría**, *venir* → **vendría**.

  After:

  > What he swore he'd do, told later — the everyday way: **dijo que iba a pasar**, **me aseguró que me lo iba a devolver**.
  > 
  > More careful, and in writing: **dijo que pasaría**, **me lo devolvería**.
  > 
  > Two have a changed stem: *poner* → **pondría**, *venir* → **vendría**.

- `312fb178` puteadas · Swearing at things

  Before:

  > Most swearing is aimed at the situation, not at a person: **¡la puta madre!**, **¡la concha de la lora!**, **¡mierda!**, **¡carajo!**, **¡qué cagada!** A bad day is *un día de mierda*. **Me chupa un huevo** is *I don't give a shit*. Two are not insults, just very informal: **una boludez** is something silly or small, and **me cagué de risa** means *I laughed my ass off* — I laughed a lot.

  After:

  > Most swearing is aimed at the situation, not at a person: **¡la puta madre!**, **¡la concha de la lora!**, **¡mierda!**, **¡carajo!**, **¡qué cagada!**
  > 
  > A bad day is *un día de mierda*. **Me chupa un huevo** is *I don't give a shit*.
  > 
  > Two are not insults, just very informal: **una boludez** is something silly or small, and **me cagué de risa** means *I laughed my ass off*.

- `c84b6903` que-novedad · Justo hoy

  Before:

  > **Justo** makes a complaint: **justo hoy** — *today of all days*. And **ya fue** — *forget it, it's done* — closes the subject: *¿no vino? Ya fue.*

  After:

  > **Justo** makes a complaint: **justo hoy** — *today of all days*.
  > 
  > And **ya fue** — *forget it, it's done* — closes the subject: *¿no vino? Ya fue.*

- `bb2b8f8c` que-novedad · Sí, claro

  Before:

  > Said slowly, **sí, claro** means *yeah, right*. **¡Qué novedad!** — *what a surprise* — is for the bondi that's late again; **¡lo que faltaba!** — *that's all we needed* — for the rain that starts on top of it. The everyday ones: **qué raro**, said flat — *¿llegó tarde? Qué raro.* — and **mirá vos** — *well, look at that*. The **tono** — *tone* — does the work.

  After:

  > Said slowly, **sí, claro** means *yeah, right*. The **tono** — *tone* — does the work.
  > 
  > **¡Qué novedad!** — *what a surprise* — is for the bondi that's late again; **¡lo que faltaba!** — *that's all we needed* — for the rain on top of it.
  > 
  > The everyday ones: **qué raro**, said flat — *¿llegó tarde? Qué raro.* — and **mirá vos** — *well, look at that*.

- `e2b47401` que-novedad · Ni a palos

  Before:

  > **Ni a palos** — *no way, not a chance*. And for a shrug: **dale que va** — *good enough, let's go*. When something's awful: **está de terror**; when it's perfect: **impecable**.

  After:

  > **Ni a palos** — *no way, not a chance*. For a shrug: **dale que va** — *good enough, let's go*.
  > 
  > When something's awful: **está de terror**; when it's perfect: **impecable**.

- `5ea0dcc7` voy-entendiendo · Voy entendiendo

  Before:

  > **Ir** + *-ando, -iendo* is something happening bit by bit: **voy entendiendo** — *I'm starting to get it*; **se va llenando** — *it's filling up*. **De a poco** — *little by little* — goes with it.

  After:

  > **Ir** + *-ando, -iendo* is something happening bit by bit: **voy entendiendo** — *I'm starting to get it*; **se va llenando** — *it's filling up*.
  > 
  > **De a poco** — *little by little* — goes with it.

- `4c901404` alguna-duda · Cualquier duda…

  Before:

  > **Cualquier duda, avisame** — *any questions, let me know* — closes half the emails in Buenos Aires. What's still to do is **pendiente**: **me quedó pendiente el archivo**. And if it needs a meeting, you **agendás** it.

  After:

  > **Cualquier duda, avisame** — *any questions, let me know*. It closes an email.
  > 
  > What's still to do is **pendiente**: **me quedó pendiente el archivo**.
  > 
  > If it needs a meeting, you **agendás** it.

- `3cb8c2d5` como-te-decia · Como te decía

  Before:

  > A porteño story wanders, so it has signposts: **como te decía** — *like I was saying* — brings it back; **el tema es que** — *the thing is* — gets to the point; **a todo esto** — *by the way* — goes off on a side road; **hablando de** jumps to the next story.

  After:

  > A story has signposts: **como te decía** — *like I was saying* — brings it back; **el tema es que** — *the thing is* — gets to the point.
  > 
  > Two more: **a todo esto** — *by the way* — goes off on a side road; **hablando de** jumps to the next story.

- `c94d2e69` como-te-decia · Y bueno

  Before:

  > **Y bueno** is the porteño shrug — *oh well, what can you do*: **y bueno, así es**. **Ah, me olvidaba** — *oh, I almost forgot* — adds the best part at the end. A story can be **larga** — *long* — or **corta** — *short*.

  After:

  > **Y bueno** is a shrug — *oh well, what can you do*: **y bueno, así es**.
  > 
  > **Ah, me olvidaba** — *oh, I almost forgot* — adds the best part at the end.
  > 
  > A story can be **larga** — *long* — or **corta** — *short*.

- `18cdbaf0` cualquier-cosa-avisame · Cualquier cosa, avisame

  Before:

  > The porteño sign-off, in emails and out of them: **cualquier cosa, avisame** — *if anything comes up, let me know*. A **consulta** is a question you send; a **respuesta**, the reply.

  After:

  > The porteño sign-off, in emails and out: **cualquier cosa, avisame** — *if anything comes up, let me know*.
  > 
  > A **consulta** is a question you send; a **respuesta**, the reply.

- `e06c9bfd` cualquier-cosa-avisame · Hola, Juan:

  Before:

  > Most work emails open with **Hola, Juan:** or **Buen día, Sofi:** and close with **Saludos**, **Abrazo** or **Un abrazo**. **Estimado Juan** / **Estimada Sofi** is the formal exception, and so is **quedo atento** (a woman: **quedo atenta**) — *I look forward to your reply*. **Te adjunto** is *I'm attaching*: *te adjunto mi CV*; **desde ya, gracias** is *thanks in advance*.

  After:

  > Most work emails open with **Hola, Juan:** or **Buen día, Sofi:** and close with **Saludos**, **Abrazo** or **Un abrazo**.
  > 
  > **Estimado Juan** / **Estimada Sofi** is the formal exception. So is **quedo atento** (a woman: **quedo atenta**) — *I look forward to your reply*.
  > 
  > **Te adjunto** is *I'm attaching*; **desde ya, gracias** is *thanks in advance*.

- `aba5baad` estoy-podrido · Harto, podrido

  Before:

  > **Estoy harto** — *I'm fed up*; porteños go further: **estoy podrido** — literally *rotten*. Both take **de** + a thing or an infinitive: **harta del tránsito**, **podrido del ruido**, **harta de estudiar**. Same with **me cansé de esperar** — *I got tired of waiting*. When someone or something else is doing it: **de que** + subjunctive — *estoy podrido de que el ascensor esté roto*.

  After:

  > **Estoy harto** — *I'm fed up*; porteños go further: **estoy podrido** — literally *rotten*.
  > 
  > Both take **de** + a thing or infinitive: **harta del tránsito**, **podrido del ruido**, **harta de estudiar**. Same with **me cansé de esperar** — *I got tired of waiting*.
  > 
  > When someone or something else does it: **de que** + subjunctive — *estoy podrido de que el ascensor esté roto*.

- `9435d1b3` fijate-si-tienen · Fijate si…

  Before:

  > Before you cross the city, you check: **fijate si abren el feriado**, **me fijo si hay turno**, **preguntale si tienen stock**. The **si** is *whether*; the verb after it stays normal.

  After:

  > Before you go, you check: **fijate si abren el feriado**, **me fijo si hay turno**, **preguntale si tienen stock**.
  > 
  > The **si** is *whether*; the verb after it stays normal.

- `b1075fbe` fijate-si-tienen · Check whether

  Before:

  > | the question | asking or telling it |
  > | «¿Tienen stock?» | **Preguntale si** tienen stock. |
  > | «¿Abren el feriado?» | **Me fijo si** abren el feriado. |
  > | «¿Tenés turno?» | **Me preguntaron si** tenía turno. |
  > | «¿Está disponible?» | No sé **si** está disponible. |
  > 
  > **Si** is *whether*, and the verb after it is the normal one. Told later, it moves into the past: *tenés* → *tenía*. If it is still true now, the present is fine too: *me preguntaron si soy argentino*.

  After:

  > | the question | asking or telling it |
  > | «¿Tienen stock?» | **Preguntale si** tienen stock. |
  > | «¿Abren el feriado?» | **Me fijo si** abren el feriado. |
  > | «¿Tenés turno?» | **Me preguntaron si** tenía turno. |
  > | «¿Está disponible?» | No sé **si** está disponible. |
  > 
  > **Si** is *whether*, and the verb after it is normal.
  > 
  > Told later, it moves into the past: *tenés* → *tenía*. If it is still true now, the present is fine too: *me preguntaron si soy argentino*.

- `2bf931e1` me-encargo-que · Llegáramos, trajeran

  Before:
