  > The other persons follow: **nos pidió que llegáramos temprano** — *she asked us to get there early*; **les dijo que trajeran algo** — *he told them to bring something*. **Encargar** is to give someone an errand: **me encargó que le llevara el pan** — *she asked me to bring her the bread*.

  After:

  > Other persons follow: **nos pidió que llegáramos temprano** — *she asked us to get there early*; **les dijo que trajeran algo** — *he told them to bring something*.
  > 
  > **Encargar** is to give someone an errand: **me encargó que le llevara el pan** — *she asked me to bring her the bread*.

- `69332cd2` me-encargo-que · De parte de…

  Before:

  > Passing on a message ends with who it's from: **saludos de parte de mi vieja**. And the classic order from home, told later: «Portate bien» → **me dijo que me portara bien**.

  After:

  > Passing on a message ends with who it's from: **saludos de parte de mi vieja**.
  > 
  > An order from home, told later: «Portate bien» → **me dijo que me portara bien**.

- `22bb24f8` me-la-jugue · Me la jugué, la pifié, la rompe

  Before:

  > More verbs that take a **la** pointing at nothing: **me la juego** — *I'll go for it*; **la pifié** — *I blew it* — and its opposite, **la pegaste** — *you got it right*; **la rompe** — *she's amazing at it*; **se la agarró conmigo** — *he took it out on me* (he got angry at me, though it wasn't my fault). **Me las tomo** — *I'm out of here*. And to make someone stop: **¡cortala!** — *cut it out!*

  After:

  > More verbs take a **la** that points at nothing: **me la juego** — *I'll go for it*; **la rompe** — *she's amazing at it*.
  > 
  > Then **la pifié** — *I blew it* — and its opposite, **la pegaste** — *you got it right*.
  > 
  > **Me las tomo** — *I'm out of here*. And **se la agarró conmigo** — *he took it out on me*. To make someone stop: **¡cortala!** — *cut it out!*

- `31e9d7c0` me-pidio-que · Insistió, sugirió

  Before:

  > **Insistió en que fuera** — *she insisted I go*. **Me sugirió que la llamara** — *he suggested I call her*. As in *me pidió que trajera*, the verb after **que** is in the past subjunctive (*fuera, llamara*).

  After:

  > **Insistió en que fuera** — *she insisted I go*. **Me sugirió que la llamara** — *he suggested I call her*.
  > 
  > As in *me pidió que trajera*, the verb after **que** is past subjunctive (*fuera, llamara*).

- `e4034940` me-pregunto-si · Si = whether

  Before:

  > A yes/no question told later uses **si** (*whether*): *¿Venís?* → **me preguntó si venía**; *¿Abre hoy?* → **no sé si abre hoy**. The verb is normal, no subjunctive. Told in the past, it usually moves back: *venís* → *venía*.

  After:

  > A yes/no question told later uses **si** (*whether*). *¿Venís?* → **me preguntó si venía**; *¿Abre hoy?* → **no sé si abre hoy**.
  > 
  > Normal verb, no subjunctive. Told in the past, it usually moves back: *venís* → *venía*.

- `ec1122a7` me-pregunto-si · A ver si…

  Before:

  > **A ver si** is *let's see if* — and, just as often, a hope: **a ver si nos juntamos** — *we should get together sometime*. To find something out, porteños **averiguan**. After **dudo que**, the verb goes in the subjunctive: **dudo que venga** — *I doubt he'll come*.

  After:

  > **A ver si** is *let's see if*, and often a hope: **a ver si nos juntamos** — *we should get together sometime*.
  > 
  > To find something out, porteños **averiguan**.
  > 
  > After **dudo que**, the verb goes in the subjunctive: **dudo que venga** — *I doubt he'll come*.

- `7b596798` me-pudri · Me pudrí, me harté

  Before:

  > The moment you snap is in the past: **me harté** — *I got fed up*; porteños say **me pudrí**: **me pudrí y me fui**. Both take **de** + infinitive: **me pudrí de esperar**. Then one word: **¡basta!**

  After:

  > The moment you snap is in the past: **me harté** — *I got fed up*; porteños say **me pudrí**: **me pudrí y me fui**.
  > 
  > Both take **de** + infinitive: **me pudrí de esperar**.
  > 
  > One word: **¡basta!**

- `5f8b9622` merezco-un-aumento · Me lo merezco

  Before:

  > | **Me merezco** un aumento. | I deserve a raise. |
  > | **Me lo merezco.** | I deserve it. |
  > | **Te lo merecés.** | You deserve it. |
  > **Merecerse** usually comes with its pronoun in speech: the bare *merezco* is correct but more formal. **Valorar** is *to value*: *en mi laburo no me valoran* — *at my job they don't value me*.

  After:

  > | **Me merezco** un aumento. | I deserve a raise. |
  > | **Me lo merezco.** | I deserve it. |
  > | **Te lo merecés.** | You deserve it. |
  > **Merecerse** usually keeps its pronoun in speech; bare *merezco* is correct but more formal.
  > 
  > **Valorar** is *to value*: *en mi laburo no me valoran* — *at my job they don't value me*.

- `53bd20f9` para-colmo · Nos enteramos, se enteraron

  Before:

  > **Enterarse** for everyone: **nos enteramos tarde** — *we found out late*; **se enteraron todos** — *everybody found out*. The one who tells it best adds **¡ni te cuento!** — *you don't even want to know*.

  After:

  > **Enterarse** for everyone: **nos enteramos tarde** — *we found out late*; **se enteraron todos** — *everybody found out*.
  > 
  > The storyteller adds **¡ni te cuento!** — *you don't even want to know*.

- `c1cd73a8` para-colmo · De golpe, para colmo

  Before:

  > The twist arrives **de golpe** — *all of a sudden* — or **de la nada** — *out of nowhere*. **Para colmo** stacks one more thing on top, like **encima**: **para colmo, llovía**. **Por lo visto** — *apparently* — is what you say when you weren't there. When someone loses a job, people say **lo echaron**; **lo despidieron** is the word of the news and of HR.

  After:

  > The twist arrives **de golpe** — *all of a sudden* — or **de la nada** — *out of nowhere*.
  > 
  > **Para colmo** stacks one more thing on top, like **encima**: **para colmo, llovía**. **Por lo visto** — *apparently* — is for when you weren't there.
  > 
  > When someone loses a job, people say **lo echaron**; **lo despidieron** is the word of the news and of HR.

- `667abe18` postergaron-la-fecha · Entregamos, entregaste

  Before:

  > The **plazo** moves: they **postergaron la fecha** — *pushed the date back* — or, worse, **la adelantaron**. Then it's **horas extra** until **entregamos** — *we hand it in* (also *we handed it in*). And the boss asks: **¿ya lo entregaste?**

  After:

  > The **plazo** moves: they **postergaron la fecha** — *pushed the date back* — or, worse, **la adelantaron**.
  > 
  > Then it's **horas extra** until **entregamos** — *we hand it in* (also *we handed it in*).
  > 
  > The boss asks: **¿ya lo entregaste?**

- `d08e315b` practica-como-no · Comprara, llegáramos, trajeran

  Before:

  > The errand told later: **me pidió que comprara pan**, **nos dijo que llegáramos temprano**, **les pidió que trajeran algo**. The question: **preguntale si tienen stock**.

  After:

  > Errands told later: **me pidió que comprara pan**, **nos dijo que llegáramos temprano**, **les pidió que trajeran algo**.
  > 
  > A question: **preguntale si tienen stock**.

- `e05a6f2c` practica-como-no · Cómo no, faltaba más

  Before:

  > With older neighbors like **don José** and **doña Marta** you still say vos, but warmly: **cómo no** — *of course*; **faltaba más** — *of course, don't mention it*; **dejá, te doy una mano** — *leave it, I'll give you a hand*.

  After:

  > Older neighbors like **don José** and **doña Marta** still get vos, but warmly: **cómo no** — *of course*; **faltaba más** — *of course, don't mention it*.
  > 
  > And **dejá, te doy una mano** — *leave it, I'll give you a hand*.

- `915c227f` practica-me-la-jugue · Te reenvío, para colmo

  Before:

  > **Te reenvío el archivo** — *I'm forwarding you the file*; **cualquier duda, avisame** — *any questions, let me know*. And the story: **de golpe** — *all of a sudden*; **para colmo** — *to top it all off*; **por lo visto** — *apparently*.

  After:

  > **Te reenvío el archivo** — *I'm forwarding you the file*; **cualquier duda, avisame** — *any questions, let me know*.
  > 
  > The story: **de golpe** — *all of a sudden*; **para colmo** — *to top it all off*; **por lo visto** — *apparently*.

- `b09d13bc` resulta-que · Resulta que

  Before:

  > **Resulta que** — *it turns out* — is how a porteño story takes its turn. **Encima** — *on top of that* — makes it worse: **encima, llovía**. **Total** shrugs it off: **total, nadie se dio cuenta**.

  After:

  > **Resulta que** — *it turns out* — is where a story takes its turn.
  > 
  > **Encima** — *on top of that* — makes it worse: **encima, llovía**.
  > 
  > **Total** shrugs it off: **total, nadie se dio cuenta**.

- `24f0169c` se-la-cree · Bancar, bancársela

  Before:

  > **Me la banco** — *I can handle it*; **se la bancó** — *she put up with it*. To say you can't stand something, drop the **la** and name it: *no me banco este frío* — *I can't stand this cold*; *no me banco a mi jefe* — *I can't stand my boss*.

  After:

  > **Me la banco** — *I can handle it*; **se la bancó** — *she put up with it*.
  > 
  > For what you can't stand, drop the **la** and name it: *no me banco este frío* — *I can't stand this cold*; *no me banco a mi jefe* — *I can't stand my boss*.

- `906103c0` se-merece-el-ascenso · En blanco, en negro

  Before:

  > Before you **negociar**, know what you're owed. A job **en blanco** is *on the books*, with aguinaldo (an extra month's pay) and obra social (health insurance); **en negro** is *off the books*. And with inflation, the regular **ajuste** — *the pay adjustment* — is the first thing you **exigir**.

  After:

  > Before you **negociar**, know what you're owed.
  > 
  > A job **en blanco** is *on the books*, with aguinaldo (an extra month's pay) and obra social (health insurance); **en negro** is *off the books*.
  > 
  > With inflation, the regular **ajuste** — *the pay adjustment* — is the first thing you **exigir**.

- `6f45a3bc` siempre-reniego · Me quejo, reniego

  Before:

  > **Me quejo** — *I complain*; **renegar** is the Argentine *to struggle with something, to grumble*. For a habit: **siempre reniego con el wifi**. For something going on now: **estoy renegando con el trámite del DNI**. Someone who's **agotado** is *exhausted*; the **estrés** is why. And the traffic? **Un caos**.

  After:

  > **Me quejo** — *I complain*; **renegar** is the Argentine *to struggle with something, to grumble*.
  > 
  > For a habit: **siempre reniego con el wifi**. For something going on now: **estoy renegando con el trámite del DNI**.
  > 
  > Someone who's **agotado** is *exhausted*; the **estrés** is why. The traffic? **Un caos**.

- `5b512731` sobre-la-hora · Sobre la hora

  Before:

  > **Sobre la hora** — *right at the deadline, at the very last minute* — is how most porteño projects get done. First a **borrador** — *a draft* — then you **corregir** it, and only then the **informe** or the **presentación**. **¿Avanzaron?** — *any progress?*

  After:

  > **Sobre la hora** — *right at the deadline, at the very last minute*.
  > 
  > First a **borrador** — *a draft* — then you **corregir** it, and only then the **informe** or the **presentación**.
  > 
  > **¿Avanzaron?** — *any progress?*

- `534e2bd7` te-la-hago-corta · Te la hago corta

  Before:

  > **Te la hago corta** — *to keep it short* — is usually followed by a long story. **La cuestión es que** — *the point is* — gets to what matters, like **el tema es que**; **en fin** closes it.

  After:

  > **Te la hago corta** — *to keep it short*.
  > 
  > **La cuestión es que** — *the point is* — gets to what matters, like **el tema es que**; **en fin** closes it.

- `6fd250c0` te-la-hago-corta · Y nada

  Before:

  > **Y nada** is the porteño *so, yeah*: it fills the pause at the end of a story. **Al toque** — *right away*: **me contestó al toque**.

  After:

  > **Y nada** is *so, yeah*: it fills the pause at the end of a story.
  > 
  > **Al toque** — *right away*: **me contestó al toque**.

- `64ddc740` te-las-arreglas · A las apuradas, a la larga

  Before:

  > Two fixed phrases to learn whole: **a las apuradas** — *in a rush* — and **a la larga** — *in the long run*. Here *las* and *la* are just *the*, not the idiom's *la*: *salí a las apuradas y me olvidé las llaves*.

  After:

  > Two fixed phrases: **a las apuradas** — *in a rush* — and **a la larga** — *in the long run*.
  > 
  > Here *las* and *la* are just *the*, not the idiom's *la*: *salí a las apuradas y me olvidé las llaves*.

- `0621b05f` te-reenvio-el-archivo · Te reenvío, te paso

  Before:

  > The reply has its own verbs: **te reenvío el mail** — *I'm forwarding you the email*; **te paso el link**; **te pongo en copia** — *I'm cc'ing you*. **Revisé la planilla** — *I went through the spreadsheet*.

  After:

  > Verbs for the reply: **te reenvío el mail** — *I'm forwarding you the email*; **te paso el link**; **te pongo en copia** — *I'm cc'ing you*.
  > 
  > **Revisé la planilla** — *I went through the spreadsheet*.

- `a0157ee6` a-esta-altura · Then and now

  Before:

  > A past that went differently, and what it would mean today: **si hubiera ahorrado** (then), **ahora tendría un depto** (now). The first half is **hubiera** + participle; the second is the plain conditional. **A esta altura** — *by now* — goes in the *now* half: **a esta altura estaría jubilado**.

  After:

  > A past that went differently, and what it would mean now: **si hubiera ahorrado** (then), **ahora tendría un depto** (now).
  > 
  > The first half is **hubiera** + participle; the second, the plain conditional.
  > 
  > **A esta altura** — *by now* — goes in the *now* half: **a esta altura estaría jubilado**.

- `ee61df72` con-normalidad · Levantaron el paro

  Before:

  > The morning after, the question is what still runs: **el subte funciona con normalidad**, or **suspendieron el servicio**. When the strike itself ends, the phrase is **levantaron el paro** or **se levantó el paro**. **Suspender** is for classes, an exam, a march or a service.

  After:

  > The morning after, what still runs: **el subte funciona con normalidad**, or **suspendieron el servicio**.
  > 
  > When the strike ends: **levantaron el paro** or **se levantó el paro**.
  > 
  > **Suspender** is for classes, an exam, a march or a service.

- `1b8d8876` el-tango · Gardel cada día canta mejor

  Before:

  > Carlos **Gardel** died in 1935, and porteños still say he **canta mejor cada día**. A tango's **letra** — *lyrics* — is usually about **nostalgia**: the barrio, the mother, the woman who left.

  After:

  > Carlos **Gardel** died in 1935, yet porteños say he **canta mejor cada día**.
  > 
  > A tango's **letra** — *lyrics* — is usually about **nostalgia**: the barrio, the mother, the woman who left.

- `22681126` fue-clausurado · Fue clausurado, está clausurado

  Before:

  > **Fue clausurado** is the event — *it got closed down*; **está clausurado** is the result — *it is closed now*. The ending agrees with the thing: **el boliche fue clausurado**, **la obra fue clausurada**, **la cancha está clausurada**.

  After:

  > **Fue clausurado** is the event — *it got closed down*; **está clausurado** is the result — *it is closed now*.
  > 
  > The ending matches the thing: **el boliche fue clausurado**, **la obra fue clausurada**, **la cancha está clausurada**.

- `5f88a88d` fue-construido · In conversation

  Before:

  > The news says **fue construido**; in conversation porteños turn it around: *construyeron el puente*, *los detuvieron*. Or they use **se**: **el puente se inauguró ayer**, **se construyó hace un siglo**. For the future the news says **será inaugurada** — *will be opened*; people say *la van a inaugurar*.

  After:

  > The news says **fue construido**; in conversation people flip it: *construyeron el puente*, *los detuvieron*.
  > 
  > Or they use **se**: **el puente se inauguró ayer**, **se construyó hace un siglo**.
  > 
  > For the future the news says **será inaugurada** — *will be opened*; people say *la van a inaugurar*.

- `de7cd7bc` fue-construido · The passive of the news

  Before:

  > | El puente **fue construido** hace un siglo. | was built |
  > | La estación **fue inaugurada** ayer. | was opened |
  > | Los ladrones **fueron detenidos**. | were arrested |
  > 
  > **Fue** + participle, and the participle agrees like an adjective. **Detenidos** is also a noun: *hay dos detenidos* — *two people were arrested*.

  After:

  > | El puente **fue construido** hace un siglo. | was built |
  > | La estación **fue inaugurada** ayer. | was opened |
  > | Los ladrones **fueron detenidos**. | were arrested |
  > 
  > **Fue** + participle; the participle agrees like an adjective.
  > 
  > **Detenidos** is also a noun: *hay dos detenidos* — *two people were arrested*.

- `cc5e231d` gracias-por-invitarme · Saying no without quedar mal

  Before:

  > Nobody wants to **quedar mal** — *look rude*. So you thank them for the **invitación**, blame a **compromiso** — *other plans* — and leave the door open: **la próxima voy**, **otro día**. And if they push: **no insistas, che**. **Rechazar** needs something after it — *rechacé dos invitaciones* — and sounds formal; day to day people say **decir que no**: *me cuesta decir que no*.

  After:

  > Nobody wants to **quedar mal** — *look rude*.
  > 
  > So you thank them for the **invitación**, blame a **compromiso** — *other plans* — and leave the door open: **la próxima voy**, **otro día**. If they push: **no insistas, che**.
  > 
  > **Rechazar** needs something after it — *rechacé dos invitaciones* — and sounds formal; day to day people say **decir que no**: *me cuesta decir que no*.

- `0c7552f3` hay-paro · Hay paro

  Before:

  > A strike is a **paro**: **hay paro de subte**, **paro de colectivos**. When protesters block a street it's a **piquete** or a **corte**, and the street **está cortada**. A **marcha** is a march.

  After:

  > A strike is a **paro**: **hay paro de subte**, **paro de colectivos**.
  > 
  > When protesters block a street it's a **piquete** or a **corte**; the street **está cortada**.
  > 
  > A **marcha** is a march.

- `6ce81526` hay-paro · No funciona

  Before:

  > **Funcionar** is *to work* for things: **el subte no funciona**, **los semáforos no funcionan**. In everyday talk porteños also say **andar**: *el ascensor no anda*. People *trabajan* or *laburan*.

  After:

  > **Funcionar** is *to work* for things: **el subte no funciona**, **los semáforos no funcionan**.
  > 
  > Day to day, porteños also say **andar**: *el ascensor no anda*.
  > 
  > People *trabajan* or *laburan*.

- `27c30a60` hubo-un-incendio · Hubo un incendio

  Before:

  > The news opens with **hubo**: **hubo un incendio**, **hubo un accidente en la avenida**, **hubo dos heridos** — who then **fueron trasladados al hospital**. At the kitchen table the same story loses the passive: *trasladaron a los heridos*.

  After:

  > The news opens with **hubo**: **hubo un incendio**, **hubo un accidente en la avenida**, **hubo dos heridos** — who then **fueron trasladados al hospital**.
  > 
  > At home the same story loses the passive: *trasladaron a los heridos*.

- `2a3a2462` las-elecciones · ¿Dónde votás?

  Before:

  > Election day is a Sunday, and everyone asks **¿dónde votás?** — usually at a school near home: **votamos en la misma escuela**. Your **mesa** is the numbered table where you vote. Before it there is **veda**: campaigning stops 48 hours before the vote, and from the night before nobody can sell alcohol.

  After:

  > Election day is a Sunday, and people ask **¿dónde votás?** — usually at a school near home: **votamos en la misma escuela**.
  > 
  > Your **mesa** is the numbered table where you vote.
  > 
  > Before the vote there is **veda**: campaigning stops 48 hours earlier, and from the night before nobody can sell alcohol.

- `67c1e923` las-elecciones · Votar

  Before:

  > In Argentina voting is **obligatorio** from 18 to 70. **¿A quién votaste?** is a real question, though many people don't answer it. **Votar a** someone: *voté a otro candidato*. **Votar por** works too: *¿por quién votás?* **Votar en blanco** is to cast a blank vote.

  After:

  > In Argentina voting is **obligatorio** from 18 to 70.
  > 
  > **¿A quién votaste?** is a real question, though many don't answer it. **Votar a** someone: *voté a otro candidato*. **Votar por** works too: *¿por quién votás?*
  > 
  > **Votar en blanco** is to cast a blank vote.

- `7ae297c7` lo-lindo-de-la-ciudad · Lo + adjective

  Before:

  > Put **lo** before an adjective to say *the … thing*: **lo lindo** — *the nice thing*, **lo feo** — *the bad part* (same as **lo malo**), **lo único** — *the only thing*. Add **de** to say of what: **lo peor de Buenos Aires es el tránsito**. **Lo** never changes: **lo feo son los precios**.

  After:

  > Put **lo** before an adjective for *the … thing*: **lo lindo** — *the nice thing*, **lo feo** — *the bad part* (same as **lo malo**), **lo único** — *the only thing*.
  > 
  > Add **de** to say of what: **lo peor de Buenos Aires es el tránsito**.
  > 
  > **Lo** never changes: **lo feo son los precios**.

- `e19109d4` lo-mio-lo-tuyo · Lo mío, lo tuyo

  Before:

  > **Lo** turns anything into *the thing*: **lo tuyo es la cocina** — *your thing is cooking*; **lo mío** — *my thing*; **lo primero** — *the first thing*; **lo justo** — *just enough*. Giving up an argument: **lo que vos digas** — *whatever you say*. **Lo que sea** — *whatever, anything*: **comemos lo que sea**.

  After:

  > **Lo** makes anything *the thing*: **lo tuyo es la cocina** — *your thing is cooking*; **lo mío** — *my thing*; **lo primero** — *the first thing*; **lo justo** — *just enough*.
  > 
  > Giving up an argument: **lo que vos digas** — *whatever you say*.
  > 
  > **Lo que sea** — *whatever, anything*: **comemos lo que sea**.

- `49e04ee1` lo-que-pasa-es-que · Lo que pasa es que…

  Before:

  > **Lo que pasa es que…** — *the thing is* — explains the real reason. **Lo raro es que…** — *the weird thing is*; **lo importante es que…** — *what matters is*. A fact takes the normal verb: **lo importante es que llegaste**. Something not real yet takes the subjunctive: **lo importante es que la pases bien**.

  After:

  > **Lo que pasa es que…** — *the thing is* — explains the real reason.
  > 
  > **Lo raro es que…** — *the weird thing is*; **lo importante es que…** — *what matters is*.
  > 
  > A fact takes the normal verb: **lo importante es que llegaste**. What isn't real yet takes the subjunctive: **lo importante es que la pases bien**.

- `c6fee335` lo-que-pasa-es-que · Lo de…

  Before:

  > **Lo de** is *the thing with*: **¿qué pasó con lo de Juan?** After **a** or **en** it is *someone's place*: **voy a lo de Sofi**, **estamos en lo de mis viejos**. And **lo de siempre** is *the usual*: at the café, at the bar, in life.

  After:

  > **Lo de** is *the thing with*: **¿qué pasó con lo de Juan?**
  > 
  > After **a** or **en** it is *someone's place*: **voy a lo de Sofi**, **estamos en lo de mis viejos**.
  > 
  > And **lo de siempre** is *the usual*.

- `83a1fba1` lo-que-paso-fue-que · Lo que pasó fue que…

  Before:

  > Tell it in the past: **lo que pasó fue que se cortó la luz**. Say what you meant: **lo que quiero decir es que…**. You already know **lo que más** — *what… most*: **lo que más me molestó fue eso**. And **en el fondo** — *deep down* — for the real reason under it.

  After:

  > Tell it in the past: **lo que pasó fue que se cortó la luz**. Say what you meant: **lo que quiero decir es que…**.
  > 
  > You already know **lo que más** — *what… most*: **lo que más me molestó fue eso**.
  > 
  > And **en el fondo** — *deep down* — for the real reason.

- `a2499f0a` lo-que-paso-fue-que · Aclarar

  Before:

  > To clear it up — **aclarar**: **quiero aclarar algo**. Then: **no quise decir eso** — *I didn't mean that*; **me expresé mal** — *I put it badly*; **me entendiste mal** — *you misunderstood me*; **no lo dije por vos** — *I didn't say it about you*.

  After:

  > To clear up is **aclarar**: **quiero aclarar algo**.
  > 
  > Then: **no quise decir eso** — *I didn't mean that*; **me expresé mal** — *I put it badly*; **me entendiste mal** — *you misunderstood me*; **no lo dije por vos** — *I didn't say it about you*.

- `54279426` me-hubiera-gustado · Metí la pata

  Before:

  > **Metí la pata** — *I messed up, I said or did the wrong thing*. More porteño: **me mandé una macana**. A **macana** is a silly mistake; an **error** is any mistake. And when something goes wrong for someone: **¡qué macana!** — *what a shame!*

  After:

  > **Metí la pata** — *I messed up, I said or did the wrong thing*. More porteño: **me mandé una macana**.
  > 
  > A **macana** is a silly mistake; an **error** is any mistake.
  > 
  > When something goes wrong for someone: **¡qué macana!** — *what a shame!*

- `eac6d092` mi-candidata-gano · Las elecciones

  Before:

  > **Las elecciones** — *the elections* — decide the **gobierno** and the **presidente**. Before them, the **debate** on TV; after, the **resultados**. If nobody wins outright there's a **balotaje** — *a runoff*. The course stays out of who won: the words, not the opinions.

  After:

  > **Las elecciones** — *the elections* — decide the **gobierno** and the **presidente**.
  > 
  > Before them, the **debate** on TV; after, the **resultados**.
  > 
  > If nobody wins outright there's a **balotaje** — *a runoff*.

- `e4442fba` no-es-que · No es que sea

  Before:

  > After **no es que** any verb goes into the subjunctive, and you already know the forms: **sea**, **esté**, **haya**, **tenga**. *No es que sea caro, es que no tengo plata* — *it's not that it's expensive, I just have no money*. *No es que esté cansado, es que laburo temprano.*

  After:

  > After **no es que** any verb goes into the subjunctive. You already know the forms: **sea**, **esté**, **haya**, **tenga**.
  > 
  > *No es que sea caro, es que no tengo plata* — *it's not that it's expensive, I just have no money*. *No es que esté cansado, es que laburo temprano.*

- `2f545600` no-es-que-no-me-guste · No es que…, es que…

  Before:

  > After **no es que**, every verb goes subjunctive: **no es que no me interese** — *it's not that I'm not interested*; **no es que no me importe** — *it's not that I don't care*; **no es que me caiga mal** — *it's not that I don't like him*; **no es que no queramos / podamos** — *it's not that we don't want to / can't*. The real reason follows with **es que** + a normal verb: *es que no llego* — *I just can't make it*.

  After:

  > After **no es que**, verbs go subjunctive: **no es que no me interese** — *it's not that I'm not interested*; **no es que no me importe** — *it's not that I don't care*.
  > 
  > Or **no es que me caiga mal** — *it's not that I don't like him*; **no es que no queramos / podamos** — *it's not that we don't want to / can't*.
  > 
  > The real reason takes **es que** + a normal verb: *es que no llego* — *I just can't make it*.

- `041f794e` paro-docente · Paro docente, paro general

  Before:

  > A strike is named after who stops: **paro docente** — *the teachers*; **paro de colectivos** — the **colectiveros**; **paro general** — everyone. The **gremio** — *the union* — **anunció un paro** for Thursday, and the news calls it a **medida de fuerza**.

  After:

  > A strike is named after who stops: **paro docente** — *the teachers*; **paro de colectivos** — the **colectiveros**; **paro general** — everyone.
  > 
  > The **gremio** — *the union* — **anunció un paro**; the news calls it a **medida de fuerza**.

- `cab9d500` practica-me-afanaron · Me afanaron, se tapó

  Before:

  > **Me afanaron la billetera**, **me estafaron**; **se tapó la pileta**, **se trabó el ascensor** — with **me afanaron**, *they* did it and we don't say who; with **se tapó**, it happened by itself and nobody did it.

  After:

  > With **me afanaron**, *they* did it and we don't say who: **Me afanaron la billetera**, **me estafaron**.
  > 
  > With **se tapó**, it happened by itself: **se tapó la pileta**, **se trabó el ascensor**.

- `55b97bb7` practica-me-hubiera-gustado · Otra vida, otra oportunidad

  Before:

  > Another life: **si hubieras estudiado, ahora serías médico**. A missed chance: **me hubiera gustado ir**, **tendríamos que haber ido**, **te lo perdiste**. And **hubieras** + participle alone is a reproach: **¡hubieras avisado!** — *you should have told me!*

  After:

  > Another life: **si hubieras estudiado, ahora serías médico**.
  > 
  > A missed chance: **me hubiera gustado ir**, **tendríamos que haber ido**, **te lo perdiste**.
  > 
  > Alone, **hubieras** + participle is a reproach: **¡hubieras avisado!** — *you should have told me!*

- `3ff85211` rumor-o-verso · ¿Rumor o verso?

  Before:

  > A **rumor** is only that until someone **confirma** it, or denies it: **el gobierno desmintió el rumor**. Before **compartirlo**, ask **¿cuál es la fuente?** A **cadena** — *a chain message* — with no source is probably **verso** — *made up*.

  After:

  > A **rumor** is just that until someone **confirma** or denies it: **el gobierno desmintió el rumor**.
  > 
  > Before **compartirlo**, ask **¿cuál es la fuente?**
  > 
  > A **cadena** — *a chain message* — with no source is likely **verso** — *made up*.

- `5c404af7` segun-el-diario · Según

  Before:

  > **Según** — *according to* — takes papers and people: **según el diario**, **según mi mamá**, **según Nico**. **Supuestamente** and **al parecer** keep a distance from what you pass on. **Al parecer** sounds like the news; in chat people say *parece que*.

  After:

  > **Según** — *according to* — takes papers and people: **según el diario**, **según mi mamá**, **según Nico**.
  > 
  > **Supuestamente** and **al parecer** keep a distance from what you pass on. **Al parecer** sounds like the news; in chat it's *parece que*.

- `6d88948a` segun-el-diario · Aparentemente, se ve que

  Before:

  > **Aparentemente** is the neutral *apparently*: **aparentemente, mañana hay paro**; **por lo visto** means the same. **Se ve que** is what you conclude from what you see: **se ve que llovió** — *it must have rained*; **se ve que está cerrado**.

  After:

  > **Aparentemente** is the neutral *apparently*: **aparentemente, mañana hay paro**; **por lo visto** is the same.
  > 
  > **Se ve que** is what you conclude from what you see: **se ve que llovió** — *it must have rained*; **se ve que está cerrado**.

- `e7e97709` si-hubiera-ahorrado · Hubiera, hubieras, hubiéramos, hubieran

  Before:

  > **Hubiera** changes with the person: **hubiera** (I, he, she), **hubieras** (vos), **hubiéramos** (we), **hubieran** (they, you all). *Si hubieras estudiado medicina, ahora serías médico. Si hubiéramos ahorrado, ahora estaríamos en Bariloche. Si hubieran construido el puente, no habría tanto tránsito.*

  After:

  > **Hubiera** changes by person: **hubiera** (I, he, she), **hubieras** (vos), **hubiéramos** (we), **hubieran** (they, you all).
  > 
  > *Si hubieras estudiado medicina, ahora serías médico. Si hubiéramos ahorrado, ahora estaríamos en Bariloche. Si hubieran construido el puente, no habría tanto tránsito.*

- `fdea7949` si-hubiera-ahorrado · Would have, I wish I had

  Before:

  > **Hubiera** + participle also works alone as *would have*: **nunca lo hubiera vendido** — *I'd never have sold it*; **¿lo hubieras vendido?** — *would you have sold it?* And **ojalá hubiera** + participle is *I wish I had*: **ojalá hubiera ahorrado más**.

  After:

  > **Hubiera** + participle works alone as *would have*: **nunca lo hubiera vendido** — *I'd never have sold it*; **¿lo hubieras vendido?** — *would you have sold it?*
  > 
  > And **ojalá hubiera** + participle is *I wish I had*: **ojalá hubiera ahorrado más**.

- `1d43e9b9` te-lo-perdiste · Te lo perdiste

  Before:

  > A chance you let go: **dejé pasar la oportunidad** — *I let the chance go*. To someone who didn't come: **te lo perdiste** — *you missed out*. **Lamentablemente** — *unfortunately* — you can't **volver atrás** — *go back* — so you drop it with **ya fue**.

  After:

  > A chance you let go: **dejé pasar la oportunidad** — *I let the chance go*.
  > 
  > To someone who didn't come: **te lo perdiste** — *you missed out*.
  > 
  > **Lamentablemente** — *unfortunately* — you can't **volver atrás** — *go back* — so drop it with **ya fue**.

- `8a29fec5` tendria-que-haber · Me arrepiento

  Before:

  > **Me arrepiento** — *I regret it*; **me arrepentí** — *I regretted it, I changed my mind*. With what you did or didn't do, **de (no) haber** + participle: **me arrepiento de no haber ido** — *I regret not going*; *no me arrepiento de haber venido*. An **oportunidad** is a chance; **aprovechar** is to make the most of it: *no aproveché la oportunidad*.

  After:

  > **Me arrepiento** — *I regret it*; **me arrepentí** — *I regretted it, I changed my mind*.
  > 
  > For what you did or didn't, **de (no) haber** + participle: **me arrepiento de no haber ido** — *I regret not going*; *no me arrepiento de haber venido*.
  > 
  > An **oportunidad** is a chance; **aprovechar**, to make the most of it: *no aproveché la oportunidad*.

- `a1c0dd99` tiene-sus-cosas · Lo que más, lo más

  Before:

  > The most natural way to say what you love about a place: **lo que más me gusta de Buenos Aires es la noche**. The opposite: **lo que menos me gusta es el ruido**. And **lo más** + adjective is *the most … thing*: **lo más lindo del barrio es la plaza**.

  After:

  > To say what you love about a place: **lo que más me gusta de Buenos Aires es la noche**. The opposite: **lo que menos me gusta es el ruido**.
  > 
  > And **lo más** + adjective is *the most … thing*: **lo más lindo del barrio es la plaza**.

- `a75b10b9` tiene-sus-cosas · Tiene sus cosas

  Before:

  > To say the good and the bad together: **Buenos Aires tiene sus cosas** — *it has its issues* — like the **inseguridad** (*crime*) and the **costo de vida** (*cost of living*). But **a la vez** — *at the same time* — **no hay nada como Buenos Aires**: *there's nothing like it*.

  After:

  > The good and the bad together: **Buenos Aires tiene sus cosas** — *it has its issues*.
  > 
  > Like the **inseguridad** (*crime*) and the **costo de vida** (*cost of living*).
  > 
  > But **a la vez** — *at the same time* — **no hay nada como Buenos Aires**: *there's nothing like it*.

- `5a5e8f75` tocas-la-guitarra · Tocar, cantar, bailar

  Before:

  > An instrument is **tocar**: **¿tocás la guitarra?**, **mi abuelo tocaba el bandoneón en una orquesta**. **¿Cantás?** **¿Bailás tango?** — and at the milonga, **¿bailamos?**

  After:

  > An instrument is **tocar**: **¿tocás la guitarra?**, **mi abuelo tocaba el bandoneón en una orquesta**.
  > 
  > **¿Cantás?** **¿Bailás tango?** At the milonga, **¿bailamos?**

- `968d241f` tocas-la-guitarra · No todo es tango

  Before:

  > Porteños grew up on **rock nacional** — *Argentine rock* — sung in Spanish in the stadiums; the provinces on **folclore**; every party ends in **cumbia**. A **cantante** is a singer; a tango singer is often called a *cantor*.

  After:

  > Porteños grew up on **rock nacional** — *Argentine rock* — sung in Spanish; the provinces on **folclore**; every party ends in **cumbia**.
  > 
  > A **cantante** is a singer; a tango singer is often called a *cantor*.

- `a52eadb9` cada-loco-con-su-tema · Como dice el dicho…

  Before:

  > **Como dice el dicho** — *as the saying goes* — and then the one that fits: **cada loco con su tema** — *to each their own*; **no hay dos sin tres** — *there's always a third time*; **el que no llora no mama** — *if you don't ask, you don't get*.

  After:

  > **Como dice el dicho** — *as the saying goes*. Then one that fits: **cada loco con su tema** — *to each their own*; **no hay dos sin tres** — *there's always a third time*.
  > 
  > And **el que no llora no mama** — *if you don't ask, you don't get*.

- `c856ee20` cada-loco-con-su-tema · The ones grandma says

  Before:

  > **Más vale pájaro en mano que cien volando** — *one sure thing is better than a hundred possible ones*; **a caballo regalado no se le miran los dientes** — *you don't complain about a gift*; **ojos que no ven, corazón que no siente** — *what you don't know can't hurt you*; **del dicho al hecho hay mucho trecho** — *easier said than done*. **El que se quema con leche, ve una vaca y llora** — *after a bad experience, you're scared of anything similar*. For someone who complicates everything: **buscarle la quinta pata al gato** — *to look for the cat's fifth leg*. And when someone walks in: **hablando del rey de Roma…** — *we were just talking about you*.

  After:

  > **Más vale pájaro en mano que cien volando** — *one sure thing is better than a hundred possible ones*; **a caballo regalado no se le miran los dientes** — *you don't complain about a gift*.
  > 
  > **El que se quema con leche, ve una vaca y llora** — *after a bad experience, you're scared of anything similar*. And **ojos que no ven, corazón que no siente** — *what you don't know can't hurt you*; **del dicho al hecho hay mucho trecho** — *easier said than done*.
  > 
  > For one who complicates everything: **buscarle la quinta pata al gato** — *to look for the cat's fifth leg*. When someone walks in: **hablando del rey de Roma…** — *we were just talking about you*.

- `e6b7166a` como-dice-el-dicho · El que avisa no traiciona

  Before:

  > **El que avisa no traiciona** — *don't say I didn't warn you*. You say it while you give the warning. Literally: *the one who warns you doesn't betray you*. Two more you'll hear in Buenos Aires: **sobre gustos no hay nada escrito** — *everyone has their own taste*; **el que quiere celeste, que le cueste** — *if you want something good, you have to work for it*.

  After:

  > **El que avisa no traiciona** — *don't say I didn't warn you*. You say it while you give the warning. Literally: *the one who warns you doesn't betray you*.
  > 
  > Two more: **sobre gustos no hay nada escrito** — *everyone has their own taste*; **el que quiere celeste, que le cueste** — *if you want something good, you have to work for it*.

- `b9bc5c55` cuanto-antes-lleguemos · Mejor todavía

  Before:

  > **Mejor todavía** — *even better*: **¿es gratis? Mejor todavía**; also **mejor así** — *better this way*. *Tanto mejor* says the same but sounds bookish. To **evitar** the **hora pico**, you go early; the place **se llena** fast; and leaving early, the day **rinde** — *goes further*. **Ganar tiempo** is *to save time*: **con el subte ganás tiempo**.

  After:

  > **Mejor todavía** — *even better*: **¿es gratis? Mejor todavía**; also **mejor así** — *better this way*. *Tanto mejor* is the same but bookish.
  > 
  > To **evitar** the **hora pico**, you go early; the place **se llena** fast; and leaving early, the day **rinde** — *goes further*.
  > 
  > **Ganar tiempo** is *to save time*: **con el subte ganás tiempo**.

- `36794004` gracias-por-todo · Al principio me costó

  Before:

  > Looking back, a finished thing takes the preterite: **al principio me costó** — *it was hard at first* — **pero me adapté** — *I adapted*. How things were takes the imperfect: *al principio era raro*. Now **me siento como en casa**. Say it **de corazón**.

  After:

  > A finished thing takes the preterite: **al principio me costó** — *it was hard at first* — **pero me adapté** — *I adapted*.
  > 
  > How things were takes the imperfect: *al principio era raro*.
  > 
  > Now **me siento como en casa**. Say it **de corazón**.

- `6b55c1f4` me-cayo-la-ficha · Me da cosa

  Before:

  > **Me da cosa** has two uses. Doing something feels awkward: **me da cosa pedirle plata** — *I feel awkward asking him for money*. Or a thing gives you a bad feeling: **ese perro me da cosa** — *that dog makes me uneasy*. **Me pone mal** — *it makes me sad*. **Me quedé con las ganas** — *I missed out, and I still want to*.

  After:

  > **Me da cosa** has two uses. Doing something feels awkward: **me da cosa pedirle plata** — *I feel awkward asking him for money*.
  > 
  > Or a thing gives a bad feeling: **ese perro me da cosa** — *that dog makes me uneasy*.
  > 
  > **Me pone mal** — *it makes me sad*. **Me quedé con las ganas** — *I missed out, and I still want to*.

- `73a05d53` me-emocione · Orgulloso

  Before:

  > **Estoy orgulloso de vos** — *I'm proud of you*. A woman says **orgullosa** and **agradecida**. **Agradecido** — *grateful*: **estoy muy agradecida**. Grateful *to* a person: **agradecido con vos**. Grateful *for* a thing: **agradecida por el regalo**.

  After:

  > **Estoy orgulloso de vos** — *I'm proud of you*. A woman says **orgullosa** and **agradecida**.
  > 
  > **Agradecido** — *grateful*: **estoy muy agradecida**.
  > 
  > Grateful *to* a person: **agradecido con vos**. And *for* a thing: **agradecida por el regalo**.

- `cca2ccce` me-emocione · Me emocioné

  Before:

  > **Emocionarse** is mostly *to be moved, to get emotional* (it can also mean *to get excited*): **me emocioné en el casamiento**. **Se me puso la piel de gallina** — *I got goosebumps*. Also **me dio piel de gallina** — *it gave me goosebumps*.

  After:

  > **Emocionarse** is mostly *to be moved, to get emotional* (also *to get excited*): **me emocioné en el casamiento**.
  > 
  > **Se me puso la piel de gallina** — *I got goosebumps*. Also **me dio piel de gallina** — *it gave me goosebumps*.

- `0904d601` me-hace-ruido · Me quedé helado

  Before:

  > Something does it *to* you: **me dejó pensando** — *it got me thinking*; **me partió el alma** — *it broke my heart*. And you end up frozen: **me quedé helado** — *I froze, I was stunned*. A woman says **me quedé helada**. The good ones: **me hace bien**, **me da ternura**, and **¡qué alivio!**

  After:

  > Something does it *to* you: **me dejó pensando** — *it got me thinking*; **me partió el alma** — *it broke my heart*.
  > 
  > You end up frozen: **me quedé helado** — *I froze, I was stunned*. A woman says **me quedé helada**.
  > 
  > The good ones: **me hace bien**, **me da ternura**, and **¡qué alivio!**

- `8845c3ad` me-hizo-reir · Me río, te reíste

  Before:

  > **Reírse** is *to laugh*: **me río mucho con vos** — *I laugh a lot with you*. You already know **me reí** and **nos reímos**; with *vos* in the past it is **te reíste**: **¿de qué te reíste?** **Llorar** is *to cry*: **lloré con esa película**.

  After:

  > **Reírse** is *to laugh*: **me río mucho con vos** — *I laugh a lot with you*.
  > 
  > You know **me reí** and **nos reímos**; with *vos* in the past it is **te reíste**: **¿de qué te reíste?**
  > 
  > **Llorar** is *to cry*: **lloré con esa película**.

- `8b879baa` me-mori-de-risa · ¿De qué te reís?

  Before:

  > **Reírse** with *vos*: **¿de qué te reís?**; with *him*: **se ríe de todo**; in the past, **se rió** — *he laughed*. **¿Lloraste?** For the big laugh: **me morí de risa** — *I died laughing*. A **comedia** gets a **sonrisa**; a sad scene, **lágrimas** and **tristeza**. And people with **sentido del humor** laugh at themselves.

  After:

  > **Reírse** with *vos*: **¿de qué te reís?**; with *him*: **se ríe de todo**; in the past, **se rió** — *he laughed*.
  > 
  > For the big laugh: **me morí de risa** — *I died laughing*.
  > 
  > A **comedia** gets a **sonrisa**; a sad scene, **lágrimas** and **tristeza**. **¿Lloraste?** People with **sentido del humor** laugh at themselves.

- `16b0f92c` mis-abuelos-italianos · Otra época

  Before:

  > **Era otra época** — *it was a different time*. The **inmigrantes** who arrived around 1900 packed into the city's **conventillos** and shaped its tango and its accent.

  After:

  > **Era otra época** — *it was a different time*.
  > 
  > The **inmigrantes** who arrived around 1900 packed into the city's **conventillos** and shaped its tango and accent.

- `a5efa082` mis-abuelos-italianos · Llegaron en barco

  Before:

  > Many porteños have an **abuelo** or **bisabuelo** who **llegó en barco** from Italy or Spain. An Italian, or anyone with an Italian surname, is a **tano** or a **tana**: *mi abuelo era tano*. That's why a porteño talks with his hands and eats ñoquis on the 29th.

  After:

  > Many porteños have an **abuelo** or **bisabuelo** who **llegó en barco** from Italy or Spain.
  > 
  > An Italian, or anyone with an Italian surname, is a **tano** or a **tana**: *mi abuelo era tano*.
  > 
  > So porteños talk with their hands and eat ñoquis on the 29th.

- `69083595` mis-raices · Gallego y la ciudadanía

  Before:

  > In Argentina every Spaniard is a **gallego**, from Galicia or not. It is informal and friendly, but some Spaniards do not like it; the neutral word is **español**. And the great-grandparents still pay off: half of Buenos Aires is getting Italian or Spanish citizenship — **sacar la ciudadanía**. Your **raíces** — *roots* — come with a passport.

  After:

  > In Argentina every Spaniard is a **gallego**, from Galicia or not. It is informal and friendly, but some Spaniards do not like it; the neutral word is **español**.
  > 
  > Half of Buenos Aires is getting Italian or Spanish citizenship — **sacar la ciudadanía**.
  > 
  > Your **raíces** — *roots* — come with a passport.

- `15d2f5ec` no-te-lo-tomes-a-mal · Entre nos

  Before:

  > **Entre nos** — *between you and me* — is where porteño gossip starts. **Sin querer** — *by accident*: **te pisé sin querer**.

  After:

  > **Entre nos** — *between you and me* — is where gossip starts.
  > 
  > **Sin querer** — *by accident*: **te pisé sin querer**.

- `b1b445dc` practica-cada-loco-con-su-tema · Lo que pasó fue que…

  Before:

  > **Lo que pasó fue que**, **lo que quiero decir es que**, **lo tuyo**, **lo que sea**, **en el fondo**. And three sayings: **cada loco con su tema** — *to each their own*; **no hay dos sin tres** — *there's always a third time*; **hablando del rey de Roma** — *we were just talking about you*.

  After:

  > **Lo que pasó fue que**, **lo que quiero decir es que**, **lo tuyo**, **lo que sea**, **en el fondo**.
  > 
  > Three sayings: **cada loco con su tema** — *to each their own*; **no hay dos sin tres** — *there's always a third time*; **hablando del rey de Roma** — *we were just talking about you*.

- `2195936f` practica-me-mori-de-risa · Me morí de risa

  Before:

  > **Me morí de risa**, **¿de qué te reís?**, **me hizo sentir…** — and **cuanto antes lleguemos, mejor**. **A la larga** — *in the long run*: **a la larga, el esfuerzo rinde**.

  After:

  > **Me morí de risa**, **¿de qué te reís?**, **me hizo sentir…**, **cuanto antes lleguemos, mejor**.
  > 
  > **A la larga** — *in the long run*: **a la larga, el esfuerzo rinde**.

- `d3c1b17e` practica-se-emociono · Se recibió, se emocionó

  Before:

  > Big moments: **se recibió** — *graduated*; **se comprometieron** — *got engaged*; **se jubiló** — *retired*. And the feelings: **se emocionó** — *got emotional*; **me llegó** — *it touched me*.

  After:

  > Big moments: **se recibió** — *graduated*; **se comprometieron** — *got engaged*; **se jubiló** — *retired*.
  > 
  > The feelings: **se emocionó** — *got emotional*; **me llegó** — *it touched me*.

- `9f9ceb73` practica-se-instalaron · Acampamos, te convido

  Before:

  > **Acampamos al lado de un lago**, **hicimos dedo**; **¿te convido un mate?** — but **no hiervas el agua**. A **carpa** is a camping tent; on the beach in Mar del Plata it is also a rented shade tent.

  After:

  > **Acampamos al lado de un lago**, **hicimos dedo**; **¿te convido un mate?** — but **no hiervas el agua**.
  > 
  > A **carpa** is a camping tent; on the beach in Mar del Plata, also a rented shade tent.

- `cf2878f8` que-andes-bien · Brindemos

  Before:

  > A porteño **despedida** — *farewell party* — ends with a toast, a **brindis**: **¡brindemos!**, **brindo por ustedes**, **¡salud!** Then **gracias por todo**, **fue un placer**, **los voy a extrañar** — and never *adiós*: you say **chau** or **hasta la próxima**, because here nobody really leaves. To leave, people say **bueno, me voy yendo** or **los dejo**; **me despido** sounds like a letter, though *me despedí de todos* — *I said goodbye to everyone* — is fine.

  After:

  > A **despedida** — *farewell party* — ends with a toast, a **brindis**: **¡brindemos!**, **brindo por ustedes**, **¡salud!**
  > 
  > Then **gracias por todo**, **fue un placer**, **los voy a extrañar**. Never *adiós*: you say **chau** or **hasta la próxima**.
  > 
  > To leave, people say **bueno, me voy yendo** or **los dejo**; **me despido** sounds like a letter, though *me despedí de todos* — *I said goodbye to everyone* — is fine.

- `17a11c47` se-emociono · Se emocionó, nos emocionamos

  Before:

  > **Emocionarse** in every person: **me emocioné**, **¿te emocionaste?**, **mi viejo se emocionó**, **nos emocionamos todos**. It was **emotivo** — *moving* — and **se me hizo un nudo en la garganta** — *I got a lump in my throat*.

  After:

  > **Emocionarse** by person: **me emocioné**, **¿te emocionaste?**, **mi viejo se emocionó**, **nos emocionamos todos**.
  > 
  > It was **emotivo** — *moving* — and **se me hizo un nudo en la garganta** — *I got a lump in my throat*.

- `4039fa21` se-instalaron-en-la-boca · Se instalaron

  Before:

  > The family story runs in the third person plural: **se escaparon de la guerra**, **se instalaron en un conventillo** — *an old shared house where each poor family rented one room* — and **trabajaron en el puerto**. One person: **se instaló**. They left **pobreza** behind, **mantuvieron** the language and the traditions, and **guardaron** the photos of the ship. *Conservaron* says the same, but it is a written word.

  After:

  > The family story runs in the third person plural: **se escaparon de la guerra**, **se instalaron en un conventillo**, **trabajaron en el puerto**. One person: **se instaló**.
  > 
  > A conventillo is *an old shared house where each poor family rented one room*.
  > 
  > They left **pobreza** behind, **mantuvieron** the language and traditions, and **guardaron** the photos of the ship. *Conservaron* is the same, but written.

- `102ea8d0` sin-ofender · Sin ofender

  Before:

  > Porteños are direct, but they soften it first: **sin ofender** — *no offense*; **te lo digo con cariño**; **no es nada personal**. A **crítica** goes down better that way, above all on a **tema delicado**.

  After:

  > Porteños are direct, but soften it first: **sin ofender** — *no offense*; **te lo digo con cariño**; **no es nada personal**.
  > 
  > A **crítica** goes down better that way, above all on a **tema delicado**.

- `bb475835` ya-sos-de-aca · Lo lograste

  Before:

  > **Lograr** — *to manage something hard, to achieve*: **¡lo lograste!** — *you did it!* And your own line: **¡lo logré!** — *I did it!* Then **felicitaciones** — *congratulations* — or, among friends, just **¡grande!**

  After:

  > **Lograr** — *to manage something hard, to achieve*: **¡lo lograste!** — *you did it!* Your own line: **¡lo logré!** — *I did it!*
  > 
  > Then **felicitaciones** — *congratulations* — or, among friends, just **¡grande!**
